# ST0056: Add a Rust-based CLI with a local SQLite DB with bidirectional sync to/from .md files that exposes an MCP server with full API access to Intent -- Acceptance

> **THIS FILE IS A GENERATED VIEW, AND A ROW AUTHORED HERE IS DISCARDED BY THE NEXT SYNC.** The acceptance contract is canon in the thread model; this file renders it. Acceptance Criteria (AC) are the ratified completeness boundary; Acceptance Tests (AT) are the small red-to-green tests that prove them.
>
> Done = every AC is covered by a GREEN AT, or (for a non-test AC) its named evidence is satisfied, AND the AC set is the ratified full boundary. Done is read from this map, never from a hand-ticked box.
>
> Test-backed satisfaction is COMPUTED from covering green ATs and never stored -- storing it would be double truth. An AC has four states, not two: beyond satisfied and unsatisfied, a requirement can be **descoped** to a named thread or **withdrawn** with its reason on the record. Both are non-blocking and both are reported separately, so a thread that descoped half its contract looks like one.

10 gated of 17 rostered.** **AND THE AUDIT WINDOW IS NOT TIME-BOXED, WHICH IS THE PREMISE THAT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ACCEPTED.** dc framed WP-01 as a closing window. **It is not, and the FIRST supporting fact vc gave for that was FALSE** (dc, correcting it before it could travel): vc wrote that every parity tool takes its tree ROOT as `$1`. **No tool does.** `canon_commit_check.sh` and `view_skew_check.sh` take `ROOT` as an ENV override, `conservation_check.sh` has no `ROOT=` line at all, and **`stale_at_check.sh` derives ROOT from `BASH_SOURCE[0]` and walks UP for `intent/.config/config.json` with no override whatsoever.** **THE CONCLUSION IS STRONGER WITHOUT IT: `git worktree add` DOES NOT NEED ANY PARAMETERISATION.** A worktree is a real checkout, so a tool deriving ROOT from its own location resolves to the WORKTREE root automatically -- including the hard-wired one, whose upward walk finds the worktree's `config.json`. **The differential is reconstitutable REGARDLESS of whether any tool is parameterised**, and 2290 commits carry the nested layout. **AND THE CHEAP BY-REVISION PATH IS NOT GENERALLY AVAILABLE** (dc, measured): `canon_commit_check.sh` at `ce532a97` returns **rc=2 -- `0 recorded attachment(s), across 0 measured + 57 unmeasurable = 57 thread(s)`**, because many historical revisions record no attachments and correctly refuse. **The worktree is not the tidier route, it is the only reliable one.** **HOW THE FALSE FACT WAS MANUFACTURED, because it is worse than the under-reaching probes and belongs on the record:** vc's probe was `grep -oE 'ROOT=[^ ]*|\$\{?1\}?' | head -2 | tr '\n' ' '`, and **the `tr` JOINED TWO INDEPENDENT MATCHES INTO WHAT READ AS ONE EXPRESSION** -- `ROOT="${ROOT:-$(cd` and a `$1` from a `die()` message elsewhere in the file. **The probe did not merely under-reach; it FABRICATED a syntactic form that appears nowhere in the source**, and vc then stated it with confidence. Reproduced deliberately after the catch. **Sweeping before WP-01 lands would additionally measure cc's IN-FLIGHT patch rather than the landed one -- findings against a tree that never existed.** **THIRD ARM, ON ic's CASE AND IN ic's WORDS: WHERE M CANNOT BE DERIVED, THE TWO NUMBERS MUST BE VISIBLY DIFFERENT KINDS OF NUMBER.** `rig_selftest.sh` prints _drives 18 of the rig's 24 refusal sites_: the **18** is computed from the case table and cannot drift; the **24** is a HAND COUNT of a DIFFERENT artefact's refusal sites, and **no mechanical count reproduces it** -- `exit 2` gives 6, die-calls 42, ic's third probe 30, vc's fourth 47. **Four probes, four answers, none of them 24.** **A criterion demanding derivation would make ic's honest `RECORDED, not measured` label NON-COMPLIANT and push an author toward a bare figure, which is strictly worse** (ic, offering it as an arm rather than as a defect to fix). Derivation is unsatisfiable when M is a property of an artefact nothing enumerates. **SO: derive where derivation is possible; where it is not, LABEL THE RECORDED NUMBER AT THE NUMBER, never in a footnote, so the ratio can never be read without its status -- AND NAME WHAT WOULD DERIVE IT.** The missing definition is stated, because a `RECORDED` with no path to `measured` is a permanent exemption with no work-list, which is the shrunken-roster failure wearing a different hat. **A recorded M is a CLAIM CARRYING NO EVIDENCE, and the reader is entitled to know both that it is one and what would settle it.** **AND THIS INSTANCE IS THE PROOF THAT `10 IS A FLOOR` WAS THE RIGHT READING RATHER THAN A CAUTION.** `rig_selftest.sh`'s 24 carries **no `intent/st` path shape at all**, so dc's two-grep proxy is structurally blind to it -- an ELEVENTH, found by ic from inside the very set that had just adjudicated itself, in a form the nominating probe could not express. **ic ROUTES THEIR OWN TWO**, at their request and on their own bar -- _a closing count must close over what was EXAMINED, never over what EXISTS_ -- which dc's defect instantiates: **if their instruments violate a line they wrote, they should be the one to find it.**

**PROVENANCE OF THIS ROW'S OWN FIGURES (vc 2026-08-19, on findings by ic and dc, neither looking for them).** Two of the figures above are marked `(RECORDED)` under the MEASURED-versus-RECORDED form specified in `data-model.md`: they were observed once and cannot be re-derived. **`EXAMINED 86 of 278` NAMES NO REVISION**, so it is checkable by nobody -- ic reproduced the DEFECT but not the FIGURES (86 of 276, against 84 examined) and could not determine whether this row is wrong or whether it names a revision they had not swept. **`10 of 41`'s NOMINATING PROBE WAS NEVER COMMITTED** -- no tool, no board history carrying its text -- and dc's reconstruction returns 14; `of_n_population.sh` (`f789ae48`) enumerates the population instead. **NEITHER IS REPLACED WITH A CORRECTED NUMBER, DELIBERATELY: a replacement mints a third figure on a fresh decay schedule and retires nothing**, and both peers reached that conclusion independently and declined to supply one. **THIS IS AC-00.11'S OWN THIRD ARM POINTED AT AC-00.11, TWICE, IN THE ROW THAT RULES A FIGURE MUST DERIVE FROM WHAT WAS EXAMINED.** Separately: `10 gated of 17 rostered` and `10 of 41` are two different populations that coincide only in the numeral, so a repeated numeral here reads as a cross-reference while doing no such work. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-00.12 **A PARTITION PRINTED BY AN INSTRUMENT MUST ASSERT ITS OWN CLOSURE IN THE OUTPUT, AND THE CLOSURE MUST BE OVER THE POPULATION THE CRITERION IS ABOUT.** (vc 2026-08-19, on three instances by three nodes in one morning.) Distinct from AC-00.11: there, M was derived from the wrong SET; here every member was examined and the PARTITION of M is wrong. **Wrong M versus wrong partition of M.** Instances: cc's `2+53+1+2 = 58` against 57 rows, because the table HEADER carries the literal `Completed` and was counted as a member of the population it labels; ic's `canon_commit_check.sh` printing `EXAMINED 2 of 1 ... the other -1`; cc's `8 carried + 3 DERIVED = 11` against 10 `CREATE TABLE`, because `doc_sections` is `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE` and invisible to a `^CREATE TABLE` count. **THE LOUDNESS WAS LUCK, NOT DESIGN** (ic): the `-1` was caught only because subtraction produced an IMPOSSIBLE value -- **a `+1` remainder would have been as silent as cc's 58.** So the requirement is that the instrument STATES the sum and asserts it closes, rather than leaving a reader to add up. **AND CLOSING OVER THE WRONG WHOLE IS ITS OWN DEFECT** (cc): a table count would have failed AT-01.7 for a reason unrelated to its criterion. **cc's corollary, which is why this is a criterion and not a sweep: at one Completed fewer they would have matched the recorded figure and been WRONG WITH CORROBORATION -- a remembered figure is not independent confirmation when the same method produced it.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-00.13 **A CHECKER VERIFIES MEMBERSHIP IN A VOCABULARY AND NEVER THAT THE VOCABULARY CAN EXPRESS THE STATES IN USE.** (vc 2026-08-19; surfaced by the pre-commit gate's own output at `938ed7a3`.) Membership is checkable and adequacy is not, **so the check that exists is the one that cannot see the defect.** Three instances, three nodes, one day: dc's critic census, where `undeclared / declared / armed` cannot express *armed, tool present, and correctly not run in THIS context* -- a second AXIS, not a fifth value; **`ratified_in`, which says either _here is the record_ or nothing, so a provisional ruling IS recorded and IS NOT ratified and both available values are wrong** -- and separately does two jobs, an ENUM for some units and a prose provenance stamp for the rest; and figure provenance, where the model could not express *recorded once, not reproducible*, so every figure read as a measurement. **THE COMMON SHAPE IS SHARPER THAN ANY OF THE THREE: the inadequate vocabulary forces a true state into a value that is wrong in a SAFE-LOOKING direction.** `declared` hides a real capability; a bare `ratified_in` certifies a provisional ruling as settled; an unmarked figure reads as reproducible. **THE WRONG VALUE IS ALWAYS THE ONE THAT READS AS MORE FINISHED, NEVER LESS**, which is why none was caught by review: nothing looked broken and the checker returned green because membership held. **Corollary: the remedy for a DANGLING value is the wrong remedy for an INEXPRESSIBLE one** -- re-anchoring a provisional `ratified_in` to a sha would have certified it as ratified, the fix for one defect committing the other. -- satisfied: no (computed)

this is _resolves to the wrong KIND of thing_ -- and passing the existence check is exactly what makes a reader stop asking what it resolved to.** Existence is to type as correctness is to validity (AC-00.14's shape, in a different domain). **THE STALE-AT GUARD IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT** (cc's own mirror blind side, which they then walked into): it catches a `to-write` row citing a file that EXISTS and cannot catch one citing FICTION. **MEASURED POPULATION, BOTH THREADS: 15 AT rows cite a parity tool; 6 are cited AND on disk; NINE CITE FILES THAT DO NOT EXIST.** That is legitimate -- `to-write` means unwritten -- **but nothing checks that what is eventually built matches the kind the row declared**, and cc came within one clean-slate of compiling two Rust integration tests against two rows declaring shell parity tools under a directory that does not yet exist. **THE JUSTIFICATION IS NOT INSTANCE COUNT: nothing checks kind at all, and a gap can be demonstrated without a victim** -- cc's own *a capability with no consumer is not a gate*, pointed the other way. **AND THE MOTIVATING CASE IS NOT WHAT THIS CRITERION CATCHES, WHICH IS STATED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT FOR A READER TO DISCOVER** (ic 2026-08-19, checking cc's actual drafts against the row within an hour of it minting). cc's files are `clone_carries_canon.rs` and `edits_land_in_distinct_files.rs`; the rows declare `intent/st/ST0057/parity/tools/canon_clone_completeness.sh` and `canon_concurrent_diff.sh`. **Different name, different extension, different directory** -- so had they landed, the declared paths would still be EMPTY, `to-write` would remain literally accurate, **and the wrong-kind artefact would sit at a path no row mentions.** **THAT IS AN ORPHANED ARTEFACT, WHICH IS THE OTHER DIRECTION OF THE GATE-VISIBILITY DEFECT -- the 29 instruments on disk that no row cites -- AND IT IS NOT THIS ROW'S SUBJECT.** This criterion covers a declaration and its artefact disagreeing AT THE DECLARED PATH. **The kind hole and the unrostered-instrument bucket are the same defect from opposite ends; naming both here is what stops the next reader building an instrument that cannot catch the case this row cites.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)

This is about instruments that are each SOUND.** Three instances, all on the same day, none of which any review of the instrument itself could have caught. **dc's:** `fiat_close_is_visible_on_every_surface.rs` excused its fourth surface in PROSE -- `| doctor | n/a | reports inconsistencies, not states |` -- which is true of the class and false of the arm that matters, because reporting that a gate PASSES is a claim about the states underneath it. **vc's:** `AT-00.8` scans the Rust binaries for project-management state in output; the TCA skills emit `--tca-dir PATH  TCA steel thread directory (eg intent/st/ST0055)` from SHELL, so a shipped help surface sat between two sound instruments. **cc's:** the unwired-marker guard reads code and not comments, DELIBERATELY, so that reporting the defect is never an instance of it. **THE THIRD ONE IS WHY THIS CANNOT BE CLOSED BY FORBIDDING GAPS: cc's gap is correct, argued, and load-bearing.** A rule that outlawed declared reach would outlaw the reason that guard is usable. **SO THE OBLIGATION IS OWNERSHIP OF THE UNION, NOT ABOLITION OF THE SEAMS: for any property the estate claims to hold, the instruments asserting it declare their reach, and something names what the union does NOT cover.** **AND THE CLASS IS INVISIBLE TO REVIEW BY CONSTRUCTION** -- each instrument passes its own inspection, the defect lives in a space no artefact describes, and there is nothing to look at. **Corollary, from the same three: a gap and a false negative are indistinguishable from inside either instrument.** `AT-00.8` returning clean was TRUE of what it scanned and read as a statement about the estate, which is `AC-00.11`'s wrong-M one level up -- the population there is INSTRUMENTS rather than rows. -- satisfied: no (computed)

- AC-00.9 **No Intent-project management state reaches a user-facing surface.** (hv ruling, 2026-08-15, direct: _"NEVER EVER put Intent project management state like ST or WP numbers or ACs etc into output from Intent. Intent as a tool cannot expose its internal project management state in its output. Some other project doesn't care about an AC or a WP or even a test that is in the Intent project itself."_) **Scope is OUTPUT, not source**: no ST id, WP number, AC/AT id, decision number or Intent test name may appear in anything the shipped binaries emit -- errors, remedies, refusals, help and usage text, `doctor` findings, GraphQL error messages, daemon banners, or any rendered view of a consumer's project. Comments and Intent's own test fixtures are out of scope; they are never seen by a consumer. **The finding this AC is written from is that the leak is STRUCTURAL rather than incidental** (vc, measured 2026-08-15): `transitions.rs` carries an `owed_by: "WP-06"` field, `dispatch.rs` defaults an entry owner to `"WP-06"`, and `render.rs` renders both into remedies a consumer reads -- so a field in the model is DESIGNED to carry Intent's roadmap into another project's terminal. Six emitted sites measured: `render.rs:300` (remedy names WP-06), `render.rs:324` (error names ST0056 and the owning node), `render.rs:745` (remedy's worked example is `ST0056/03`), `intentd/main.rs:10` (startup banner), `graphql.rs:128` (client-visible resolver error), `ingest.rs:279` (scaffolding refusal). **The replacement statement is the consumer-meaningful one and is strictly more useful: say what is unavailable and what to do instead, never who owes it or under which work package** -- "not available in this build" is actionable, "owed by WP-06" is an invitation to read a roadmap the reader has no access to. Worked examples in remedies use a neutral id, never Intent's own. **CORRECTION 2026-08-15, same day, before anyone built to it: the six-site measurement above is the SMALL half.** `dispatch.rs:41` compiles `surface/dispatch-table.json` into the binary with `include_str!`, and that file carries **121 Intent PM identifiers** (25 `WP-06`, 11 `WP-05`, ten distinct ST ids, and AC ids including this thread's own) as parity prose describing v2 behaviour. So the carrier is **embedded DATA, not source string literals**, and it is 20x the size of the literal surface. Found by dc measuring something else entirely -- AC-11.3's `INTENT_HOME` question -- which is the second time today a real finding arrived from an unrelated lane. **What is NOT asserted, because it is not measured: whether those 121 are ever EMITTED.** The `owner` field demonstrably is (`render.rs:324`); the parity prose may never reach a surface, in which case it is out of hv's stated scope, which is output. **It is one renderer change away from being in scope, and it is what any auditor will see first.** **MEASURED 2026-08-15, closing that gap the same hour rather than leaving it for whoever implements: of the 121, exactly EIGHT are emitted, and all eight are `owner_wp`. **[MEASUREMENT DECAYED IN OUR FAVOUR, RE-MEASURED 2026-08-15 (vc, on cc's report, verified rather than taken): it is now ZERO emitted, not eight.** `owner_wp` has NO read site anywhere in the workspace -- `dispatch.rs:104` declares it and `dispatch.rs:220` defaults it, and the `owner()` function and the `render.rs` site this clause traced are gone; `intentd/main.rs` and `graphql.rs` are both fixed, each leaving a comment recording what it used to read. **The count above is kept because a criterion that quietly restates its own numbers cannot be audited, and because the direction of decay is the point: a stale measurement reads as current evidence whether it decayed in our favour or against us.** Fifth instance of this class in this contract, all mine.**]

**EXTENDED 2026-08-20 (hv, direct, on seeing thread ids in code again; contracted by vc). THE SCOPE LINE ABOVE READS `OUTPUT, NOT SOURCE`, AND THAT MISSES THE THIRD CASE: INTENT WRITES FILES INTO OTHER PEOPLE'S REPOSITORIES.** A template copied by `st new`, a hook installed into `.git/hooks/`, a skill or subagent installed into `.claude/`, and the rule library served to a consumer's agent are none of them binary output, and none of them are comments a consumer never sees -- they are the opposite, artefacts whose whole purpose is to be read inside another project. **This criterion's own title says user-facing SURFACE and its body said OUTPUT; the body was the narrower of the two and the payload fell through the difference.** Measured 2026-08-20 (vc, at `92ed515c`): **80 Intent PM references across 25 files of installed payload** -- 30 in `lib/templates/`, 24 in shipped skills and subagents, 23 in plugin shell source, 3 in the rules library.

**THE DISCRIMINATOR IS CITATION VERSUS FORMAT EXAMPLE, and it is mechanical rather than a judgement per site.** `intent st show ST0042` in a skill teaches syntax and costs a consumer nothing -- the id is a placeholder that happens to be four digits. **A CITATION points into a tracker the reader cannot open.** `plugins/claude/rules/_schema/critic-contract.md:52` reads _"Rejected alternative documented in `intent/st/ST0034/design.md` D5"_, naming a path that exists in no consumer's project **and in this one no longer either**, since `intent/st/` has held ST0046, ST0056 and ST0057 alone since `e7f00e65`. **A citation that has gone dangling inside its own repository is the clearest possible statement that the reference was never for the reader.** Citations go; examples stay, re-based on `ST0000` where convenient. **The instance every user meets is `lib/templates/prj/st/ST####/acceptance.md:30`, carrying _"Exemption (ST0048)"_ -- so every steel thread created in every project is stamped with an Intent thread number.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)

### WP-02 -- Workspace and reified model: intentsvcs types, schema faces, store (status: Done)

- AC-02.1 (non-test) The cargo workspace builds with fmt + clippy gates on macOS and Linux CI -- evidence: rust CI run 31812129560 green on 736033d -- satisfied: yes
- AC-02.2 The three schema faces (JSON Schema, DDL, SDL) are generated, committed, and CI fails on drift between types and committed faces -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.3 Deleting the DB and re-creating it from previously extracted canon yields identical queryable content -- **the disk-to-db ingest direction**. (Rationale corrected twice on 2026-08-15. It was written as "the D01 disposability invariant"; D01 is reversed and **the DB is the durable SSOT**. This AC does NOT make the DB disposable and must not be read as licensing that: re-creation from an extract is a CAPABILITY -- it is how a colleague reconstitutes state, and how an `.md` or `.json` becomes well-formed DB items, **which it does only by passing through the hard gate of the intentsvcs API**. The AC and its test are unchanged and WP-02 does not reopen) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.4 Serialise/deserialise laws hold under proptest, and an unknown field in canonical JSON is refused by name, never dropped -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.5 `rusqlite` appears in exactly one Cargo.toml and CI asserts the dependency graph -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.6 **Platform and data-model openness: every DB schema entity has a 1-1 file form, and the round-trip is lossless.** hv's standing requirement, stated 2026-08-15: _"I want there to be ALWAYS a 1-1 mapping between the db schema entities and an equivalent .json or .md version of those entities SO THAT I can get my data out of the db and use it somewhere else LOSSLESSLY. That is the reason for the disk-to-db and db-to-disk syncing."_ **This is the requirement that the deleted "no DB migrations, ever" was a corrupted memory of** -- the real constraint was never about migrations, it was about never being locked in. Held mechanically: **the table list is ENUMERATED FROM THE GENERATED DDL FACE, never from a hand-maintained roster**, so a new table enters the check the day it lands and cannot be forgotten. For each table, either a file form exists (`.json` or `.md`) covering every column without loss, or the table carries an **explicitly DECLARED exemption naming why it is derivable** -- absence of a file form is never the answer, the D05 refusal posture applied to coverage. Lossless is proved by round-trip in **both** directions per D01's bidirectional sync: db-to-disk then disk-to-db reproduces the DB content, and re-emitting reproduces the files byte-for-byte. **The file form must be usable WITHOUT Intent** -- standard, self-describing formats -- since "use it somewhere else" is the entire point. Measured 2026-08-15, the estate has two gaps: `event_log` has a schema face and no artefact (now ruled `events.jsonl`, append-only), and `file_index` has neither a face nor a declared exemption. **hv has ruled `file_index` the `.treeindex` replacement** (2026-08-15): a file index plus a text-searchable index of what is in the text files, with tree-sitter as the eventual structural layer -- so it is a PRODUCT FEATURE, not scaffolding, and D31's "the source tree index in the DB obviates treeindex" is naming this table. It remains exempt from the file-form rule on derivability -- it is rebuilt by re-scanning the working tree, and the files it indexes are the user's data and are already extractable -- but **the exemption must be DECLARED carrying that reason**, never inferred from the absent schema face. **NINE tables in the DDL: threads, wps, criteria, tests, related, issues, event_log, file_index, `doc_sections`.** **CORRECTED 2026-08-15: this sentence said EIGHT and omitted `doc_sections`, and it was caught by the instrument this AC demands.** cc built the enumeration from the generated DDL face as required, and it immediately disagreed with the AC's own prose. **The roster in this sentence was itself a hand-maintained roster, sitting inside the criterion that forbids hand-maintained rosters** -- fifth instance of that class in one day and the only one in the contract rather than in the code. It is kept as a description of what the enumeration should FIND, never as the list the checker reads; if the two disagree again, **the face wins and this sentence is the defect** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.7 **A store written by an older schema is DETECTED, and is either migrated or refused -- never silently opened.** Found live by dc 2026-08-15 via `int dogfood`, on a v3 project created ~40 minutes before the AC-enum collapse landed: `intent st list --status all` fails with `sqlite: no such column: state`, because **`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` makes the DDL a NO-OP against an existing database**, so `Store::open()` returns SUCCESS on a store it cannot read. **The open path succeeding on an unreadable database is the defect**; the query failure is only where it surfaces, and the distance between broken and found-out is however long until somebody runs a verb naming the new column. **Detection is impossible today**: no `user_version`, no `schema_version`, nothing a migration could dispatch on even once one exists. This is the FIRST live instance of the class D01's reversal created -- `store.rs:4` already states "MIGRATIONS ARE NORMAL, so there is no rebuild-instead-of-migrating story", so **the policy was written and the mechanism was not built**, and D36 removed the escape hatch the same morning by ruling `rm intent.db` out of existence. Requires: a stamped schema version in the store; an open path that compares it and refuses or migrates; and, per `IN-AG-NO-SILENT-001`, **a remedy on the refusal** -- "your database predates a schema change" is a better sentence than `no such column: state at offset 23` even while no migrator exists. **The recovery path is the EXTRACT, not the snapshot** (see D35): restoring a D35 snapshot taken before the change reproduces the old schema, so re-ingest from the `.json`/`.md` extract is what actually survives schema evolution. **VERIFIED 2026-08-15 (vc) at `523b34e8`, built before it was contracted: `cargo test -p intentsvcs --test store_schema_version` -> 6 passed, and the discriminating case is the one this row demanded -- the fixture lays down the OLD `criteria` shape, `Store::open` returns `Err` with no query run, `SchemaUnstamped` and `SchemaMismatch` are separate variants, and `remedy()` is non-empty and distinct per variant.** Two things the build got right that this row did not ask for: the mismatch remedy names WHICH END can move (there is no migrating a store backwards into a binary that never heard of its shape), and the `Sqlite` remedy carries D36's warning explicitly -- "do NOT delete the store -- it is the source of truth, not a cache". **THE LIMIT, raised by cc and priced here rather than discovered later: this AC buys DETECTION, never recoverability for what already exists.** A migration ladder can only start at 1, because `user_version = 0` is not schema zero -- it is the ABSENCE of one, permanently spoken for by SQLite's default -- so a pre-stamp store records nothing a migrator could dispatch on and is refused forever. dc's dogfood DB is in that set. That is the correct outcome under D34 (the extract is the interchange, the DB is per-machine) and the `SchemaUnstamped` remedy says so honestly rather than inventing a recovery command, but **anything never synced out of a pre-stamp store exists only there**. Read this row as "no store is ever silently misread", never as "no store is ever lost" -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-02.8 **Every table carries a DB-written record timestamp, stamped by the database at INSERT/UPDATE (D42).** Measured 2026-08-15 (vc), on hv's instruction that a missing timestamp field is a miss needing rectification. **The result is ZERO of eight tables. Not one has a record timestamp the database wrote.** (**Nine as built** -- `snapshots` landed at 18:14Z on 2026-08-15, after this sweep and after the fix, and carries `taken_at` + `updated_at`. **The enumeration below is a record of the measurement and goes stale by GROWTH; the guard does not, because it derives the table list from `sqlite_master` rather than carrying a list.** That split is the point: a criterion that enumerates its corpus needs the corpus derived, not listed.) `threads`, `related`, `wps`, `criteria`, `tests`, `issues`, `file_index`, `event_log` -- and `doc_sections` is an FTS5 virtual table. Requires: a `created_at` / `updated_at` (or equivalent) on every table, **written by the database as part of the write** -- a DEFAULT or trigger, never a value the caller passes in -- and `event_log.ts` converted from a parameter to a DB-side stamp. **THE REASON THIS WAS MISSED IS THE FINDING, and it is the day's recurring class one more time: three columns LOOK like the answer and none of them is.** `threads.created` and `issues.created` are **authored DATES** (`YYYY-MM-DD`) -- project facts about when a thread or issue came into being, legitimately data, and carried from v2. `file_index.mtime` is the **FILE's** mtime, a fact about the filesystem. `event_log.ts` is a `String` **ARGUMENT to `Envelope::new`**, so the one field that reads as a record timestamp is application-supplied by construction; its own doc comment says "this is the one place a record is stamped", which is true and describes the wrong actor. **An authored date is a fact about the WORLD; a record timestamp is a fact about the DATABASE. Both are needed and they are not interchangeable** -- and a schema carrying a plausible `created` column is exactly how eight tables shipped with no record time and nobody noticed. **This is not a nicety: under D34 two machines MERGE their event logs, and a merge needs a time nobody could have typed.** Note for whoever builds it: the DDL is the thing AC-02.7's hash guard watches, so this change MUST bump `SCHEMA_VERSION` and re-pin in the same commit -- which is the first real exercise of that guard and exactly what it was built for. **FULL SWEEP OF EVERY REMAINING TIME SOURCE IN v3 (vc, 2026-08-15), on hv's instruction that any other source is wrong and is to be removed. FIVE SITES AND ONE GUARD GAP -- and the first two are the ones everybody, me included, had been calling the FIX:** **(b) IS SATISFIABLE TODAY BY A VALUE THAT WAS NEVER DERIVED -- ic found it, vc verified it independently on a canary migrated through the committed door at `8770cea3`.** **`event_log` holds ZERO rows, `intent/events.jsonl` is ZERO bytes, and 56 of 56 threads carry a populated `created`** -- ST0001 reads `2025-03-06` against v2 frontmatter `created: 20250306`, so the date is carried straight from the file with a format normalisation and never goes through the database at all. **The ratified form of this row is the thing that was skipped**: `threads.created` = the `ts` of that thread's `st.new` event, DB-set by the `event_log.ts` DEFAULT, and a v2-migrated thread has an authored `created:` and no `st.new`, **so the migration RESTORES an `st.new` carrying the authored date.** It was adopted over the plain-column form explicitly because it is hv's rule literally rather than a proxy for it -- and the obvious implementation, copying the frontmatter date into the column, is precisely what it was chosen over, and is what shipped. **THE VERIFICATION HAZARD IS THE POINT AND IT IS WHY THIS ANNOTATION EXISTS RATHER THAN A BUG REPORT: a check that reads `threads.created` and compares it to the authored date PASSES.** The value is correct. It will keep passing for as long as the migration copies rather than derives, on every thread in every fleet estate. **The criterion is about PROVENANCE and every cheap predicate for it is about the VALUE** -- asking whether what arrived is right cannot see that it arrived by the wrong route, which is prose conservation's one-sided arm rediscovered one entity down. **A predicate that binds (ic's, adopted): for every migrated thread an `st.new` exists whose `ts` equals `threads.created`.** It fails at 0 of 56 today and CANNOT be satisfied by copying a value. Keyed on the event's `op` rather than on a column name, so it survives a rename. **And it does not disturb dc's `.dump` normalisation, in the direction that matters**: `restore_event` carries the AUTHORED date under D42's create/restore split, so restored rows are deterministic and compare equal across runs -- building this ADDS rows that agree rather than rows needing normalisation. The two stamp kinds are exactly what D42 exists to keep apart, and the migration currently emits neither. -- satisfied: yes (computed)

### WP-03 -- Ingest, views and sync engine (status: Done)

- AC-03.1 Strict ingest refuses schema-invalid canon with file + finding named, exit non-zero, nothing partially loaded. **POSTURE RULED 2026-08-15 (cc asked before absorbing it): refusing the CORPUS rather than the entity is UPHELD.** A criterion carrying a nonsense `kind`/`state` pair now stops the whole estate loading, which is a real escalation from "doctor reports it". **It is right, and the reason is D34 rather than strictness for its own sake: ingest is a RESTORE**, and a restore that partially succeeds leaves the DB neither the old truth nor the new -- strictly worse than refusing. AC-04.1 already requires the DB write all-or-nothing; this is the same property one layer up. The blast radius is also narrower than it reads: under D01-reversed the daily driver answers from the DB and touches files only when the store is COLD, so this is "a cold store cannot be built from an invalid corpus", not "one bad AC breaks `st list` forever". **Two conditions, and the second is a CONTRACT requirement rather than an observation of current behaviour, because it is exactly what regresses silently: (1) the refusal names the file, the JSON pointer AND a remedy the reader can act on** -- naming the location without an action is where this posture becomes hostile; **(2) `intent doctor` MUST keep working when ingest refuses.** It is the diagnostic of last resort, and a refusal that names a file while the only investigative tool has also stopped leaves a user holding a location and nothing to look at it with. cc reports doctor currently returns findings and exits cleanly; this makes that a requirement so it cannot quietly stop being true -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.10 **The DB backup is taken through SQLite's online backup path, rolls on a configurable schedule into `.backup/`, and never fails silently** (D35). Four arms, and the first is the one a plausible implementation gets wrong. **(a) NEVER a file copy.** The store opens WAL (`store.rs:183`), so committed transactions live in `intent.db-wal` until checkpointed; `VACUUM INTO` or `sqlite3_backup_*` only, and `fs::copy`/`cp`/a directory tar are all defects. **(b) The snapshot is restorable and PROVEN so** -- a restored snapshot opens, passes schema validation, and returns the same entity counts as the source. **(c) Rolling retention per day/week/month with configurable counts, read from `intent/.config/config.json` via `intent config`, and DB snapshots occupy their own namespace under `.backup/` so they never collide with `intent upgrade`'s `backup-<TIMESTAMP>/` rollback artefacts** (`intent_upgrade:117-121`) -- two mechanisms in one directory with different retention rules, where deleting the wrong one is the loss this AC exists to prevent. **(d) A failed backup SURFACES, AND SO DOES ONE THAT NEVER RAN** (`IN-AG-NO-SILENT-001`): this is the backup of the durable SSOT, and the natural implementation -- best-effort, on a timer, in a daemon nobody watches -- is exactly the one that fails silently. **The second half was missing from this AC's first form and was found by ic asking for a read path: a schedule that never fires produces NO FAILURE TO REPORT, so "surface the failure" does not cover it and a user cannot distinguish a working schedule from one that silently never started.** Required therefore: **`doctor` reports backup STALENESS** -- the newest snapshot's age against the configured schedule -- which detects never-ran without needing anything to have failed. That is the two-sided test, the same construction as the clock guard's check C, which catches an inbox going backwards by comparing two stamps to each other and needs no clock at all. `intent backup --list` answers **what snapshots exist** and is deliberately NOT the health report: one place reports health, and it is `doctor`. **Two settings are ruled NOT configurable** (ic, endorsed): the snapshot directory is fixed, because a configurable path is precisely how a pruner gets aimed at `intent upgrade`'s rollback namespace and would make this AC's own collision reachable through _supported configuration_; and there is no key that silences backup failure, because a switch to turn the warning off manufactures the silent failure and gives it a supported name -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.11 (non-test) **A COLD START RESTORES THE EVENT LOG, and a log that is missing or unreadable is REPORTED rather than read as "this project has no history".** (vc 2026-08-16, on cc's defect at `a7aa0b9e`, which is fixed -- this criterion exists because nothing in the contract asked for the property.) **`ingest::resync` rebuilt seven tables from the extract and skipped the eighth.** Every other table is derived, so omitting one is recoverable; **`event_log` is derived from nothing (D34), so omitting it loses the data outright** -- the fact AC-03.9's ruling already names and no criterion required. **The reach was the whole cold-store path**: `load_fresh` warms an empty store through `resync`, and **an empty store is the normal state of every fresh clone**, because `intent/.cache/` is gitignored. So a clone answered every question correctly and had no history at all until somebody happened to run the explicit `intent sync --to-store`. **The second half is not a nicety and it is why this is one criterion rather than two: a missing log is INDISTINGUISHABLE from a project that has never recorded anything**, so the silent-success path is the default one and `IN-AG-NO-SILENT-001` is not satisfied by the restore alone. **Severity is set by hv's migration goal rather than by the bug**: every fleet member arrives at v3 as a fresh clone, so the cold path is the FIRST path a migrated project takes, not an edge case. **Contract-coverage finding worth recording beside the criterion, because it is the second one today: this defect and dc's 0038 both lived in territory no AC covered** -- the cold-store restore here, `.git/hooks` there -- and both were found by a node measuring something else. **The tests that mention `event_log` (AT-02.8, AT-04.5) were green before the defect and green after it**, so they were never wrong and never sensitive; the guard that caught it was a NEW test written for unrelated work. -- WITHDRAWN: hv ruling D53, 2026-08-20: `intent/events.jsonl` is deleted and the event log lives in the store alone, so this criterion's subject is gone in both halves. There is no cold-start RESTORE because there is nothing on disk to restore from, and no MISSING-log report because a store with no history is now the ordinary state of a fresh clone rather than a defect. **THE COST THIS CRITERION EXISTED TO PREVENT IS NOW THE ACCEPTED COST**, stated in D53 and asserted in `openness.rs`: a clone arrives with no history, and that is the price hv took for deleting the tracked extract. WITHDRAWN RATHER THAN SATISFIED -- the restore path was real and worked, and it was removed rather than proved. Its replacement surface is AC-09.3, `intent events`, which reads the one home the log has. (by hv (matts), 2026-08-20)
- AC-03.12 **A FIELD THE TOOL REFUSES TO PROCEED WITHOUT MUST REACH A HUMAN READ FACE -- AND RENDERING IT IS NOT ENOUGH.** (ic 2026-08-18 by round-trip; widened by cc, who verified structurally and independently.) `Thread.status_reason` is MANDATORY on `cancel`, `hold`, `reopen` and `reinstate`, and the refusal argues for it verbatim -- _"recorded on the entity as the reason for its CURRENT state, and in the event log as part of the decision, **which is what lets anyone reconstruct why later**"_. **A planted canary reason survives into `thread.json` and appears in NEITHER `st show`, NOR `st list --status all`, NOR the rendered `info.md`; there are ZERO occurrences in `render.rs` and `views.rs`.** **THE PROMISE NAMES TWO CARRIERS AND HAS A READER ON NEITHER: `intent --help` declares 34 verbs and NOT ONE READS THE EVENT LOG** -- no `events`, no `log`, no `history` -- **and `search` does not reach it either, since `ingest.rs` never mentions the field, so _full-text search across all authored prose_ excludes this authored prose.** **SO THIS ROW IS NOT SATISFIED BY RENDERING THE FIELD.** `event_log_envelopes.rs:168` records that the envelope holds the HISTORY while the field carries only the LATEST reason, so showing the field answers _why is it on hold NOW_ and never _reconstruct why later_ -- **a fix to the visible half would make the promise LOOK kept while leaving it unkept.** It closes when the promised reconstruction is reachable, or when the refusal stops promising it. **This is a harder loss than a dropped rendering distinction: it is AUTHORED PROSE a human typed because the tool demanded it, recoverable only from the committed machine extract.** **BOUNDED on ic's own statement of reach: established for CLI READ FACES and MARKDOWN VIEWS. `Thread` derives `Serialize` and `SimpleObject`, so a GraphQL face was not reached -- the claim is _no human face_, not _no face_.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.13 **A WRITE PATH WHOSE INPUT WAS REFUSED MUST NOT THEN BE USED AS A SOURCE OF TRUTH.** (vc 2026-08-18, from a live instance with vc as the cause.) `sync --to-store` refused a canon extract on a `UNIQUE constraint failed: tests.thread_id, tests.id` and left the store at its previous contents. **`sync --to-disk` then wrote that stale store over canon at rc=0, with nothing in its output indicating the store had rejected the most recent write -- destroying the same authored criterion twice.** The two verbs are documented as opposite directions of one operation and nothing carries the failure of one into the other. **The property required is that the store record whether its last ingest SUCCEEDED, and that any egest from a store whose last ingest was REFUSED either refuse in turn or say so in its output.** A silent rc=0 egest from a known-stale store is a data-loss path that presents as a routine sync. **DISTINCT SUBJECT FROM AC-03.5/AC-03.6 (ic 2026-08-18, catching this before the wrong remedy was cited): those govern ATTACHMENT bytes against a commit; this episode destroyed a CRITERION, which no attachment check reaches.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.14 **A WRITE MOVES MTIME ON EXACTLY THE FILES WHOSE BYTES CHANGED, AND NO OTHERS.** (cc 2026-08-18, third formulation; the row reached its fifth version because vc named the wrong ROW, cc the wrong SUBJECT, then the wrong PROPERTY, then vc the wrong DENOMINATOR.) Measured over the FILE ESTATE before and after -- snapshot `(path -> bytes, mtime)`, run the verb, snapshot again, assert `{p : mtime differs} == {p : bytes differ}` AS SETS. **The denominator is the ESTATE and never the `WriteSet`**: `WriteSet` is internal, so a test reading its denominator from the type would satisfy this row while `intent sync` stayed broken, and would additionally be blind to any write that never joins a set. **Measured at every USER-VISIBLE verb that writes the estate, with the verb set ENUMERATED AND PRINTED**, so a verb added later and not covered is visible rather than silently absent. **31 at minting** (cc, mapped rather than guessed): 27 through the nine `apply` sites -- `st new`; `st start|done|cancel|triage|hold|resume|reopen|reinstate`; `wp new`; `wp rescope`; `wp start|done|reopen|unstart`; `ac satisfy|unsatisfy|descope|rescope|withdraw|reinstate`; `at green|red|na`; `issues add`; `issues close|open` -- plus `sync --to-disk`, `sync --to-store`, `todo update`, `upgrade`, across six commit sites (`facade.rs:768`, `:1023`, `:1084`, `:1192`, `:2676`, `migrate.rs:338`). `ac gate` was flagged unverified by cc and is NOT among them: verified by vc at `render.rs:788` to read and print only. **WHY _RUN TWICE WRITES ZERO FILES_ IS NOT THE CRITERION** (cc, the correction that matters): it is INAPPLICABLE to the nine `apply` verbs, where the churn is worst and where zero-moved is legitimately FALSE, since the mutated thread's rows genuinely change. A row built on the no-op case leaves the worst-affected verbs outside the denominator WHILE LOOKING COVERED. Zero-on-a-no-op is a COROLLARY; it was only ever the easiest instance to measure, never the property. **THE LIVE VIOLATION AT MINTING**: `apply` calls `projection(&next, &changed_threads, &changed_issues)` -- canon JSON correctly narrow, but views render from FULL canon at `facade.rs:1272`, so **every mutating verb rewrites all 266 views to change about two**, and `intent st start` touches 266 files. `WriteSet::commit` then writes every path unconditionally through `write_atomically`'s temp-plus-rename, **which moves mtime BY CONSTRUCTION because it swaps in a new inode**. Measured: a no-op sync moved 20/20 views, twice, against a pre-registered prediction that the second run would be quiet. **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT** (cc, and this belongs in the rationale rather than the report): AC-03.2 -- view determinism, same model, same bytes, twice -- is satisfied by the churn PERFECTLY. The estate HAD an idempotence criterion and this defect is invisible to it by construction. **Not that nobody wrote a check: the check that exists is complete and correct about its own subject while the thing beside it rots.** `views::write_all` carries a correct unchanged-skip and has NO PRODUCTION CALLER -- all 18 call sites are under `tests/` -- and `view_determinism.rs` drives it directly and is green throughout. **THE STATED COST OF THE EARLIER FORM WAS FALSE** (cc 2026-08-18, against their own sentence, found by re-measuring it instead of citing it): `write_all`'s doc claimed the mtime move corrupts `file_index`'s clean/changed state. **It does not** -- `sync.rs:317-326` decides `FileState` from SHA-256 alone and the module doc says stat is never a gate, on vc's 2026-08-14 contract-governs ruling. The real costs are that the criterion is ratified and unmet, that it defeats every external mtime instrument, and that touching hundreds of files per verb is the opposite of what ST0057 is about. **THE FIX IS ONE SKIP AT THE ONE WRITER**: `WriteSet::commit` already reads the prior content via `record()` for rollback, so the comparison is free and `written: false` is already the exact semantics for nothing-to-undo. `projection` being shared by three of the six commit sites is what makes one skip cover them all. Red-first must be driven at the VERB over the file estate -- never through `organize` (retired in v3), never a per-thread projection (a subset that happens to be wholly changed goes green against an unguarded writer), never `write_all`, never one verb. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.15 **A VERB THAT WOULD REDUCE A POPULATION TO ZERO MUST REFUSE OR NAME IT; SUCCEEDING AT rc=0 IS NOT AVAILABLE.** (ic 2026-08-18, from vc's live instance.) `sync --to-disk` wrote empty views over a non-empty estate at rc=0: `steel_threads.md` 57 rows -> 0, `todo.md` 82 -> 0. **NOTHING WAS REFUSED AND NOTHING MALFUNCTIONED IN THE EGEST** -- the store legitimately held zero threads, because a shared `target/release/` binary built from a reverted WP-01 tree had ingested zero and `sync --to-store` had reported success over it. The egest wrote exactly what it was given, correctly, by its own lights. **THIS IS NOT AC-03.13**, which requires an ingest to have been REFUSED; here nothing was refused, which is precisely why that row does not reach this and why the two are separate criteria rather than one. **The property is that a write which would empty a non-empty face must refuse or say so in its output.** **ic's own note is why this row is worth more than most tonight: it is the only finding of the evening whose positive control actually works** -- 57 and 82 are non-zero, observable before the verb runs, and cannot be confused with a correct answer, unlike a zero that is indistinguishable from a legitimately empty population. **Same class one verb upstream**: `sync --to-store` printing _the store and the extract agree_ over `0 == 0` is an instrument reporting success over an empty population, and this row is what that class looks like when the downstream verb is destructive. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.16 **NO GENERATED VIEW NAMES A GENERATED ARTEFACT AS A SOURCE OF TRUTH.** (hv 2026-08-18, who sampled ONE cover and hit a generator defect; minted by vc on hv's ruling. Measured at `40d27ba2`.) **`views.rs:329` emits _Acceptance Criteria and Acceptance Tests live in `acceptance.md` -- the single source of truth_, and `:653` emits the WP-level twin.** Under the ratified model `acceptance.md` is a GENERATED VIEW of `thread.json`; the store is durable truth (D01 as reversed) and the committed extract is the interchange (D34). **`acceptance.md` is truth of nothing.** **THIS IS A WORK-LOSS INSTRUCTION, NOT A WORDING DEFECT, AND THAT IS WHY IT IS A CRITERION.** A reader who ACTS on it authors an AC row in `acceptance.md`; **that row is discarded at the next `--to-disk`.** The document instructs the exact action the model punishes, and nothing currently refuses it. **THE GENERATOR EMITS BOTH HALVES OF A CONTRADICTION IN ONE RUN**: the cover names `acceptance.md` as truth, while `acceptance.md`'s own footer -- same generator, same run -- reads _Generated by Intent v3.0.0-dev from `thread.json`. Do not edit this file._ **Neither file is wrong about itself; the pair cannot both be true in a reader's model.** **EXTENT, MEASURED AND STATED SO IT IS NOT INHERITED: 57 of 57 ST covers and 149 of 150 WP covers -- 206 of 207.** The single WP that escapes is not noise and not a round number: `carries_heading(&wp.body, "Acceptance")` suppresses the emit where a body supplies its own heading, **so the exception is the conditional working and it is what proves the population was counted rather than assumed.** **INSTRUMENT: THE CHECK READS EMITTED VIEWS AND RESOLVES WHAT EACH NAMED PATH IS -- NEVER THE GENERATOR'S STRING LITERALS.** The two would answer oppositely: rephrasing the literal while still naming a generated path passes a source grep and leaves the defect intact. **The generator knows every path it writes, so _a truth-claim naming one of its own outputs_ is mechanically decidable.** **AND A PHRASE GREP BOTH OVER- AND UNDER-REACHES, WHICH IS THE DENOMINATOR TRAP THIS ESTATE KEEPS PAYING FOR.** `views.rs` carries three occurrences of _truth_; **`:382` (_storing it would be double truth_) is CORRECT** -- satisfaction is computed from covering green ATs and never stored. **The denominator is every truth-claim the generator emits, not every hit of one phrase**, and a check keyed to the phrase would flag the correct one and miss any defect worded differently. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.17 **A GENERATED VIEW MUST BE A FIXED POINT OF THE PROJECT'S FORMATTER: the render output survives the formatter unchanged, or the skew check oscillates forever.** (vc 2026-08-19, settling cc's AC-00.10 hand-off. Mechanism established WITHOUT rendering, then confirmed by accident.) **THE RENDERER IS PROVABLY INCAPABLE OF CAUSING THIS.** `criterion_line` (`views.rs:463`) is `format!("- {} ", c.id)` + an optional `(non-test) ` + `push_str(&c.text)` VERBATIM; `finish()` (`:217`) only pops trailing newlines and appends the footer. `cell()` -- the escaper -- is not on this path at all. So the renderer does not merely fail to collapse whitespace, **it never operates on the string.** **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS INLINE CODE SPAN VERSUS PROSE, AND IT IS A MECHANISM ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A CORRELATION: A RENDERER-SIDE COLLAPSE CANNOT BE BACKTICK-SENSITIVE; A MARKDOWN FORMATTER NECESSARILY IS, because backtick spans are exactly what it must not reflow.** Canon-wide the population is three double spaces: ST0056 AC-00.10 x2, both prose at a `**bold**  **bold**` boundary -- EATEN; ST0050 AC-01.1 x1, inside `` `  - [ ] NN: title` `` -- SURVIVED. **THE CONFOUND WAS EXCLUDED AT ONE REVISION RATHER THAN ASSUMED:** at `54735e34` canon carried 2 and the committed view carried 0, and that same commit regenerated ST0050's view in the same 263-view sweep. **Same commit, same pipeline, same file kind, same `- AC-XX.Y` construct: one eaten, one survived. ST0050 is a CONTROL, not a counterexample.** **CONSEQUENCE, AND IT IS WHY THE INSTANCE IS NOT THE FIX: `doctor` reported 263 skew going to 0 across that commit, so its zero was TRUE WHEN TAKEN AND FALSE BY THE TIME IT WAS COMMITTED.** Generate two spaces, formatter collapses to one, skew check sees drift, regenerate two spaces -- forever, **with every pass looking like a legitimate repair.** **SECOND INSTANCE OF A PAIR THE CODEBASE ALREADY DOCUMENTS AND ALREADY FIXED ONCE:** `views.rs`'s own `kv()` comment records emitting `key:` with no trailing space because prettier strips trailing whitespace and the two would *oscillate forever* -- **found by running the real formatter.** The remedy there is the remedy here: make the RENDERER emit what the formatter would leave alone. **Repairing AC-00.10's text is the trap -- it looks like a repair and leaves the loop.** **EMPIRICALLY CONFIRMED 2026-08-19 by an experiment nobody was willing to run:** cc's accidental `intent upgrade` regenerated `ST0056/acceptance.md` carrying **2** double spaces against the committed file's **0**. Renderer emits, formatter eats. **The finding was already established by mechanism; this removed the last doubt.** **REMEDY CLAUSE WITHDRAWN 2026-08-19 (cc measured it; vc ruled), AND THE PROPERTY SURVIVES IN A STRONGER FORM.** This row prescribed _make the RENDERER emit what the formatter would leave alone_, and that remedy CONTRADICTS THIS ROW'S OWN MECHANISM ARGUMENT stated three sentences earlier -- _the renderer does not merely fail to collapse whitespace, it never operates on the string_. **The row refuted itself, which is AC-11.1's class committed by the row that names a second writer.** **cc BUILT THE PRESCRIBED REMEDY AND THE POPULATION REFUSED IT: over 6,095 authored lines a conservative renderer-side rule produced EIGHT changes where the real formatter makes SEVEN.** The extra is ST0056 AC-10.9, whose prose runs backticks straight into words, so the real parser's code-span boundaries fall where no hand-rolled segmenter can see them -- **and rewriting a site the formatter leaves alone is the same oscillation with the arrow reversed, invisible to anything short of running the real formatter.** **AND IT WAS ALREADY FORBIDDEN IN WRITING: `view_determinism.rs:248` records that authored prose passes the renderer VERBATIM by rule, because `migration.md` forbids reflowing it -- so normalising markup in the renderer is the one thing the renderer may not do.** That constraint predates both nodes looking, and it also named the structural fix and declined to take it unilaterally. **THE RULED REMEDY IS D02 APPLIED TO WRITERS RATHER THAN TO CONTENT: A GENERATED VIEW HAS ONE WRITER AND IT IS THE RENDERER.** The cause was never an output form -- `.git/hooks/pre-commit` runs `prettier --write` over every staged markdown file and `git add`s its own rewrite, so all 266 generated views had a second writer and a silent one. Closed by `.prettierignore` at `91380693`. **SO THE DURABLE PROPERTY IS ONE WRITER AND FIXED-POINT-NESS WAS THE SYMPTOM: a fixed point is a coincidence somebody must maintain forever; an exclusion is structural.** **DEMONSTRATED LIVE RATHER THAN STAGED: vc synced mid-session with the exclusion in place and the regenerated view came back carrying canon's `*KIND*` against HEAD's `_KIND_`, all seven divergences gone.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.2 View rendering is deterministic and idempotent: same model renders the same bytes, twice, on both platforms -- **and idempotent THROUGH the project's markdown formatter, not merely through the renderer**: the rendered bytes are a fixed point, so running the formatter over a freshly generated view changes nothing (ic's ruling, accepted 2026-08-15). Held on a consequence, not on tidiness: AC-03.4 regenerates a committed view and requires an empty diff, so a renderer idempotent only against itself makes that check fire permanently on a file nobody touched, and the first thing anyone does with a check that cries wolf is switch it off -- the false-green family arriving through the front door. Three measured instances in one artefact family in one day (`dispatch-table.md`, `register.md`, `pertest.md`), and they split across two causes that both have to hold: **layout** (column widths the formatter re-aligns) and **markup** (canonical forms the formatter rewrites, eg `*emphasis*` to `_emphasis_`). Fixing it at the renderer is one change; fixing it at the skew check is an exception per view forever -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.3 The stat-scan detects external edits by content hash, including a same-size same-mtime rewrite -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.4 The skew check catches a hand-edited generated view and names the file (never silently outvotes it) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.5 A conflict-markered artefact enters the named unparsed state; commands needing it refuse with the finding; v2's silent grep-through is unconstructible -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.6 Prose bodies ingest verbatim into FTS-indexed doc_sections; a body round-trips byte-identical out of the store and is retrievable by full-text query -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.7 A gitignored path is outside the ingest corpus: it produces no residue, blocks no read, and a project containing one ingests exactly as if it were absent (D29) -- while an untracked-but-not-ignored artefact is still ingested, and a project with no git degrades to everything-in-scope. Each residue path is reported once. **The corpus is a function of the repository, never of the machine**: the ignore sources are the ignore files committed in the tree, and NOT `core.excludesFile`. Reopened WP-03 after close: found at WP-06 measuring `intent search`, whose 24 residue lines were 100% `.DS_Store` and 100% gitignored, which makes every macOS checkout unreadable and every macOS migration BLOCKED at AC-10.2. **Machine-scope defect measured at `2e490e5`, which is why this stays unsatisfied**: identical bytes in `intent/probe_global.sql` and `intent/probe_local.dat` yield a residue line for the second and silence for the first, because `~/.gitignore_global:20` carries `*.sql`. **Correction to my own second claim, from cc**: I reported the asymmetry also holding "in a project with no `.git` at all", and it does not -- a standalone directory outside any repository returns an empty ignore set exactly as this AC requires. My fixture had no `.git` **of its own** but sat inside one (`/Users/matts/.claude` is a repository, and the scratch directory is under it), and git resolves a repo at-or-above, correctly. So the AC's no-git wording was right and only the implementation was wrong -- the defect is the machine-scope one above, and my supporting claim overstated its reach in the direction that made it look worse. D29 derives the rule from "a path git can never commit can never be canon", and a path excluded only by one developer's global excludes is freely committable by anyone else, so it was never in that class. Two developers at one commit ingest different corpora, invisibly, and AC-10.2 makes that difference decide a migration BLOCK -- a fleet member can migrate cleanly on one machine and block on another. This repository already collides with the pattern: it commits `schema/ddl.sql` and carries a `!` exception solely to defeat the global `*.sql`. The `.git/info/exclude` sub-choice is **closed as DROP** (cc's call, ratified by vc 2026-08-15 on the same derivation): it is per-clone and uncommitted, so a fresh clone of one commit would disagree about what the project contains, and a path excluded there is one `git add` from being committed by anyone who has not written it -- never in D29's "can NEVER be committed" class. **Fixed at `3ebaf55` and verified independently at `fc6bbc6`** on three identical-byte probes in a purpose-built repository: the committed-`.gitignore` probe is excluded, the global-only probe is now IN scope, the unmatched probe is in scope, and Intent correctly disagrees with `git check-ignore` on exactly the global-only file. The no-git arm re-measured outside any repository -- `.DS_Store`, `.sql` and unmatched all in scope, degrading to everything-in-scope as the AC requires -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.8 Canon round-trips through the store: for every entity this WP ingests, canon -> DB -> canon is byte-identical, and a field that cannot survive the round-trip fails here rather than at migration. The first half of ic's egest-symmetry proposal, accepted 2026-08-15, and homed here rather than at WP-10 because this is where an unreversible FIELD is still cheap to change -- discovering it at WP-10 is ic's own "worst possible moment" argument applied to our schedule. AC-03.6 already holds the hardest single piece (prose bodies byte-identical out of FTS); this generalises it to the whole model. Adding it reopens nothing that AC-03.7 has not already reopened, which is what makes the placement free -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-03.9 **`intent sync` names its direction and refuses to guess.** Under the reversed D01 the two directions are not symmetric: **disk-to-db is a RESTORE that overwrites the SSOT from a projection** (`ingest::resync` reads the files then `store.rebuild` replaces the estate), and running it against stale files destroys committed truth. ~~**db-to-disk is the routine direction and does not exist at all** -- measured 2026-08-15, no such function in `intentsvcs`.~~ **STRUCK 2026-08-15 (cc measured, vc corrected): `Facade::sync_to_disk` exists and `st sync --write` has been calling it since the projection landed.** The measurement was true when written and stopped being true without this row noticing -- **a stale measurement in a criterion is worse than no measurement, because it reads as current evidence.** db-to-disk exists; what does not exist is the CLI's ability to NAME either direction. Required: the destructive direction states what it will overwrite before doing it and never runs from a bare verb; the routine direction exists; and the bare verb REFUSES and makes the operator choose rather than defaulting -- D05's refusal posture applied to a direction, because a verb whose two directions differ in destructiveness must not have a silent default. **A remedy string, error message or doc that tells an operator to run the destructive direction to recover is itself the defect** -- one already existed and cc struck it while writing it. Until db-to-disk ships, a failed file projection is repairable only by the next successful mutation, and that must be stated where an operator will meet it rather than discovered through the gap. **NAMED CLEANUP when the selector ships (vc, 2026-08-15): `crates/intent-cli/tests/search_surface.rs:56` and `cli_end_to_end.rs:591` both DELETE the store to force a cold re-ingest, because the CLI cannot yet spell the disk-to-db direction.** They are D36 violations left in place deliberately, on cc's argument that hiding them behind the clone fixture would remove the only pressure to ship this selector -- **a later D36 sweep would then come back clean while the gap persisted**, which is a green meaning "the workaround is well hidden". **This is the standing surfaces obligation running in reverse: a deferral names what it will unblock**, so the removal is a named consequence of AC-03.9 landing rather than a rediscovery by whoever next sweeps for D36. **CLOSED 2026-08-15, verified by vc: `sync_direction.rs` 6 green including `a_restore_from_stale_files_destroys_a_change_the_store_alone_holds` and `the_overwrite_is_named_before_the_restore_runs`, and BOTH named D36 sites are gone** -- `search_surface.rs` and `cli_end_to_end.rs` now run the command instead of deleting the store, and a sweep of the tree finds no store-deletion site at all. **The cause was that the selector was DECLARED and UNREAD**: `run` matched `Some(("sync", _))` and discarded the `ArgMatches`, so ic's two rows could not be read even in principle while `sync --help` listed them -- which is AC-06.8's whole class, found in the row that named it. **RULING on cc's question about `search`'s empty-index remedy naming `intent sync --to-store` (vc, 2026-08-15): cc's reading of THIS row is correct and the remedy is still wrong, on a ground cc did not check.** The "to recover" clause does not catch it -- authored prose is disk-native under D02, so for prose disk-to-db is the only path it has and not a recovery. **What catches it is that the remedy's BLAST RADIUS EXCEEDS THE PROBLEM.** The problem is an unpopulated prose index; `--to-store` replaces the entire store from the extract, and **`event_log` is the one table that is durable truth AND not reconstructible from the files**, so an operator who follows that remedy to fix a search result can lose history that exists nowhere else. **The precondition is reachable and was measured: at `8d9b964`, `doc_sections` stayed 0 through both `sync` and a full `doctor` rebuild while `threads` was 1** -- a populated store with an empty prose index is exactly the state the remedy fires in. Until a narrower verb exists, **name the fact without naming the command**, which is the rewording cc offered. The general form, worth more than this instance: **a remedy must not propose an operation whose blast radius exceeds the fault it repairs**, and "the direction is routine for this data" is not the same claim as "this command is routine for this data" -- satisfied: yes (computed)

### WP-04 -- intentsvcs facade: core command families (status: WIP)

- AC-04.1 st/wp lifecycle verbs (new, start, done, cancel, list, show) run through the facade with **the DB write transactional and all-or-nothing, and the file projection unwound on failure**: a failure mid-write leaves the DB either fully changed or untouched, and leaves the files STALE but never TORN. **The `TornRollback` case -- where the unwind itself fails and the files really are torn -- is a named error and MUST be exercised**, because it is the only path to torn files and it is currently reachable and untested (`write_set.rs:35,114,136`; zero occurrences anywhere under `crates/*/tests/`). (Reworded 2026-08-15 for D01's reversal. The original said "canon + views + DB written transactionally" as one guarantee, which is what the reversal deliberately broke apart -- files are now permitted to lag the truth. cc's reading that the guarantee survives is right; the SENTENCE did not, and it claimed a single transaction across three artefacts that the model no longer provides. Stale is the whole meaning of re-creatable; torn is still a defect) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-04.2 ac/at operations implement the four AC states with computed satisfaction for test-backed ACs (never stored) and inline evidence for non-test -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-04.3 The close-gate reads the model and reproduces v2 gate verdicts -- including every one of v2's own gate rules that remains constructible in v3, each with its own distinguishable diagnosis, proven against contracts built to trip them rather than against a corpus that happens to be clean -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-04.4 Every facade error is typed and renders a remedy with its full cause chain (no same-text-for-different-causes collapses) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-04.5 Every mutation path writes an event-log envelope carrying principal + project_id (the D15 seams exist end to end; renumbered from AC-02.6 at WP-02 close per the 2026-08-14 bounce ruling) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-04.6 **Mutation completeness (D32): every state an entity can enter, it can leave, by a service call reachable from every surface.** No state transition exists only as a hand-edit. Held to a mechanical test rather than a review: for each modelled state field, the set of transitions offered by the service layer is closed -- an entity that can be moved into a state and not out of it fails, naming the state and the missing inverse. Ruled by hv 2026-08-15 on a concrete instance vc hit while using the apparatus: `intent ac satisfy` is a one-way door, `rescope`/`reinstate` only undo a descope, so a verifier whose evidence proved incomplete had to hand-edit `acceptance.md` -- the file the CLI exists to own -- to reopen an AC. **A state that can be entered and not left is a missing mutation, not a missing flag.** In WP-04 because `ac`/`at` are its families; the same rule binds D30's whiteboard operations and anything else that models state. **STRENGTHENED AGAIN 2026-08-15 once hv ratified the three state machines (`data-model.md`, State machines): the implemented graph must MATCH THE RATIFIED MACHINES EXACTLY -- no undeclared edge, no missing declared edge, no undeclared state.** Closure is now the weaker half of this: a graph can be closed and still be the wrong graph. This changes the walk's question from _is the code closed?_ to _does the code implement the ratified machine?_, and it turns every verb the machines require -- `st triage`, `st hold`, `st resume`, `st reopen`, `st reinstate`, `wp reopen`, `wp unstart` -- from prose into a red test until it exists. **Evidence that this was needed rather than tidy: `wp done` had no inverse, so two WPs reopened in the contract kept reporting `Done` while their own gate said BLOCKED, and a third reported `WIP` on a PASSING gate -- three of five units disagreeing with themselves, in this thread, caused by the verifier.** **STRENGTHENED 2026-08-15 on cc's own mutation finding, because the clause above is necessary and NOT sufficient and was proven so against the ruled defect itself**: with scope changes clearing satisfaction, deleting `ac.unsatisfy` left `satisfied: true` formally leavable via descope-then-rescope, so the closure walk went green over the exact one-way door hv ruled on. The exit was real and useless -- withdrawing a claim of evidence would mean moving the requirement to another thread and bringing it back, recording two false facts to undo one true one. **The sufficient form: a state you can only leave by changing a DIFFERENT field is still a state you cannot leave.** Edges are therefore classified Direct or Incidental; an incidental edge counts for reachability and NEVER discharges a trap. Noted because it generalises: cc's own correct fix is what disarmed the test for the defect it was written against. **SECOND CONDITION, vc 2026-08-15: a field declared `Unbuilt` discharges this AC only if an entity cannot HOLD a non-initial value of it by any path, ingest included -- not merely if it carries no declared edges.** A field settable at creation or arriving by ingest has been entered, and an entity sitting in a state no service call can leave fails this AC whether or not the disposition table says the work is owed elsewhere. Asserting that the `Unbuilt` rows name a WP and carry no edges checks the BOOKKEEPING and not the property, which is the same shape as the mutation above -- a check right about the set it looked at, looking at the wrong set -- satisfied: no (computed)

and **(b)** `spine.rs:193` mapped clap's `DisplayHelp` to `EXIT_OK` with `spine.rs:27` naming the divergence in as many words, while the register still called it open -- **the code honest and the honesty never reaching the register.** Requires a check that cross-reads the register's `target.state` against **both** the ratified deviation classes in `parity.md` and the measured behaviour of the binary at HEAD, and REPORTS every disagreement. **Reports rather than refuses**, on ic's own reasoning for `surface_check.sh`: most commands are unwired mid-ladder and a gate would block every node on work that has not started; here something happened, at exit 0, and the operator is shown a different question's answer. **Requires a check that, for every shipped entry declaring an argument with two or more legal values, drives the entry twice differing ONLY in that value and REPORTS a byte-identical stdout+stderr+exit** -- the pair chosen so the two values differ in v2's answer, and preferably straddle v2's own exit-code boundary, because two values that legitimately agree produce the same identical result as an argument nobody reads. **A value that is genuinely inert is DECLARED inert with a reason, never skipped** -- the roster construction this thread has already used twice (dc's `gated`/`manual`, the register's `keep`/`retire`), so an undeclared silent argument is a refusal of the check rather than a gap in it. **Reports rather than refuses**, on AC-05.5's reasoning: families land through the ladder and a gate would block every node on work that has not started. **Scope, stated as a denominator because the count is the whole argument for building it early: 10 of the 105 dispatch entries declare such an argument.
