### WP-06 -- CLI parity long tail (status: WIP)

- AC-06.1 The full command surface is ported and the conformance suite is green minus register-recorded deviations only, with `coverage_map.sh` reporting no command family at zero burning coverage -- `intent config` lands a conformance test BEFORE its behaviour is designed, or the `undefined` ruling on it is unverifiable by construction -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-06.10 **Every published schema face carries `INTENT_VER` and `SCHEMA_<TYPE>_VER`, injected from code and discoverable without parsing the artefact** (D41, hv 2026-08-15). Three face types -- `SCHEMA_JSON_VER`, `SCHEMA_DDL_VER`, `SCHEMA_SDL_VER` -- across five files, matching AC-06.5's count; the three JSON Schemas share one version because they are one contract in three documents, and splitting per file would let `thread` and `issue` drift apart with nothing saying they had. **The two parts answer different questions and move at different rates**: `INTENT_VER` says which tool produced the artefact, `SCHEMA_<TYPE>_VER` says whether the contract a consumer compiles against has changed. A patch release moves the first and must not move the second. Requires: **(a)** the versions are constants in code and the generator injects them into each face in that face's own idiom -- **hand-writing a version into a generated artefact recreates the exact defect this closes**, which is the "generated artefact with a hand-kept companion" pattern this thread met three times in one day; **(b)** a surface reports both parts for every face, so a consumer deciding whether to upgrade can ASK rather than diff; and **(c)** AC-06.5's byte-identity property holds WITH the versions present, so the injected values are compared rather than exempted. **Measured before ruling (vc, 2026-08-15): zero occurrences of "version" in any of the five faces**, while the internal never-committed store had carried `SCHEMA_VERSION` and a hard refusal since that morning -- the internal artefact versioned and the external contract not, which is backwards, because the store rebuilds from the extract and a consumer's code rebuilds from nothing we hold. **Deliberately NOT extended to help text**: changing a help string breaks nobody's code. **The line is whether a consumer COMPILES against it**, which is also why the instrument is a version and not a text baseline -- a baseline tells us something changed, a version tells the consumer -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-06.11 **Every remedy the binary suggests names something the binary can actually do.** **WIDENED 2026-08-15 (vc) on ic's measurement, deliberately as ONE row rather than two**: the property is that a remedy is ACTIONABLE, and it fails identically whether the remedy names a verb that does not exist or a verb-SPACE that does not exist. **Measured: 17 commands are unimplemented and NINE are leaves with zero verbs** -- `info`, `init`, `bootstrap`, `learn`, `fileindex`, `version`, `export`, `ingest`, `mcp` -- and each sends the user to `run intent <cmd> --help for the verbs that are`, a `--help` listing no verbs at all. **One property over one corpus, the emitted remedies**, which is the test: I split the schema faces off AT-00.8 the same afternoon on a corpus argument and had to reverse it within the hour when the corpus turned out to be the same one. (vc 2026-08-15, ruled to the contract on dc's question rather than into `int macos publish`, which is where dc proposed it and correctly declined to build it.) **Live instance**: `brew install` puts v3 at PATH position 1 while a v2 install sits at 17 and 19, so a v2 user meets v3's unmigrated-project refusal without having asked for anything -- and **that refusal's remedy names `intent upgrade`, which the v3 binary does not have** (`migration.md:3` says the migrator IS that verb, so it is WP-10 unbuilt, not a wrong string). **The ruling is about WHERE the control goes, and it is what un-couples WP-11 from WP-10.** dc could have made `publish` refuse to ship a binary whose remedies name unreachable verbs, and declined because gating publish on another work package's progress is a sequencing ruling wearing a mechanism's clothes. **That objection is right, and it dissolves once the check moves**: the harm is not "we published", it is "a binary tells a user to run a verb it does not have" -- **a property of the artefact, true the moment the string is written and equally true of a binary nobody ever publishes.** A publish-time gate catches it at the last possible moment, on the node least able to fix it, and only if a release happens; a build-time invariant fires when the string lands, on whoever wrote it, and asserts something true of v3 independent of when `upgrade` arrives. Requires a check that walks every operator-facing remedy string and fails naming any that references a verb the surface does not carry -- **the same shape as AC-06.8's declared-versus-read walk, one artefact over.** Its acceptance test is **held RED with an explicit note** until WP-10 lands `upgrade` (the AT-06.4 / AT-06.7 practice): **a red row with a note is the honest state and names its dependency, where a missing check reads as covered** -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-06.12 **An operator's spelling of an id resolves to the id, and a spelling that names nothing is refused AS A SPELLING rather than reported as a missing artefact.** Two-sided, and the second limb is the one that was failing widest. v2 accepts five spellings via `bin/intent_helpers:688 normalise_st_id` -- `46`, `ST46`, `0046`, `046`, `ST0046`, driven in the v2 checkout with the error echoing the NORMALISED id -- and v3 kept the issue-side equivalent (`render.rs:3035 issue_number`, whose doc cites v2's `normalize_id` BY NAME) while dropping the thread side entirely. **THE REGRESSION WAS NOT THE HALF THAT WAS FILED.** `st edit 59` refused through `address::promote`; the other nine thread verbs hit a raw `t.id == id` and answered `no steel thread 59 in this project` -- **A NOT-FOUND FOR SOMETHING THAT WAS NEVER A NAME**, which is precisely the answer `promote`'s own doc names as the one that matters, honoured in one place and routed around by nine. **AND v2 IS WHERE THAT DEFECT COMES FROM, ONE STEP WORSE:** its fourth branch echoes any input back with `ST` glued on -- `foo` becomes `STfoo` -- so v2 FABRICATES a plausible id and then reports it missing. **The parity contract is the five accepted forms, never the function.** Two v2 behaviours are deliberately not ported and both are holes: that branch, and `printf "ST%04d"` being a MINIMUM width, which turns `99999` into `ST99999` -- reproduced exactly by `"99999".parse::<u32>()` unless the range is checked, so being immune to v2's octal hazard bought nothing there. The `10#` guard IS ported as a property and not as a mechanism: without it /bin/bash 3.2 reads `0044` as octal and yields ST0036, a different real thread, which Rust cannot express. Adds `s46`/`i46` explicit tags, which exist so the one collection-agnostic door has a spelling to RECOMMEND rather than only a refusal -- measured, `59` names both `ST0059` and issue `0059` in this estate and both exist. **AUTHORED BY cc, LANDED INTO CANON BY vc**, who holds ST0056: the write serialises through one hand to remove the race, and the knowledge is cc's. -- satisfied: yes (computed)

- AC-06.4 `intent search` returns hits across ST prose, issue bodies and WP text from the FTS index, in the shipped voice and exit codes (new surface with no v2 antecedent -- an ADDITION in the register, never a deviation). **First measurable at `8d9b964` once `sync` was wired. It did not pass then; it does now -- `search_surface.rs` is green and finds a word in authored thread prose, in an issue body, in a WP title and in a thread objective, refuses a malformed query with cause and remedy, and refuses outside a project. That is real and this row credits it.** On a purpose-built v3 fixture: `thread.json` carries a unique phrase in `context`, `sync` exits 0 and regenerates `info.md` **containing that phrase**, `file_index` reaches 6 rows and `threads` 1 -- and `doc_sections` stays **0** through both `sync` and a full `doctor` rebuild. So `search` for that phrase returns exit 0 with zero bytes, byte-identical to the negative control for a phrase that exists nowhere. **The shape is the load-bearing part, not the missing rows**: a search over an unpopulated index is indistinguishable from a search with no matches, which is cc's own sentence about the `doc_sections` staleness bug arriving one level up -- and it is the AC-10.7 silent-empty class in a fourth command, this time on a project that IS migrated. Whatever populates the FTS index must be observable from the outside, and a no-match should not be the same bytes as a no-index. Whether a genuine no-match is silent at all is a voice question for the register, not settled here. **STILL OPEN 2026-08-15 (vc), and it is this row's own load-bearing sentence: the green covers the HITS and does not cover the SHAPE.** `no_match_is_exit_zero_and_silent` asserts a genuine miss is exit 0 with empty stdout; nothing anywhere asserts that an UNPOPULATED index is distinguishable from that miss, which is the property this row was written for. **CLOSED 2026-08-15, verified by vc re-running it: `an_unpopulated_index_is_not_the_same_answer_as_a_genuine_miss` (10 pass).** An empty result over an unpopulated index says so on **stderr** and names what populates it, while **stdout stays empty in BOTH cases** -- so a grep-shaped caller keeps its contract, a miss stays exit 0, and the diagnosis goes where it cannot corrupt a pipe. The count is asked only on the empty path, so a successful search is not taxed to inform the empty one. **The finding inside the fix is the better half and it is cc's own test: `no_match_is_exit_zero_and_silent` WAS the defect.** Its fixture was a bare `st new`, so the index was empty -- the test believed it was proving "searched and found nothing" and was exercising "never searched anything", **which is precisely the pair this criterion exists to separate, and it passed either way.** A test written to prove a distinction, whose fixture collapses the distinction, is the purest form of the vacuous green in this thread -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-06.5 `intent schema` prints the three committed faces (JSON Schema, DDL, SDL), and what it prints is byte-identical to the files under `schema/` (re-homed from WP-02's deliverable list -- see the WP-02 note below) -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-06.6 `intent export --format <fmt>` projects the canon into a non-canonical format and round-trips: what it emits re-ingests to a byte-identical canon, or the format is refused by name rather than emitted lossily. This is D03's mechanism -- "YAML/md/anything else are `intent export --format` projections" (design.md:57) -- and it is the reason v3 can refuse YAML canon without refusing YAML users (surfaced by cc: `export` was an `owner_wp:WP-06` row in the dispatch SSOT with no acceptance criterion at all -- the same uncovered-deliverable class as `intent schema`, one artefact over, and this time in an artefact that IS read mechanically) **SCOPE NOTE (vc 2026-08-19, PROVISIONAL-PENDING-hv, raised by dc while building ST0057 WP-06): THIS CRITERION'S TWO DISPOSITIONS ARE THE INTERCHANGE POPULATION, AND THE TEXT REALISATION IS A THIRD DISPOSITION OUTSIDE IT.** ST0057's AC-06.3 requires `export --format md` to be ACCEPTED, and AC-06.2 requires the realisation to have NO read-back route -- asserted rather than intended. **Read together with this row's _round-trips OR is refused by name_, that looks like a contradiction. It is a CONFLATION.** **AC-06.6 governs INTERCHANGE PROJECTIONS -- canon into another MACHINE format, which is D03's mechanism and the reason v3 can refuse YAML canon without refusing YAML users. The text realisation is not one:** `.backup/text/<UTC>/`, no import path, `classify` never sees it, never authoritative. **It is a HUMAN FALLBACK, and md is simply not in this criterion's population.** **CONSEQUENCE FOR WHOEVER BUILDS AT-06.6: `Projection` has exactly two variants and the realisation fits neither** -- it cannot be `RoundTrips` (no `read`, and AC-06.2 FORBIDS one) and it cannot stay `Lossy` (that is the refusal AC-06.3 retires). **A third variant is entailed by AC-06.3 and vc ruled it in scope as the work rather than an addition to it.** **`RoundTrips`'s own doc says both halves are required _which is the state AC-06.6 exists to rule out_ -- that remains correct for interchange formats and must not be relaxed for them.** The realisation is exempt because it is not offered as interchange, not because the rule is soft. **A two-valued type meeting a third state, and the fourth instance in one day: `ratified_in` cannot express PROVISIONAL, `cargo test` rc=101 cannot separate DID NOT BUILD from a test failure, and `realised_threads`' `None` cannot separate an ABSENT manifest from an UNPARSEABLE one.**

**RULED BY hv 2026-08-20 AND NO LONGER PROVISIONAL.** The scope note above stands as written: **AC-06.6 governs INTERCHANGE projections, and the text realisation is a third disposition outside that population.** hv ruled the consequence dc surfaced while building to it -- `export --format md` DELEGATES to `Facade::realise()` rather than emitting a second markdown document. **dc's addition, which the note could not have known: it is a third OUTPUT SHAPE and not only a third disposition.** Both existing `Projection` variants assume a single-String artefact; the realisation is a directory tree, so the variant carries NO function pointer at all, as `Lossy` already does -- the roster declares what a format CAN do and the caller performs it. -- satisfied: no (computed)

Happy to withdraw it for now as a mutation."_ **hv's question is the better half of the ruling and it generalises: a diagnostic that NAMES the exact remedy may be strictly better than one that performs it.** The operator sees what will happen, decides whether it is what they meant, and keeps the blast radius in their own hands -- which is D05's refusal posture arriving one level up, and the same reasoning as dc's "the control goes where the harm is" and cc's declining to wire it in the first place. **A repair verb is a claim that the tool understands the fault well enough to act on it unattended; The table row carries disposition: retire with disposition_basis citing vc's measurement of bin/intent_doctor:66 (v2 really implements --fix, so retire is the right class rather than pending). Withdrawal is by construction, not by omission: Flag::ships() is disposition == keep, a POSITIVE match, and spine.rs skips any flag that does not ship. Guarded both directions by dispatch_ssot.rs, which fails on (ships=false, present=true) against the rendered help block, using a boundary check written specifically because --fix is a substring of --fixup. Falsified: at HEAD, --fix occurs in native/rust only in comments and in the at lint --fix refusal remedy, never as a doctor flag reaching the surface -- satisfied: yes

A green from a runner that asked no question is the swallow this estate forbids, and it is present TODAY, before any rule is armed. **(a) UNCONDITIONAL, AND NOT PART OF ANY TRADE-OFF: a rule the runner could not check is REPORTED -- named, with WHICH of three reasons applies (no proxy / no declaration / tool absent).** Reporting is not refusing; it costs no consumer anything and is owed however the rest is ruled. **(b) REFUSAL IS SCOPED TO A RULE THE PROJECT ARMED, and arming is the OPT-IN.** A project with no `.intent_critic.yml` has armed nothing, so nothing refuses and upgrade day is uneventful for it. **hv's ruling -- a tool-armed rule REFUSES when its tool is absent -- applies unweakened to the case it names: a project that armed a rule and then lost the tool** (a CI image change, a machine without shellcheck) is refused, not silently passed. **(c) THE DILEMMA DISSOLVES BECAUSE ITS TWO HORNS SHARED A FALSE PREMISE.** dc posed refuse-by-default (honest, and a fleet-wide breakage on upgrade day) against optional-by-default (safe, and _the invisible failure with a friendlier name_). **The invisibility was never a property of OPTIONAL; it was a property of the SILENT SKIP.** Fix the silence and an un-opted-in project is told, on every run, which rules are unenforced and what would enforce them -- **unenforced-and-stated is not invisible.** Nothing forced optional and silent together except the runner. **AND THE NAMED-TOOL BOUNDARY GAINS ITS WORKED EXAMPLE, WHICH NEEDS NO ADVERSARY** (dc, measured against clippy 0.1.97): `IN-RS-CODE-001`'s remedy is the **ABSENCE** of `--all-targets` -- default `cargo clippy` flags a production `.unwrap()` and not a colocated `#[cfg(test)]` one. **A rule file naming that flag would be a rule file contributing shell in order to make a lint see LESS.** The rule names WHICH tool; the runner owns HOW. **`critic_proxy_is_simple` remains an injection boundary and is not relaxed.** **A DECLARE-NONE MUST SAY WHY IT IS ONE:** `IN-SH-CODE-004` is declare-none because **shellcheck refuses zsh outright (`SC1071`) -- no tool exists** -- not because the runner is narrow, and those are different sentences to whoever reads the output next.

**CORRECTION 2026-08-20 (vc, against my own elaboration above, driven at zero hops on build `sha256 326990c5597284e7`). THE CLAUSE _a project with no `.intent_critic.yml` has armed nothing_ IS FALSE, AND IT WAS THE SAFETY ARGUMENT FOR THE WHOLE RULING.** That file carries `severity_min`, a `disabled:` list and `post_tool_use_advisory`, and **no arming key of any kind**. Arming is declared by the RULE -- `IN-SH-CODE-001(shellcheck)` -- never by the project. Driven: a scratch project with no yml at all, shellcheck hidden from PATH, one shell file -> **v2 exits 3**, and adding the yml changes nothing. **So the opt-in that 'upgrade day is uneventful for it' rests on does not exist.** The true statement is narrower and still sound: refusal is scoped to a rule that is ARMED and whose LANGUAGE the project declared, and **the opt-out is the `disabled:` list, not the file's absence.** The (c) dissolution is untouched -- unenforced-and-stated is still not invisible, and that never depended on the yml.

**THE SAME DRIVE FOUND (b) UNIMPLEMENTED IN v3, AND IT IS THE SHAPE OF INV-04 ONE FILE OVER.** Tool absent, `IN-SH-CODE-001` and `IN-SH-CODE-002` armed: **both binaries REPORT the absence correctly and name both rules -- case (a) is at parity -- and then v2 exits 3 while v3 exits 0.** The cause is two meanings of one word, stated five lines apart in v3's own file: `critic.rs:37` gives the header table _3 = refused -- a rule was armed and could not be enforced here_, which is this criterion's meaning, while `critic.rs:225` declares `refused` as _rules whose PROXY the contract refused_, which is ST0039's. `render.rs:3042` keys `Failure::Refused` on the latter, so exit 3 fires for a refused proxy and **never** for `not-run:tool-absent`. v2 sets `CRITIC_REFUSED=1` inside the `c_absent` block at `intent_critic:319`, a different condition entirely. **A machine missing shellcheck -- a CI image, a new laptop -- silently passes what hv ruled must block, and it passes every test that exists.**

**[THE PARAGRAPH ABOVE DECAYED, RE-MEASURED 2026-08-28 (vc, own two-sided drive, after dc's independent one the same hour): (b) IS IMPLEMENTED IN v3 AND THE 2026-08-20 MEASUREMENT IS STALE.** Against the delivered pair (`4479264f`): shellcheck absent from PATH -> rc=3, `REFUSED: 2 rule(s) armed by this project could not be enforced here: IN-SH-CODE-001 IN-SH-CODE-002`; the SAME PATH with shellcheck symlinked back in -> rc=0. One variable moves between the arms, so the 3 is caused by the tool's absence, not the stripped environment. dc's census arms 12/13/14 (`c5c9cce1`) assert exactly this two-sidedly -- arm 14 exists to guard this very gap and passes -- and the hook's distinct `3)` arm landed `2870b99d` on 2026-08-19, EIGHT DAYS BEFORE the assertion, so the 'both ends in one commit' condition AT-07.4's red note waited on was satisfied in the opposite order. ### WP-08 -- intentd daemon (status: WIP)

- AC-08.1 The registry serves N projects with per-connection binding; a moved or deleted root surfaces in doctor, never as a crash -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-08.10 **EVERY NEW STACK DECISION THE DAEMON INTRODUCES IS RULED AND RECORDED BEFORE IT IS ADDED, TO THE STANDARD THIS WORKSPACE ALREADY HOLDS.** **THE COUNT IS DERIVED FROM THE LOCK AND IS NOT WRITTEN HERE.** An earlier wording said *three*, fixed at a moment when a template layer was still in the stack; D56's reversal removed it hours later and the literal would have gone stale in a criterion nobody re-read. **A criterion that names a number instead of deriving one rots silently** -- third instance on this thread, after a row that stated a count and a row that stated line numbers. Measured 2026-08-29 (cc, verified by vc): the lock carries **no async runtime, no server and no filesystem watcher** -- zero rows for tokio, axum, hyper, actix-web, async-std, smol, tower, notify -- and `intentd/Cargo.toml` has no `[dependencies]` section at all. `async-graphql` is present and has only ever been used SYNCHRONOUSLY for the SDL export, so it pulled no runtime in. **So the additions WP-08 makes are this workspace's firsts of their kind**, and the check is that each dependency added to `intentd` carries a written rationale -- `uuid` carries three paragraphs explaining its addition alongside `ulid` -- **with D06's rusqlite prohibition surviving all of them.** The rationale may cite the sibling estate rather than re-argue from scratch: `../Conflab`'s `conflabd` runs tokio and axum on a 127.0.0.1 management plane whose `mgmt/` module is 10,341 lines including 3,648 of tests. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-08.11 (non-test) **THE DAEMON'S SIDE OF THE ROUTING CONTRACT: IT ANSWERS ON THE ACCEPT PATH, AND IT NARROWS THE DESCRIPTOR WINDOW IT CANNOT CLOSE.** Two obligations, both on `intentd` and neither testable by a client, split out of `AC-08.3` on 2026-08-30 (cc's finding).

**ONE -- THE PROBE IS ANSWERED AHEAD OF ANY PER-REQUEST WORK.** A liveness answer that can queue behind a slow request turns `AC-08.3`'s bounded deadline into a false NEGATIVE on a healthy daemon, and that is the worse direction: the CLI then runs in-process and **two sync engines are on one store, which is the single thing `design.md:22` exists to forbid.** So the daemon answers on accept, before dispatch, before any lock, before any store read.

**AND ANSWERING ON ACCEPT IS NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT, BECAUSE IT ORDERS THE WRONG AXIS.** The sentence above orders operations WITHIN a connection; the starvation that defeats the deadline happens BETWEEN them. A per-connection task answers on accept for its own connection and satisfies the clause literally, while the accept loop -- itself a task on the same runtime -- is never polled, because the facade is blocking `rusqlite` and N concurrent slow requests occupy N async worker threads. The probe's connection is still in the kernel backlog when the deadline expires, the client routes in-process against a store a live daemon owns, and **the row is satisfied by a daemon exhibiting the exact failure the row exists to prevent.** So the obligation is structural: **NO BLOCKING STORE CALL EVER OCCUPIES AN ASYNC WORKER THREAD** -- `spawn_blocking`, or a dedicated store thread, and handler code that cannot reach a blocking handle at all is the strongest form because the compiler then holds it. **AND THE WITNESS MUST BE STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN BEHAVIOURAL**: a latency test passes with the discipline deleted on an unloaded machine, so it measures the machine and not the code. **cc's finding, 2026-08-30, and cc declined to widen the row they were closing** -- which is the discipline the finding depended on: a builder who rewords their own criterion has laundered a defect through a green, and a widening that costs the builder nothing is the one nobody argues about.

**AND THIS OBLIGATION DOES NOT MAKE THE DEADLINE SOUND -- IT MAKES IT SOUND FOR THE ONE FAILURE MODE IT NAMES.** cc's sentence generalises further than cc applied it: *raising the constant is not the fix, because no constant is.* A daemon meeting this row exactly still misses any deadline under `SIGSTOP`, a suspended VM, or a loaded machine. **A probe is a guess about liveness; only a lock is a statement about it. THAT CLAUSE IS NOW CLOSED, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT NO LOCK REQUIREMENT IS OWED HERE** (cc, 2026-08-30, constructed against a fixture holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on the store, with a control before and after). **The store already serialises writes**: a second writer is REFUSED CLEANLY at rc=1 with an accurate message and a remedy, readers never block, and nothing is corrupted. **A whole sync is ONE transaction** -- `commit_mutation` has exactly one caller and takes every changed thread, issue, section, removal and creation as slices in a single `Mutation` -- so two engines cannot produce a half-applied sync: one wins entirely, the other is refused entirely.

**SO THE ROUTING RULE IS NOT A CORRUPTION GUARD, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT OF THIS PARAGRAPH.** What the probe actually buys is narrower: avoiding redundant ingest work, and giving one consistent view. **Neither is a safety property.** A criterion believed to prevent corruption that does not is worse than a known gap, because nobody looks for the missing control -- and the mutual exclusion is enforced WHERE THE WRITE HAPPENS rather than by anything upstream knowing who else is alive. **The estate learned that by losing data**: before the fix, `next_issue_number()` read a stale max against an `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` landing, so on 2026-08-28 a second writer was told `created:` while the first writer's issue was silently overwritten. The remedy was moving the uniqueness check INSIDE the transaction, which is the same shape as this answer.

**THE RESIDUAL IS DUPLICATED WORK, NOT CORRUPTION, AND IT IS STATED RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE FOUND.** The store serialises writes; it does not stop two engines both watching the filesystem and both ingesting. That is wasted work and last-writer-wins nondeterminism about which lands. **And the contended path costs a user a five-second pause before a clean rc=1** -- carried as an issue against the observable cost, never as a corruption risk this row would have implied.

**TWO -- `FD_CLOEXEC` IS SET IMMEDIATELY AFTER BIND, AND CHILD SPAWNS ARE KEPT OFF THE BIND PATH.** macOS has no atomic `SOCK_CLOEXEC`, so the window between the two syscalls cannot be removed, only narrowed. **The daemon owes the narrowing and the client owes surviving it** (`AC-08.3`), and neither substitutes for the other.

**WHY THIS IS A SEPARATE ROW AND NOT A CLAUSE, WHICH IS THE FINDING RATHER THAN THE FIX.** Both obligations sat inside `AC-08.3` until today, where they made a CLIENT criterion unsatisfiable by the client: there is no daemon, so no client behaviour could close the row. **vc wrote that clause and then, hours later, ruled that a daemon obligation must not live in a client criterion -- while the row being defended already carried one.** The rule failed in the artefact stating it, and cc found it. **The general form: a criterion's OWNER is whoever can satisfy it, and a row with two owners has none.** -- evidence: BOTH obligations. ONE (structural): the Facade never leaves native/rust/crates/intentd/src/store.rs, owned by a std::thread the tokio runtime does not schedule; handlers hold only a ProjectHandle, which is an mpsc sender, so no handler is given a type that can block. Witness is structural per the row's own requirement, not behavioural: tests/one_store_door.rs asserts Facade is named in exactly one module and that no other module calls blocking_recv/blocking_send/block_on/blocking_lock, with the predicate positive-controlled and comments stripped. TWO (FD_CLOEXEC): witnessed by tests/daemon_descriptor_hygiene.rs, which reads the flag off the descriptor each bind constructor returns, with a control that clears it, checks the mutation landed, and restores it. Landed 4b6b96e9 / 25f99c6f. -- satisfied: yes
- AC-08.12 (non-test) **SINGLE-DAEMON MUST NOT REST ON A LIVENESS PROBE. THE EXCLUSION IS HELD BY A KERNEL-OWNED LOCK WHOSE RELEASE IS A CONSEQUENCE OF THE PROCESS DYING, NOT OF THE PROCESS TIDYING UP.**

**MINTED 2026-08-30 (vc) ON cc's OWN DISCLOSURE, which is why the row exists at all: cc built the probe-based form, recognised it as the shape vc had ruled against one file over, and raised it rather than letting it be inherited.** `AlreadyRunning` today asks `Endpoint::answers` before deciding whether an existing socket path may be taken. **The row is not a criticism of that build** -- see the sequencing note below -- it is the guarantee the build must not be mistaken for.

**THE PROBE IS ADEQUATE FOR ROUTING AND INADEQUATE FOR EVICTION, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE COST OF BEING WRONG RATHER THAN THE ACCURACY OF THE ANSWER.** `AC-08.3`'s client uses the same predicate and a false negative there is WASTEFUL -- one redundant in-process run. Here the same false negative is DESTRUCTIVE: a daemon under `SIGSTOP`, on a suspended VM, or merely on a loaded machine fails the probe while perfectly alive, its socket is unlinked, and it is left **holding a listener on an unlinked inode, reachable by no path, serving nobody, silently.** **A predicate is not sound or unsound in itself; it is sound relative to what is done with the answer**, and this estate has now shipped one predicate into two decisions with opposite blast radii.

**AND IT IS EXACTLY THE RESIDUAL `AC-08.11` NAMES:** *no constant is the fix* -- because `SIGSTOP` and a suspended VM defeat any deadline. That sentence was written about a routing deadline and applies unchanged here, where the consequence is eviction.

**WHAT THE ROW REQUIRES IS THE OUTCOME, NOT THE MECHANISM** (`AC-11.1`'s rule): no live daemon is ever evicted, and no dead daemon ever blocks a start. **`flock` is the known-adequate mechanism and is named rather than mandated** -- it has NO false negatives, because the kernel releases it on death by any means including `SIGKILL`, so staleness cannot arise and slowness is never mistaken for absence. **It strictly dominates both options cc weighed**: refusing whenever the path exists makes one crash permanent, and probing evicts the living, while a lock does neither. **Priced before minting: `libc` is already a DIRECT dependency of `intent-cli` and already in the workspace lock, so this is a `Cargo.toml` line rather than a lockfile move** -- it does not collide with the deliberate hold on tokio and axum.

**THE ONE CAVEAT, STATED SO THE ROW IS NOT READ AS UNCONDITIONAL: `flock` is unreliable over NFS**, and a network home directory is not impossible on a developer machine. That does not change the requirement; it means a chosen mechanism owes a statement about what it does where its guarantee does not hold.

**SEQUENCING, AND THE PROBE-BASED FORM IS CORRECT UNTIL THIS LANDS.** cc's `Bound` already refuses to evict on the way out, compares `(dev, ino)` IDENTITY rather than content because a socket file has none, and reuses the CLIENT's definition of liveness so a daemon never uses a weaker test than its own clients -- **which is the Highlander half of this done right, and it is why a corpse socket no longer blocks every future start.** The lock replaces the probe in the START decision only. **The doc comment on the type already says the socket is not a mutex; this row is what stops that sentence being the only thing standing between the estate and a safety property resting on a side effect.** -- evidence: native/rust/crates/intentd/src/main.rs::serve_under calls Bound::bind_socket_under, whose try_lock on the daemon lock file IS the exclusion; released by fd close on process death rather than by tidying. Production call site as of e6aba646; refusal driven by daemon_address.rs::a_second_daemon_is_refused_even_when_the_first_is_not_answering (16 green). -- satisfied: yes
- AC-08.2 The dual-path conformance suite is green across the verb surface (in-process vs daemon, identical results) -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-08.3 **WHEN THE CLI ROUTES AT ALL, THE PREDICATE IS `EXISTS AND ANSWERS`, NEVER `PRESENT`, AND TWO SEPARATE CASES SEPARATE THEM.** **THE PREMISE THIS ROW WAS WRITTEN ON HAS SINCE BEEN REVERSED AND THE ROW SURVIVES IT INTACT, WHICH IS THE POINT OF RECORDING IT HERE** (vc, 2026-08-30, under the pen, on hv's ruling): `design.md:22` used to read *if the intentd socket exists and answers, the CLI MUST route to it (never two sync engines live at once)*, and **both halves were wrong**. Routing is now **OPT-IN** -- a local command uses `intentsvcs` in-process and `intent --daemon ...` is the exception -- because `intentd` is for CROSS-PROJECT work (hv, 2026-08-21 and again 2026-08-30). And the parenthetical was refuted on its own terms before the default ever moved: the store serialises writes and a whole sync is one transaction, so **routing was never a corruption guard** (`AC-08.11`). **WHAT THIS ROW ACTUALLY GOVERNS IS UNCHANGED BY EITHER, AND THAT IS WHY IT IS NOT WITHDRAWN: it is about the PREDICATE, not about the default.** A `--daemon` run asks exactly the same question this row answers, and asks it about the same two failure cases; only the number of callers changed. **The rule is keyed on the ENDPOINT and not on a transport**: D56 gives the daemon one output contract, GraphQL/JSON over the socket AND over HTTP (`design.md:487`), so routing folds over a LIST of candidates whose body names no transport and a third one is a new variant rather than an edit.

**CASE 1 -- THE STALE SOCKET. A dead daemon leaves its socket file behind**, so a presence test routes every verb at an endpoint nobody is listening on. Asserted by driving a stale endpoint explicitly, never by driving only the live and absent cases. **The earlier wording of this row said `socket present` and that is the defect this row now names** -- a précis that rots while the citation it summarises stays right (cc, 2026-08-29, found while building the seam).

**CASE 2 -- THE INHERITED LISTENER, AND IT IS THE SAME DEFECT ONE LAYER DOWN: A SUCCESSFUL `connect()` IS NOT AN ANSWER.** macOS has no atomic `SOCK_CLOEXEC`, so binding a listener and setting `FD_CLOEXEC` are two syscalls with a window between them; any `fork()`+`exec()` on another thread in that window inherits the LISTENING descriptor. If that child outlives the daemon, the socket file is on disk AND the listen backlog is open, so `connect()` succeeds against an owner that is dead and **nothing ever accepts** -- a case a liveness test built on connect reports as live forever. Measured by cc 2026-08-29: **1 in 300 with a sibling thread spawning children, 0 in 2000 without.**

**SO THE PROBE IS A COMPLETED REQUEST-RESPONSE ROUND-TRIP UNDER A BOUNDED DEADLINE, AND A DEADLINE THAT EXPIRES IS ROUTED AS ABSENT** -- fall through to in-process rather than hang, because a CLI that blocks on a dead daemon is the outage the routing rule exists to prevent. **THE ROUTING RULE MUST NOT DEPEND ON THE DAEMON HAVING CLOSED ITS OWN WINDOW**: whatever the daemon does about `FD_CLOEXEC` is a race it can narrow and cannot eliminate, so the client is where it is made harmless. **The daemon's own obligations were IN THIS ROW until 2026-08-30 and are now `AC-08.11`** -- cc's finding, and vc's error: this row was made unsatisfiable-by-construction by a clause about a component that does not exist, written by the same author who then ruled that a client criterion must not carry a daemon obligation. **A row cannot be closed by the party it governs when half its text is about somebody else.**

**BOTH CASES ARE CONSTRUCTED, NEVER WAITED FOR.** A 1-in-300 fault does not appear in a test run often enough to be evidence, and a suite that passes because the race did not fire is the vacuous-instrument class this thread has now recorded four times: assert case 2 by handing a child an inherited listening fd deliberately and killing the parent. **A criterion that ruled out one false positive while a second one stood unstated would be the same defect one layer down** -- which is what case 1 was to `present`, and what case 2 is to `answers`.

**AND THE PAIR IS REQUIRED, BECAUSE THE CLIENT CANNOT SEE THE MECHANISM AND THE CRITERION MUST NOT NAME ONE IT CANNOT TEST FOR.** An inherited descriptor and a listener that simply never accepts are the SAME OBSERVABLE -- connect succeeds, nothing arrives -- so the routing rule is keyed on that observable and both cases must be constructed and asserted to route IDENTICALLY (cc, 2026-08-30; their argument, adopted over the mechanism-only wording that stood here first). **The deterministic half is what makes it evidence:** a 1-in-300 race asserted by construction is still a construction of a race, where a never-accepting listener fails the same way on every run. **Do not drop the deterministic case as redundant** -- it is the one that fails when the predicate regresses, and naming only the exotic mechanism here would be the same defect as `AC-00.3` naming a transport a later ruling moved.

**CASE 3 -- THE ENDPOINT THAT ACCEPTS AND CLOSES WITHOUT WRITING, WHICH IS THE MOST LIKELY OF THE THREE AND THE ONLY ONE THAT ANSWERS PROMPTLY.** A daemon shutting down, or a server dropping input it cannot parse, accepts the connection and closes it. **A clean EOF is `Ok(0)` and not an error**, so a probe written as *did the read fail?* routes it as LIVE, and unlike cases 1 and 2 it does not fail at connect and does not hit the deadline -- **it returns quickly and successfully, which is the shape most likely to be mistaken for health.** The probe therefore requires BYTES, not the absence of an error (cc, 2026-08-30, built as `an_endpoint_that_accepts_and_closes_runs_in_process`).

**AND THE DEADLINE MOVES THE RISK RATHER THAN REMOVING IT, WHICH IS STATED HERE SO NOBODY READS THIS ROW AS SETTLED.** Bare `connect` failed toward a false POSITIVE -- route at a phantom, the request fails, the store is intact. A bounded round trip fails toward a false NEGATIVE -- the deadline expires on a daemon that is alive but slow, the CLI runs in-process, and **two sync engines are on one store, which is the single thing `design.md:22` exists to forbid.** That is the worse direction, so the client's deadline is sound only while the daemon answers on the ACCEPT path ahead of any per-request work -- **an obligation on the daemon, carried by `AC-08.11` rather than here, because this row governs a client that cannot honour a promise about the server's scheduling** (cc raised it; vc ruled it out of this row 2026-08-30). **No constant closes the class**: a daemon that meets that obligation exactly still misses any deadline under `SIGSTOP`, a suspended VM or a loaded machine. **THAT CLAUSE IS CLOSED AND THE ANSWER IS THAT NO LOCK IS OWED HERE** (cc, 2026-08-30, measured against a fixture holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, with a control before and after; ruled into `AC-08.11`): the store already refuses a second writer cleanly at rc=1, readers never block, and nothing is corrupted -- **so the residual of a wrong routing answer is duplicated work, never a safety failure.** **THE LOCK REQUIREMENT DID NOT DISAPPEAR, IT MOVED TO THE QUESTION WHOSE BLAST RADIUS EARNS IT:** `AC-08.12` requires a kernel-owned lock for single-daemon EVICTION, where the same false negative unlinks a live daemon's socket instead of costing one redundant run. **A predicate is not sound or unsound in itself; it is sound relative to what is done with the answer.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)

- AC-08.6 `projectChanged` and `fileChanged` subscriptions deliver over the socket (D20 -- and nothing more in 3.0.0) -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-08.7 Policy-stamp self-healing works: a generated local artefact whose version marker is missing or stale is regenerated on boot, and an old install heals without a migration -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-08.8 **The daemon schedules the rolling DB backup through the SAME service call the CLI uses** (D35 + D32). One implementation, so `intent backup` and the daemon's timer cannot drift into two retention policies -- the check is that the scheduled path and the manual path are the same function, not two that agree today. A backup that the daemon skipped, deferred or failed is reported where an operator will meet it, never only in a log nobody reads -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-08.9 **THE WEB FACE IS AN `intentsvcs` SURFACE WITH TWO SKINS, AND IT IS SERVED BY THE SAME SERVICE CALL THE CLI REACHES.** `intentd` serves it while running and `intent daemon run` serves the identical code in the foreground -- **which is `AC-08.2` restated for a second surface rather than a new promise**: in-process and daemon must return identical results, so a web face that answers from anywhere else has reversed D32. **RESOLUTION IS SIBLING-ONLY, NARROWED FROM THE DELIVERABLE AND RECORDED HERE BECAUSE THE DELIVERABLE CANNOT BE EDITED** (cc's finding, ruled by vc 2026-08-30). WP-08's text says *PATH-then-sibling binary resolution*, borrowed from conflabd's pattern. **The `$PATH` read broke `AC-11.3`'s guard** -- the shipped surface reads exactly one environment variable, because a brew-installed binary meets machines with no developer environment -- **and a criterion outranks a pattern description.** More importantly the search was the ONLY way to resolve a DIFFERENT `intentd`, which is the failure `exec` created: **removing it removes the failure mode instead of checking for it**, and in every real installation (homebrew, `cargo install`, a target directory) the pair are already siblings. **THE VERSION CHECK STAYS REGARDLESS**, because a sibling can still be stale from a build where only one of the pair was rebuilt -- one failure mode eliminated, the other still guarded. **This is `0154`'s second live instance: the WP body still says PATH-then-sibling and no verb can edit it**, so the correction lives in the row that gates instead of the prose that describes, and a reader meeting the deliverable alone will be told something no longer true. **A realiser holding a `rusqlite` dependency fails by construction** (`AC-02.5`, and `dep_graph_guard.rs` already enforces D06 for `intentd`). **MINTED 2026-08-29 BY vc, ON cc's FINDING, AND THE FINDING IS THE REASON THIS ROW EXISTS: the web view was scoped in conversation, carried in three sessions' instructions and on two boards, and appeared in NO criterion** -- so the gate would have computed WP-08 satisfaction without ever seeing it. **Scope that arrives through conversation and never reaches the artefact is invisible to every instrument the estate owns.** -- satisfied: no (computed)

### WP-09 -- MCP server and agent guide (status: WIP)

- AC-09.1 The typed tool tier is generated from the dispatch table with schemars-derived parameter schemas. **Each entry DECLARES two fields the generator needs and the table does not carry today -- whether it is exposed on the MCP surface, and whether it READS or MUTATES -- and a row declaring neither is REFUSED.** (ic, EXP-03, raised 2026-08-15 before WP-09 opened, by reading this AC against the artefact it names.) Measured across all 103 rows: neither fact is present or derivable. `observed.side_effects` sits on 10 of 103, so **its absence means "not recorded", not "no side effects"** -- absence-as-meaning in the one place it decides whether an agent may close a steel thread. Without the fields a generator has exactly two options and both are defects: expose everything, so `intent mcp` becomes a tool that starts an MCP server from inside one; or carry a skip list, which is **a hand-maintained command list living one command away from AC-09.4, which forbids hand-maintained command lists** -- correct when typed and silently wrong at the next command added, because the act that invalidates it is not the act that updates it. **DECLARED per row, never derived from the verb**, and the proof that derivation fails is in the surface itself: `st sync` and `sync` are different commands sharing a spelling, and **`ac gate` reads while `wp done` consults the same gate and writes**. The refusal is what makes it hold -- with it there is no default to drift; **for a HUMAN resolving an uncertain row the safe directions are opposite** (`exposed` defaults toward no, `mutates` defaults toward yes), since a command wrongly omitted is an inconvenience and one wrongly included lets an agent run `daemon`, and a mutation mislabelled as a read lets an agent close a thread believing it is querying. **EXTENDED 2026-08-17 (vc, on ic's finding, under hv's standing grant): the generated tool DESCRIPTION -- not only the parameter schema -- carries `read_or_mutate` and, for a mutation, `recoverability`, in the D45 projection order the CLI guide already uses.** As written this AC obliged the generator to describe a tool's ARGUMENTS and obliged it to say nothing about what calling it does to the estate. **The consequence is already declared in the table and is not hypothetical: `at green`, `at red`, `at na` and `ext new` are all `exposed_on_mcp: true` AND `recoverability: one-way`** -- one-way because issue 0033 destroys the row's authored note, 1,447 characters measured on AT-03.12 and 14,253 at risk across 34 rows. **So the identical destructive operation would warn a CLI agent at the point of call and say nothing at all to an MCP agent**, which is the surface with less context. **D45 does not license the omission and reading it that way inverts it**: hv ruled the CLI precise and MCP imprecise, and `imprecise` is a claim about capability granularity, not about disclosure -- an agent with less context needs the safety fact more, not less. **This invents no policy and adds no field**: `recoverability`, its vocabulary, its refusal and its renderer all exist and are proven on the CLI surface, and this is one more projection of the same declaration, which is the whole argument for why `recoverability` is intrinsic to a row rather than an exposure flag. **Ruled BEFORE the tier is built deliberately** -- today it costs a sentence in an AC, and after WP-09 it is a change to what agents have already been told. **Sharpened by `design.md:310-320`, which is the same cut**: `schemars` lifts `///` into the JSON Schemas and a `///` on a derived type is _"an unreviewed publication channel -- the author is writing a comment and the consumer is reading a contract"_. **The generated description will carry prose either way; the only question this settles is whether that prose is the safety fact or whatever doc comment happens to sit on the type.** -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-09.2 The `intent_graphql` escape hatch reaches the full API surface -- satisfied: no (computed)

### WP-10 -- Migration and fleet ingest harness (status: WIP)

- AC-10.1 A pre-2.19.0 project is refused with the two-hop instruction named. **CLOSED SAME DAY: found unsatisfied 2026-08-17 (vc), fixed by cc at `f2b7b97a`, re-measured satisfied by vc against the same estate -- `intent upgrade` on pinned Utilz now exits 1 naming `2.18.0`, the `v2.19.0` floor and `install intent@2`, writes nothing (101/101 verified, stamp untouched), while the 2.19.0 canary still converts.** The fix is one arm before `legacy::scan` -- refuse iff `Pending { below_floor: true }`, remedy delegated to `Pending::remedy()` rather than copied -- and a distinct `BelowMigrationFloor` variant rather than reusing `Unmigrated`, because both carry the same data and **the operator's situation differs**: _this project has not been migrated_ is news to someone who asked a question and a restatement of their own intent to someone who just ran the migrator. The record of the original finding follows, because how it was found is the reusable part. **MEASURED UNSATISFIED 2026-08-17 (vc), against the only real pre-2.19.0 estate in the fleet corpus: Utilz declares `2.18.0` and `Facade::upgrade` MIGRATED it** -- 61 files, 9 threads, 19 carried findings, no refusal, and `config.json` left reading `3.0.0-dev`. **The mechanism is a floor enforced on the door that READS and not on the door that WRITES**: `Facade::open` calls `Self::readable()`, which returns `FacadeError::Unmigrated(pending)` whose `remedy()` is delegated precisely so the sub-floor case gets the two-hop (`facade.rs:406` says so); `Facade::upgrade` never calls `readable()` and goes straight to `legacy::scan`. **Why it is worse than a missing guard: the two-hop exists because 2.19.0 is where the AT row grammar landed**, so a 2.18.0 estate migrated straight to v3 skips that migration and its AT rows arrive in a grammar v3 was never told about, silently, because nothing on this path is looking. AT-10.1 (`migrate_floor.rs`) is `to-write`, so this is an unbuilt criterion with a measurement attached rather than a regression. **It could only have been found by a real sub-floor estate** -- the canary and Lamplight and Baize all declare 2.19.0, and a constructed fixture would have been built by someone who already knew the floor was the question -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-10.10 **A migrated project's whiteboard guards still REFUSE what they refused before the swap** -- asserted by driving the shipped hook and watching it BLOCK, never by checking that the guard files are present. (vc 2026-08-16, issue 0042, measured.) **This is the gap in my own AC-10.9 and the distinction is the same one that made 10.9 a new criterion rather than a widening of 10.4.** AC-10.9 asks whether a migrated project can commit; **a commit that succeeds with both guards silently not running satisfies it completely.** Necessary, not sufficient. The measured cause: `lib/templates/hooks/pre-commit.sh:104` resolves the guard directory with `intent info 2>/dev/null | sed -n 's/^ *INTENT_HOME: *//p'`, **v3 did not implement `info`, the value came back EMPTY, and both guards took the hook's fail-open branch.** **CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (cc, measured; vc verified): THIS NO LONGER REPRODUCES.** `intent info` now resolves, and the hook's own extraction run against the v3 binary returns the real path rather than nothing. **The resolver's live failure mode is a different one and it is about INSTALL LOCATION, not about `info` being unimplemented**: the value is `<not set>` when the binary sits outside its install, which cc hit in a worktree. **The criterion itself is UNAFFECTED and is the reason it was written as a behaviour rather than as a bug**: it demands the guards be shown to REFUSE by driving the shipped hook, which no longer has a known way to fail and therefore has no evidence either. **Two further corrections to this row's own text, and the second is my own rule biting me**: the cited `pre-commit.sh:104` has drifted -- line 104 is now a comment -- so the citation is to a line number rather than to a thing; and a criterion whose measured cause is fixed while the criterion stands is exactly the state that needs saying out loud, because the alternative is a row that reads as an open defect when what is open is the absence of proof. **It is independent of the exit code, which is why `d2b8e76d` could not have touched it -- the hook reads stdout and `2>/dev/null` discards the error either way.** **And it is the worse shape of the pair: 0038 failed CLOSED, so it was loud, blocking, and fixed inside a day; this fails OPEN, so the commit succeeds and the only symptom is a line on stderr during a green commit.** The controls at stake are not decorative -- the clock guard refused a commit carrying a fabricated timestamp after six prose resolutions of the same rule had failed to stop the previous six, and the header guard is newer still and has the least field time of any control in the estate. **Intent's own dogfood migration hits this** (this repo has `intent/whiteboard/`) **and so does Lamplight, second in the corpus.** The fix is forced rather than chosen: 0016 forbids rewiring the hook and a silent exit 0 is a silent failure, so implementing `info` is what remains -- **but the durable half is that a hook must not resolve a path by parsing a display command's output**, since nothing in `info` declares itself machine-read and nothing in the hook declares it needs `info` -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-10.11 **An INTERRUPTED migration and an uninterrupted one reach the same end state, and the interruption is DEMONSTRATED rather than simulated.** (vc 2026-08-18, minted on the moratorium lift; the instruments predated the criterion.) A partial run killed mid-flight and then re-run must produce the estate an uninterrupted run produces -- **and the evidence must show the kill was real**, since a hand-rolled partial write proves only that the harness can truncate a file. The discriminating artefact is the temp-and-rename residue a real process death leaves and a simulated one never does. **Both readings must be taken against a BINARY PINNED BY CONTENT HASH, never by its self-reported provenance marker** (ic 2026-08-18, on a live casualty): a marker can stay byte-identical across two behaviourally different builds, so a paired reading keyed on the marker certifies pairs that are not pairs. **AND THIS IS NOT A `dirty-` WORKAROUND (dc 2026-08-18): NO PROVENANCE MARKER OF ANY DESIGN CAN DISTINGUISH TWO BUILDS OF ONE SOURCE**, so a future clean marker fails identically. The pin is by content hash because the property required is byte identity of the ARTEFACT, which no self-report can carry. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-10.12 **A VERB THAT REPORTS AN ESTATE UNCHANGED MUST NOT HAVE CHANGED IT.** (vc 2026-08-19, re-cutting this row on cc's architectural finding; the row's first wording is retained below because it was minted and then disproved.) **MEASURED:** `intent upgrade` over an already-migrated project rewrote **all 40 issue bodies** while printing *already migrated ... their content is unchanged*. **The falsifiable property is the REPORT, not the rewrite** -- re-running a migrator over an estate may legitimately overwrite what it produces; **saying nothing changed while changing forty files is the defect, and it is red today.** **THIS ROW WAS FIRST MINTED AS _the migrator's output must reproduce the canon it produced_ AND THAT WORDING IS WITHDRAWN**, because cc established that the migrator never produced those bytes and that satisfying it would require reversing a documented deliberate decision. **THE EVIDENCE, VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY: `9a487cc9` (2026-08-18 15:12:53Z) wrote canon's issue bodies -- 40 files changed, ALL under `intent/issues/`, ZERO code, and 0001's body already trimmed -- six hours and eighteen minutes AFTER `36bc02c5` (08:54:34Z) removed the `Issue::body` trim.** So the divergence is migrator-versus-BACKFILL, and it is determinable from git without the old binary. **AND THE THREAD/ISSUE ASYMMETRY IS ARCHITECTURAL RATHER THAN A MISSING TRIM** (cc, measured, verified): `legacy.rs:382` DECOMPOSES a thread body into `(heading, text)` pairs and REASSEMBLES it as `format!("## {k}\n\n{v}")`, **so the raw slice never survives and leading or trailing whitespace structurally cannot appear**; `legacy.rs:685` is `body: body.to_string()`, **the raw slice verbatim, carrying bytes ON PURPOSE**. Populations close in both directions: **54 of 54 thread bodies begin `## `; 40 of 40 issue bodies begin `# ` and ZERO begin `## `.** That is the whole explanation for cc's re-run changing zero thread canon and all forty issues -- **not one path trimming and the other forgetting, but two mechanisms, one of which CANNOT EXPRESS the defect.** **AND THE CARRY IS ARGUED AT THE SITE** (`legacy.rs:680`): *a trim guarantees a one-byte loss on every round trip unless a second place remembers to put the byte back. A normalisation that needs a future component to compensate is a scheduled defect; carrying the bytes needs nobody to remember anything.* **A CRITERION MUST NOT REQUIRE A DEFECT THE CODE HAS ALREADY REASONED ITS WAY OUT OF**, which is what the first wording did. **WHO OWNS ISSUE-BODY NORMALISATION IS A SEPARATE, OPEN, DESIGN QUESTION ROUTED TO hv AND DELIBERATELY NOT SETTLED HERE** -- canon over-normalised and should be re-derived, or the migrator should trim and the renderer compensate. **This row is independent of that answer**, which is why it could be re-cut without waiting for it. -- satisfied: no (computed)

- AC-10.14 **THE MIGRATOR'S POPULATION IS DERIVED FROM CANON, NOT FROM THE FILESYSTEM.** Every artefact with committed canon is in the model the migration builds, whether or not it has a directory on disk -- so the project-level views it regenerates (`steel_threads.md`, `todo.md`) name the whole estate rather than the realised part of it. **MEASURED (cc, driven, 2026-08-20): a fresh clone of `183f7342` at binary `ada1ce8c0241e3a9`.** `intent sync --to-store` first, reporting `57 thread(s)` and the store and the extract in agreement; then `intent upgrade`, rc=0, printing `migrated: 3 thread(s), 0 issue(s), 38 file(s) written` and `ok: this project is now Intent v3.0.0-dev`. **`steel_threads.md` went from 57 rows to 3. 239 lines were deleted across two committed views and the exit code was zero.** Canon is untouched -- all 57 files remain -- so nothing is lost; **the committed index of the estate now names three threads out of fifty-seven and nothing said so.**

**IT IS NOT A MISSING-STORE PROBLEM, AND THAT WAS CHECKED SPECIFICALLY** rather than assumed: the run above loads the store from the extract FIRST and `upgrade` still truncates to 3.

**MECHANISM, READ AT SOURCE (vc, re-read at `183f7342` rather than carried):** `legacy::scan` at `legacy.rs:222` iterates `thread_dirs(project)` -- `legacy.rs:711`, a `read_dir` over `intent/st/` and its three v2 status buckets. `.intentfiles` declares three, so three directories exist; **the other 54 are dehydrated, have no directory, and never enter the loop.** `views::render_all` then regenerates the project-level views from a 3-thread model. **The migrator is disk-driven in an estate where disk is a sparse projection** -- it predates ST0057's model entirely, and nothing about it is wrong for the world it was written for.

**THE SITE ALREADY CARRIES THE ARGUMENT AGAINST ITSELF, WHICH IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING.** The comment at `legacy.rs:222` is ic's correction of an earlier `continue`, and it says in these words: *`views::render_all` renders `steel_threads.md` and `todo.md` FROM THE WHOLE THREAD LIST, so a thread missing from `threads` is a thread missing from the index ... Trading unbounded accretion for a silently truncated index is not a fix.* **That fix reads canon for the ids the disk walk already yielded -- it was applied INSIDE the enumeration rather than TO it** -- so it reaches the re-run population and cannot reach the dehydrated one. **A correction lodged one level below the defect looks, at the site, exactly like a correction that covers it.**

**AND THE ONE ANSWER ALREADY EXISTS.** `Project::thread_ids()` at `project.rs:936` returns *every thread id with committed canon, sorted*, treating an absent `st/` as an empty project rather than an error. **Two answers to `what threads exist` live in one crate and the migrator calls the v2 one** -- so this is a Highlander defect with the survivor already written, and the fix is the union rather than a new enumeration.

**WHY THIS IS A NEW ROW AND NOT AC-10.12 READ BROADLY (vc ruling, on cc's refusal to stretch a criterion to fit a finding).** AC-10.12's property is the REPORT: *a verb that reports an estate unchanged must not have changed it*. **`upgrade` makes no such claim here** -- it says `38 file(s) written`, and its `their content is unchanged` is scoped to the three already-migrated threads, of which it is true. **Satisfy AC-10.12 completely and the 239 lines are still deleted, now correctly announced.** That is the same discriminator that made AC-10.9 a new row rather than a widening of AC-10.4: **a criterion about what a verb SAYS cannot see a defect in what it ENUMERATES**, and widening it would add reach in the axis that already works.

**REACHABLE BY A USER ON FIRST CONTACT.** Clone, `intent upgrade` -- which `intent/restart.md`'s opening neighbourhood makes a plausible first act on a new machine. Nothing warns, and the destructive result is a clean `ok:`.

**THE SWEEP, WITH ITS LIMIT STATED (vc, `183f7342`).** cc marked as INFERRED that they had not checked whether `organize` or `sync --to-disk` share the enumeration. A `read_dir` sweep of `crates/*/src/*.rs` finds two enumerations that DERIVE a population -- `legacy.rs:711` (disk) and `project.rs:937` (canon) -- while `realise.rs:263`, `sync.rs:341` and `write_set.rs:382` walk a tree to COMPARE against a model they were handed. **So the class has one member in `src` today. That is a sweep result and not a proof:** a sweep keyed on `read_dir` cannot see an enumeration built any other way, which is this estate's own recorded defect -- a zero from a name search is a fact about the search.

**AND THE VIEWS ARE THE SMALLER HALF. THE STORE IS REBUILT FROM THE SAME PARTIAL MODEL** (cc, driven, extending their own report twenty minutes later; vc verified the site at `105faa01`). `facade.rs:1127` calls `store.rebuild(&threads, &issues)` where `threads` is `migrate::plan(project, ctx, scan)`'s output -- the 3-thread scan. Driven on the clone that had just loaded 57: `sync --to-store` reports 57, `upgrade` returns rc=0 and `ok:`, and `count(*)` on the database answers **3**, with all 57 canon files still present. **Under D01-reversed the database is the durable SSOT, so this discards 95% of it without a word.** Recoverable -- `sync --to-store` restores it, driven -- but recoverability is not the property in question.

**THE INSTRUMENT FINDING, WHICH IS LARGER THAN THIS ROW AND IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE THIS IS WHERE IT WAS MEASURED (cc's formulation, taken as theirs).** cc's first report said *canon is untouched, so nothing is lost* -- true, and incomplete, because the blast radius was measured with `git status` and a hash diff over TRACKED files. **The store is gitignored under D21, so the destruction of the thing the architecture calls authoritative was invisible to the instrument used.** Two files changed, said git; 54 SSOT rows went, said nobody. **A gitignored SSOT is invisible to `git status`, so a diff-based blast radius systematically under-reports damage to exactly the artefact the model says matters most** -- and it reads as a complete answer every time. Every node here reaches for `git status` to answer *what did that change*. Under D01-as-written that was adequate; **under D01-reversed it is structurally blind in the one direction that counts.**

**THE ENUMERATION IS NOT SHARED, AND THAT IS DRIVEN RATHER THAN INFERRED (vc, `105faa01`, answering the question cc marked INFERRED twice).** `legacy::scan` has exactly TWO callers, both in `facade.rs`: `:1105` (`upgrade`) and `:2489` (`ingest_from_md`, the WP-10 markdown-parser seam). **`organize` does not share it** -- `organize.rs:392` calls `crate::sync::scan`, a different function that walks the tree to COMPARE against a model it was handed. **`sync --to-store` does not share it either** -- `ingest.rs:180` iterates `project.thread_ids()`, which is canon, which is why that direction answered 57 in cc's own run. **The defect is local to migration and has one live consumer.**

**SHAPE RULED: FIX THE REBUILD, NOT THE SCAN (vc 2026-08-20, advisory on contract; cc builds, hv owns scope if it grows).** cc offered two: (1) `legacy::scan` enumerates canon rather than `thread_dirs`; (2) `upgrade` rebuilds the store and the views from FULL canon, leaving the scan alone. **Shape 2, and the reason is Highlander read forwards rather than backwards.** Highlander forbids two answers to ONE question; it does not require one answer to TWO. `legacy::scan` answers *what must be converted from v2*; `Project::thread_ids()` answers *what threads exist*. **Those are different questions and both answers are correct today.** The defect is that `upgrade` asked the first when it needed the second. **Shape 1 collapses them, and the second caller is the proof it must not:** `ingest_from_md` exists to read a v2 estate AS MARKDOWN, and a canon-only thread has no markdown to read -- so shape 1 makes a correct function wrong at its other call site, and every residue class in `Scan` (all of which describe something a v2 AUTHOR left behind) acquires a population it must specially ignore. **Neither shape is constrained by this criterion -- both satisfy it -- so this is advice on cost and blast radius, not a contract requirement.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
