semantic break, so widening its path list to include `.git/hooks` would add coverage in the axis that already works and none in the axis that failed. **`.git/hooks` is covered by no AC in this thread and never was, which is why nothing caught this.** The measured failure: a migrated project with `languages: ["shell"]` and one staged file gets `error: \`critic\` is a known command that is not implemented yet`followed by`commit blocked by findings at severity >= warning`, **and the remedy cannot be followed because there is no finding.** The only escape is `--no-verify`. **The exit-code contract is the mechanism and v2 honours it: the gate reserves 1 for "your code has findings" and 2+ for "the critic tooling itself is unavailable", and `intent critic nosuchlang`exits 2 under v2.** v3 collapsed unavailable into the code meaning your-code-is-bad, so the gate's fail-open branch exists, is correct, and is never reached. **Wider than`critic`**: `agents`and`llm`(not implemented),`organize`and`treeindex`(unrecognised subcommand) and a missing`<LANG>`(usage error) all exit 1 -- three different kinds of event sharing one code, of which only the last is arguably the caller's fault. **Severity is set by 0036 rather than by this bug alone:`brew install`SHADOWS a v2 install, so first contact is in a project the user was not thinking about, and the gate refuses everything in it.** And it trains the bypass -- the first`--no-verify`is correct and unavoidable, and the habit outlives the cause. **Ordering consequence worth stating because it is a scheduling decision and not a coding one: WP-10 landing before WP-07 puts every migrated project in this state.** **WIDENED 2026-08-17 (vc, on issue 0045) from "a migrated project" to "migrated or not", and the widening is the point rather than a tidy-up.** The original wording was written against dc's 0038, whose fixture is migrated because that is what 0038 was about -- so the criterion, the test cc built from it, and every instrument pointed at it all inherited that scope, and **the UNMIGRATED project, which is the state every project in the estate is in until WP-10 runs on it, was covered by nothing.** Measured 2026-08-17:`Facade::open`gates on migration before anything else, the refusal maps to`EXIT_ERROR`, and the pre-commit gate reads 1 as *blocked by findings* -- so a commit in an unmigrated project is refused with `review the findings above, fix them, and re-commit`over a project that has none. **It does not reproduce today, and only because`critic`is unbuilt and exits 2 into the fail-open branch**, which is a reprieve nobody chose and that ends the moment WP-07 lands. **Two arms, both driven end to end through the shipped`pre-commit.sh` against a command that reaches the facade: migrated commits, and unmigrated commits.** Widened rather than split into a new AC because -- unlike the AC-10.4 case argued above, where the axis genuinely differed -- this is the same axis, the same instrument and the same consumer, with only the project state changed; a second criterion here would be two rows asking one question. -- satisfied: yes (computed)

### WP-11 -- Distribution: cargo-dist, Homebrew, signing (status: WIP)

- AC-11.1 (non-test) A tagged version yields installable artefacts and a working tap formula for both binaries, such that `brew install` on a machine that has never seen this repo produces a runnable `intent` and `intentd`. **REWRITTEN 2026-08-15 (vc) on dc's measurement, and the rewrite is the finding: this row named cargo-dist as its mechanism and demanded "release CI run" as its evidence, and both were wrong in a way that made the row unsatisfiable by the approach the estate has already validated.** cargo-dist 0.32.0 **does not notarise at all** (`strings`: `notarytool`, `notariz`, `stapler`, `altool`, `xcrun` -- zero hits each); it signs only, via a base64 p12 imported from a CI secret into a temp keychain. D38 requires both binaries signed **and** notarised unconditionally, so the named mechanism covers at most half of the criterion beside it, and covers that half by exporting the signing identity out of the login Keychain into a repository secret -- strictly worse than `int macos sign|notarize|verify`, which is proven end to end and keeps the identity in the Keychain. **The general rule this row now carries: AN AC NAMES THE OUTCOME, AND THE MECHANISM BELONGS IN THE WORK PACKAGE.** A criterion that names a tool can be invalidated by a measurement of that tool while the thing the project actually wanted is still perfectly achievable -- which is exactly what happened here, and it cost a rewrite rather than a rethink only because dc measured before wiring. **SCOPED BY hv RULING 2026-08-15 (D39, D40): macOS arm64 ONLY, tap `matthewsinclair/homebrew-intent`, artefacts on the source repo's own releases, cargo-dist DEFERRED.** So the mechanism is the local `int macos sign|notarize|stage|formula` path and the row no longer has a tool question in it. **The evidence line is now STRONGER than the one it replaced, not weaker**: a CI run proves a pipeline executed, whereas a `brew install` actually run once proves the artefact, the checksum, the formula and the tap all agree -- which is the only claim this row was ever making. **BLOCKER CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (vc, on dc's report, both claims re-measured rather than relayed): ONE of the two named blockers was cleared two days ago and this row went on asserting it.** `matthewsinclair/homebrew-intent` **exists** -- `gh repo view --json createdAt,isEmpty` returns `2026-08-15T15:19:58Z`, `isEmpty: false`, created the afternoon after this row was written. **dc's distinction is kept because the row needs it: `isEmpty: false` means "has a commit", NOT "has a formula"** -- the tap holds one README explaining why it is deliberately empty, which is the state this row's own argument below calls for. So the tap is not a blocker and never was after 2026-08-15; the absent FORMULA is a deliberate choice. **What IS still live is the version: `Cargo.toml:6` declares `3.0.0-dev` and the release binary reports it, and the publish step refuses to emit a formula for a dev build.** That is held deliberately until WP-12, because wiring `Cargo.toml` into `int build release`'s sidecar sync now would make a **v2** release stamp its version into it. **AND THE ROW REFUTED ITSELF BEFORE ANYONE NOTICED, WHICH IS THE FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX**: the stale clause said the tap "does not exist yet" while a later sentence in the SAME row says "the tap is deliberately LIVE AND EMPTY" and reasons from it. Two clauses of one criterion contradicting each other, added at different times, with nothing comparing them -- **so the row was not merely stale, it was internally inconsistent, and a reader could quote either half.** **The general rule, and it is the mirror of AC-00.9's**: a criterion that carries its own EVIDENCE decays exactly like a measurement that names a revision, and this one decayed in our favour -- someone could have spent an afternoon creating a repository that already existed. A stale blocker reads as current work whichever direction it decayed. **REQUIRED REFUSAL, from dc 2026-08-15: the PUBLISH step refuses to emit a formula for a dev build.** dc's framing is the rule and it generalises past this row -- **the control goes where the HARM is, which is publication, not printing.** A dev-version formula printed to a terminal harms nobody; the same bytes in a tap install a build nobody meant to ship. **The tap is deliberately LIVE AND EMPTY on the same reasoning, and it is the better half of the argument**: dc had a generated, verified formula in hand and did not push it, because it would have pointed at a release tag that does not exist -- so `brew tap` SUCCEEDS and `brew install` fails with a download error, and **a user reads "the tap is broken" when the truth is "the release is not out yet". A wrong artefact is not a neutral placeholder; it makes a confident false statement, where an empty tap says the true thing** -- satisfied: no
- AC-11.2 (non-test) The signing/notarisation decision is recorded and implemented -- evidence: D38 (design.md) + int macos stage|sign|notarize|verify|checksum implemented and canaried both ways, run as one pass by int macos prepare; verify_notarised remains ONE implementation serving verify and checksum. Apple Accepted three times -- satisfied: yes
- AC-11.3 The binary is fully functional with no INTENT_HOME in the environment **and the variable is not read at all, which is stronger than an override and is deliberate** **PARENTHETICAL CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (vc), on dc's measurement, on a row that is already GREEN -- which is where a stale claim survives, because nothing re-examines a closed criterion (issue 0032, this contract's own weighing).** The row read _"demoted to dev override"_, and **there is no override: `$INTENT_HOME` is never read from the environment.** Measured by dc on one binary three ways -- set in the environment: `<not set>`; absent: `<not set>`; from inside an install: the real path -- and confirmed by vc at source, where **zero `var("INTENT_HOME")` or `var_os("INTENT_HOME")` calls exist in any crate.** `intent info` self-locates by walking up from its resolved executable path. **THE MAIN CLAUSE IS TRUE AND THE GREEN STANDS**; AT-11.3 proves the binary is fully functional without the variable. It is the parenthetical that described a mechanism which does not exist -- **and the danger of leaving it is that a reader implementing to the contract would build the override back, which is precisely what happened once already: the first version of `install.rs` built one, and ic's `no_intent_home.rs` refused it.** **THE REASON IT MUST NEVER EXIST, hoisted here out of `install.rs:20` where it has been living as a code comment (cc):** the assets are VERSIONED, and `lib/templates/.claude/scripts/` in a v2 install and a v3 install are **different files with the same names**. A machine mid-rollout has both trees and a `$INTENT_HOME` left over from v2, so an override would make the v3 binary exec **v2's hook scripts**, silently, from a variable the operator set years earlier for a different tool. **The environment cannot know which version is running; the executable's own location always does.** **THAT IS WHY THIS IS A CONTRACT EDIT AND NOT A COMMENT: a ratified reason living in a transcription is found only by whoever next touches that transcription (cc's own rule, third instance today), and the contract is what a builder reads first.** **AND THE FIXTURE THAT FOUND IT PASSED FOR THE WRONG REASON FIRST:** dc's first attempt set the variable and PASSED, because `cargo test` puts the binary under `target/` inside the repository, so walking up found a tree with `lib/templates/` in it. **The variable was doing nothing and the LAYOUT was doing all of it.** It surfaced only under a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, which moved the build out of the tree -- the subject moving out from under the instrument, caught by moving it deliberately. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-11.4 (non-test) **The published checksum matches the bytes the user downloads.** Signing and checksumming are ORDERED and the order is silent when wrong: `codesign --force` **rewrites the binary in place**, whereas `notarytool submit` uploads a zip of a copy and we cannot staple a bare Mach-O, so notarisation leaves the file byte-identical. Therefore **sign BEFORE you checksum; notarise whenever.** Get it backwards and the formula ships a sha256 computed over pre-signature bytes -- which never fails for us and fails for **every single `brew install`**, at the point in the pipeline where we have the least visibility. Raised by dc 2026-08-15 from the Conflab port, where two sha256 lines are hand-maintained in the formula and a `release sync` command exists partly to heal this class of drift. **Hand-maintained checksums beside a generated artefact is the same shape as the hand-kept `SCHEMA_VERSION` beside the DDL in AC-02.7**, and it wants the same answer: the release path computes the checksum from the artefact it just signed, or a check refuses the publish. **MECHANISM BUILT 2026-08-15 (dc) as `int macos stage`, ahead of this row and independently of it**: it names artefacts per target triple from `rustc -vV` rather than `uname` (the artefact name is a claim about what the compiler targeted, and only the compiler can answer that), proves each **staged copy**, refuses outright unless every artefact is both signed and notarised, and only then checksums. **RESTRUCTURED at `3ab8844e` and this row is corrected to match (dc reported the drift; vc ruled): `stage` now COPIES ONLY and `checksum` proves-then-hashes, with `prepare` running stage/sign/notarize/checksum as one pass.** The staged copies are what get SIGNED, in a directory only `int macos` writes, so the de-notarisation race shrinks from the whole multi-minute sequence spanning a round trip to Apple down to one `ditto`. **And the refusal deliberately no longer deletes the staging directory** -- it removes only `SHA256SUMS.txt` and leaves both binaries in place. That was harmless when staging copied the build output; it is actively wrong now the staged files carry a signature and a notarisation, because discarding them spends another Apple round trip rebuilding something already correct. **The stale CLAIM cannot survive while the WORK does, which is the right split**: `formula` treats that file's existence as its proof, so removing it is sufficient to make the refusal binding. Canaried in both directions, which is the part that makes it evidence: a control that has only ever refused proves as little as one that has only ever passed. **The row stays unsatisfied because nothing is published yet** -- the mechanism existing is not the criterion; the published hash matching the published bytes is. **Two things to carry into the release path when the matrix is ruled:** the staged copies should be SIGNED rather than copied-after-signing, so the signed artefact lives where only the release pipeline writes (dc recorded the inversion and deliberately did not refactor proven code ahead of the ruling); and `verify_notarised` must stay ONE implementation serving both `verify` and `stage`, because a release-time check that is a second copy of the development-time one is a check that drifts. **STRENGTHENED 2026-08-17 (dc reported; vc ruled, and dc was right that the row always meant this): CONSISTENCY IS NOT CORRESPONDENCE.** Every check in this pipeline -- the round trip, the sums file, the classification refusal, the tag-exists check -- asks whether the bytes agree with EACH OTHER, and **a set of bytes built from a peer's uncommitted work is perfectly self-consistent.** Nothing asked whether these are the bytes the TAG names, so `publish` could establish that a tag exists and then publish artefacts with no evidence they came from it. **Two reachable routes, neither hypothetical**: `stage` COPIES out of `native/rust/target/release` rather than building, and that directory is shared mutable state in a clone four nodes write to (dc has twice measured a rebuild adopting a peer's in-flight edit); and the support tarball is archived with `tar -C "$ROOT"` from the WORKING TREE, so uncommitted shell ships verbatim -- and `lib/templates/hooks/pre-commit.sh` was dirty for most of 2026-08-17. **Fixed by recording the commit at `stage` and refusing at `publish`, compared against the TAG's commit rather than HEAD** (publishing may legitimately happen after the branch moves on; a HEAD comparison looks correct and would refuse every late publish, and there is a mutant for exactly that). **So the evidence line grows a second half rather than being replaced**: the checksum half proves the artefact is intact, and the commit half proves it is the RIGHT artefact, and neither implies the other -- satisfied: no
- AC-11.5 **An artefact asserts its own provenance, and the assertion is READ FROM THE ARTEFACT, never from a record written beside it.** (vc 2026-08-17, on dc's finding; dc asked whether to strengthen AC-11.4 and the ruling is that this is a different criterion.) **A manifest states what was MEANT to be built; only the artefact can answer what was.** Three states are currently indistinguishable, all measured live and unplanted rather than constructed. **(a) The signed binary cannot name its commit**: `int macos stage` records `commit: <HEAD>` and `traceable: yes` -- where traceable means the working tree is clean -- and never asks when the binaries were BUILT, because `stage` copies them rather than building them. Measured with HEAD at `2026-08-17T15:11:14Z`: `intent` built three hours earlier, `intentd` **forty-two hours** earlier, forty-two hours apart from each other. Commit the tree and the whole chain goes green over bytes from two different earlier trees. **(b) The vendored tree cannot name its source**: `int vendor` compares the vendored files against a manifest that ships beside them and is rewritten by the same act that changes them, so a re-vendor and a local patch produce the identical `27 of 27`; and `devbin_version: 0.1.0` cannot separate them either, because **the field that exists to carry provenance is constant across exactly the thing it must distinguish.** **(c) An UNCOMMITTED REGENERATION is invisible to both** (dc, correcting their own report and vc's explanation of it): a tree that agrees with itself because someone re-derived the record locally is indistinguishable from a tree that agrees because it is correct. Measured at HEAD `702b58f8`: `bin/devbin` is `41017e54` while the manifest committed beside it records `8016f112`, so **a fresh clone reports DIVERGED right now** -- and every local check ran green throughout, over a regenerated manifest that was never committed. Verified by vc that `upstream/main` at `5765c5da` is self-consistent, so nothing broken is published; **that containment is an artefact of hv's CI-budget freeze and is recorded as luck rather than as a control**, because filing it as a save would teach the opposite of what happened. **Requires two things, and the second is what closes (c)**: the artefact carries its own source commit, embedded at build time and read back FROM the binary -- dc's own `staged_version` already argues this one function over, read the version from the BINARY and never from a manifest; and every provenance check compares against what the COMMIT holds rather than the worktree, for the same reason the whiteboard clock guard's check C blocks only on what the commit adds. **This is deliberately NOT folded into AC-11.4.** That row's criterion is _the published hash matching the published bytes_, which stale binaries satisfy perfectly -- the hash is honest and the commit attribution is not. Folding a provenance control under a checksum row would make a green start meaning something the row's text does not say, **which is the third axis (a remedy whose subject is not the report's) arriving inside a remedy.** dc declined to do it unprompted and asked first **MARKER CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (vc): this row carried the doc-check marker, and that made it UNCLOSABLE.** `ac_is_satisfied` returns on the doc-check branch before it ever looks at a covering AT, so **AT-11.5 could go green and this AC would not move**; and the only route the marker leaves is hand-writing a `yes` into this line's own inline satisfaction field, **which is a record written beside the artefact -- the exact form this criterion forbids.** Two routes on paper, zero in the gate, and the one the doc points at is the one the row prohibits. It was marked non-test by copying AC-11.1 to AC-11.4, which genuinely are doc checks; this row then acquired a real instrument and the marker did not follow. **`at lint` reports all rows conform over it.** L5 catches the mirror pairing -- a doc-check AT covering a test-backed AC -- and names the symptom exactly, _"an unclosable contract whose only symptom is a gate that will not move"_; **the reverse pairing produces the identical harm and is not checked.** Reported to hv rather than fixed here: `bin/**` is not vc's to edit. **AND THIS NOTE SATISFIED ITS OWN ROW ON THE FIRST WRITE (vc, caught in the same minute by the marker scan, gate 46 -> 47).** The sentence warning that a hand-written flag would close the row SPELLED the flag, and the parser cannot tell a row's data from a row's prose about its data -- **the document is its own format, so writing about a marker writes the marker.** This week's class again, at the smallest scale it has appeared: the measurement's subject and only hit. **The gate cannot catch it: a row that hand-satisfies itself is a row that is satisfied**, and `at lint` reads all rows as conforming either way. **AND THIS ROW HAS NOW DONE IT TWICE, IN doc-check marker three times, and `ac_is_nontest` matches it ANYWHERE IN THE LINE (`bin/intent_acceptance:90`), so the repair undid itself and the row was unclosable again -- gate 46 while my own parser said 47.** **The disagreement is the only reason it surfaced, and it surfaced by luck: my scan reads the marker POSITIONALLY (immediately after the id) and the gate reads it anywhere, so for every row where the token appears only in its proper place the two agree exactly -- which is what they had been doing all session.** **Two readers of one format, agreeing until the first row that separates them.** The scan is being corrected to the gate's reading, because a checker that disagrees with the authority about what the data MEANS will keep certifying the authority's answer as its own. **The general form, and it is the day's class at the smallest possible scale: in a document that is its own data format, writing ABOUT a marker writes the marker -- so a note explaining a repair is itself a repair, in whichever direction the token points.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-11.6 **A REBUILD INTO THE SHARED ARTEFACT PATH IS REFUSED WHEN THE TREE IS DIRTY WITH PATHS THE BUILDER DOES NOT OWN.** (cc + ic 2026-08-18, both declining to propose the row; vc mints it.) **`native/rust/target/release/` is shared by four nodes and there is NO per-node isolation underneath it**, so the only control that currently exists over what enters it is the convention _do not rebuild while I am measuring_. **A control that looks like a courtesy is the one that gets dropped the first time somebody is in a hurry** -- which is approximately what happened on 2026-08-18, with nobody being careless: a build at 19:33Z from a live tree carrying uncommitted WP-01 source produced a binary that resolved canon at a path the reverted source no longer used, and it emptied the store for every node until rebuilt. **TWO MITIGATIONS WERE OFFERED AND THE SECOND IS RULED: building from a clean extract PREVENTS the bad artefact; refusing a dirty cross-owner rebuild prevents it AND TELLS SOMEONE WHY, before the artefact exists, rather than producing a working-looking binary four nodes then invoke.** Failing loudly at the right moment beats failing silently at the cheap one.

**AMENDED 2026-08-25 BY vc AS CONTRACT STEWARD, ON dc's ROUTING. THE GREEN ARM AS I FIRST MINTED IT ASKED FOR A DISCRIMINATOR THAT CANNOT EXIST.** I wrote it as _dirty with paths the builder does not own_, so the green arm allowed a builder dirty in paths they DO own -- and dc could not make the red-first arm red, recording that as a fact without naming the cause. **The cause is that the criterion's green arm reintroduces the defect the criterion exists to prevent**, and the discriminator is unavailable for two INDEPENDENT reasons, either of which is sufficient. **(1) Ownership of a path is undeclarable by design.** Whiteboard Protocol 3.0 invariant 3, verbatim at `in-whiteboard/SKILL.md:300`: _Claims by ST ID (in the `wip.md` header block), never glob paths._ So there is no oracle and building one would violate the protocol. **(2) Even with an oracle it would not discriminate** (cc, 2026-08-20): a shared-tree build carries the UNION of every node's dirty bytes whoever invokes it, so authorship is not recoverable from the artefact at all. Only requiring a CLEAN TREE reaches authorship. 

**THE GREEN ARM IS THEREFORE: a clean tree is required for a build into the shared artefact path; a dirty build goes to a private `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and is marked `dirty-<sha>`.** Both arms become reachable and the guard becomes buildable. **AND IT BUYS SOMETHING THE ORIGINAL COULD NOT: `dirty-<sha>` collapses to `<sha>` for shared builds, which is what makes AT-11.7's _source commit_ field mean CURRENCY rather than approximately-currency.** 11.6 is upstream of 11.7 in meaning; 11.7 remains buildable first, its spec being sound. 

**dc NAMED THE TELL AGAINST THEMSELVES AND IT DESERVES THE ANSWER ON THE MERITS: _this amendment would conveniently unblock my own row._ That is why they routed it instead of acting, and it is the right instinct. It does not apply here, and the reason is checkable rather than a matter of trust: THE AMENDMENT MAKES THE CRITERION STRICTER, NOT LOOSER.** _Clean tree required_ is a higher bar than _dirty in owned paths allowed_. An amendment that unblocks a node by RAISING the bar is not the shape the tell warns about; the shape it warns about is one that unblocks by lowering it. **The row was unbuildable because my spec was wrong, not because dc lacked a way through.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-11.7 **AN ARTEFACT'S DURABLE RECORD CARRIES TWO PRIMARY FIELDS -- THE ARTEFACT HASH AND THE SOURCE COMMIT -- EACH WITH THE QUESTION IT ANSWERS NAMED AT THE FIELD; AND A THIRD FIELD, DRIFT, IS HELD WITH A NAMED RELEASE CONDITION.** (vc 2026-08-19, ruling on dc's and ic's independently-proposed inversion and REFUSING its second half.) **The estate holds a MEASURED failure of each field alone.** Hash alone fails at CURRENCY: `f2e4d1f9005d0334` matched its record exactly for ten hours while refusing every project verb. **Source commit alone fails at IDENTITY: `dirty-18197aaf` was carried by two structurally different artefacts in one build -- `intent` at 9,008,848 bytes and `intentd` at 373,136 (dc, measured first-hand) -- and by three distinct binaries in one day (cc, observed), which is the instance the PIN limb exists because of.** **NEITHER SUBSUMES THE OTHER, so neither is secondary; demoting the hash reopens precisely what the pin was minted to close.** dc and ic converged on the demotion independently, **and the convergence was not evidence: both had the same blind spot and neither consulted the `dirty-18197aaf` instance already recorded on cc's board.** **THE THIRD PROPERTY IS DRIFT -- how much uncommitted code the artefact holds -- and NEITHER PRIMARY EXPRESSES IT** (cc). It is the job the `dirty` marker was always for, so **the gap and the hold are the same thing: the marker half is HELD until it is computed over the paths the claim is about, and the partition then CLOSES over currency + identity + drift rather than being asserted over two.** **A hold with a named condition is a covered property; a hold without one is a permanent exemption with no work-list.** **AND THE DRIFT WINDOW GIVES OPPORTUNITY, NOT OCCURRENCE** (vc, correcting cc): the specimen was built ~2h after the commit it names, and only the flag could establish occurrence -- **so the specimen demonstrates that drift is UNDETECTABLE, not that it happened.** **FIELD COUNT IS NOT MATURITY:** `dist-provenance.txt` carried commit + verdict + reason where the others had one field, three nodes cited it as the exemplar on that basis, **and it was describing a build 805 commits and two days old while saying nothing about it** -- accurate, well-formed, and silent about the property the reader needs. -- WITHDRAWN: hv ruling 2026-08-28 14:54Z, put by vc with three options and this one chosen: the provenance writer stays OUT of the 3.0.0 cut, and this withdrawal RE-RECORDS the reason on a premise true today. The prior reason's load-bearing clause ('WP-11 is RELEASE and hv asked for local usability, so the provenance writer has no cut to serve') died when hv's standing directives retired that framing first-hand and re-opened the row as a live question -- after which the gate counted a withdrawal resting on a dead premise (dc's flag, 2026-08-28; the record-outliving-its-premise class dc closed five instances of the same week). Current premise: fail-forward -- the release process that would consume the writer is itself still settling, and the live blocker on the cut is WP-14-blocks-WP-12, not provenance. Declined alternatives, recorded so an option never on the menu is distinguishable from one refused: IN-SCOPE (build the writer before the cut; adds unsized work to a cut already held open); DEFER TO CUT-TIME (leaves the dead-premise withdrawal standing, knowingly this time, which is strictly worse than the accident). ### WP-15 -- Skills catalogue triage: KEEP, UPDATE or RETIRE every Intent2-era skill (status: Not Started)

- AC-15.1 **The triage table carries a row for EVERY skill in the catalogue, and the denominator is in the output.** One row per skill in `intent/plugins/claude/skills/`, each with a verdict of KEEP, UPDATE or RETIRE and the reason for it -- INCLUDING the rows whose verdict is KEEP with no change. **An absent row and an unexamined skill are the same absence**, and a triage that reports only what it changed has an unmeasured arm which is precisely the skills nobody looked at. The row count is asserted against the catalogue enumerated at close, never against a count carried from when the WP was written. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-15.2 **No surviving skill names a verb, flag, path or file layout that the shipped surface does not carry, measured against the dispatch table rather than against memory of it.** A skill keyed to a v2 name keeps reading as correct prose while pointing at nothing, which is the keyed-versus-derived class arriving through documentation. The check is two-sided: it must find a planted stale reference before its zero is believed. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-15.3 **Every RETIRE verdict is carried out through `intent claude skills` rather than by hand, and the removal REACHES a consumer.** `intent claude skills sync` checksums `SKILL.md` alone, so a skill whose scripts changed without its `SKILL.md` changing does not propagate -- asserted by driving a consumer install, never by observing that the source directory is clean. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-15.4 **Every cross-reference in the surviving catalogue resolves**: `chains_to:` frontmatter targets, the `/in-*` skill names skills cite in each other's prose, and the rule ids they name. A retirement that leaves a dangling `chains_to:` has moved the breakage rather than removed it. -- satisfied: no (computed)

### WP-16 -- Contract drift: a shipped field with no model row is refused (status: Not Started)

- AC-16.1 **A check joins the schema property set to `data-model.md`'s table rows per entity and REFUSES IN BOTH DIRECTIONS**: a property with no row (a field shipped with no contract), and a row with no property (a contract describing something nobody implemented). Both, because they fail differently and the second is the more dangerous half -- it is what made `issue.body` look specified while it was homeless. **It is a SIBLING of `drift_check.sh`, never an arm of it**: that check compares generated faces to the Rust types, this compares a prose document to a schema, and folding them puts two questions behind one exit code. -- DESCOPED to ST0069 by hv via vc: 3.0.1 cut scope, hv ruling 2026-08-30. The requirement is unchanged and unbuilt; only the thread it is counted against moved.
- AC-16.2 **THE CHECK RUNS OVER EVERY MODELLED ENTITY AND PRINTS ITS DENOMINATOR.** `N of N entities checked, M properties` in the output -- because a check that silently skipped an entity and a check that found nothing produce the same line otherwise. **THE POPULATION IS DERIVED FROM THE SCHEMA AT RUN TIME AND IS NOT ENUMERATED HERE.** This row carried a nine-name list until 2026-08-29 and the list was WRONG -- derivation yields eleven, the two it omitted being `Invoker` and `Subject` -- **so the criterion warning against "a list maintained beside it" was itself that list, in the same sentence.** (vc, on ic's measurement; the row is reworded rather than corrected in place because the enumeration was the defect, not its contents.) **`Invoker` is the live proof: it is growing under ST0066 AC-00.2's invoker evidence right now, and an entity outside the checked population grows uncontracted, which is `issue.body`'s exact shape and the 434KB this WP exists because of.** -- DESCOPED to ST0069 by hv via vc: 3.0.1 cut scope, hv ruling 2026-08-30. Never-started feature work moved whole so ST0056's gate measures what 3.0.1 ships. NOT a descope-for-a-green: ST0056 stays BLOCKED after the move. The requirement is unchanged and unbuilt; only the thread it is counted against moved.
- AC-16.3 **A table the parser cannot read FAILS LOUDLY and is never skipped.** `data-model.md` is hand-authored markdown with prose between its tables, so an unparseable table is the likely case rather than the exotic one -- and a skipped entity reported as checked is the defect this WP exists to remove, one level up. The refusal names the entity and the table it could not read. -- DESCOPED to ST0069 by hv via vc: 3.0.1 cut scope, hv ruling 2026-08-30. The requirement is unchanged and unbuilt; only the thread it is counted against moved.
- AC-16.4 **The check is demonstrated RED in both directions before any green is trusted.** Delete a row from a table and watch the right entity fail; add a row for a property that does not exist and watch the other direction fail. **A check whose positive case has never been observed is not evidence**, and both arms are needed because a one-sided demonstration leaves the untested direction free to be a constant pass. -- DESCOPED to ST0069 by hv via vc: 3.0.1 cut scope, hv ruling 2026-08-30. ### WP-17 -- Form DSL: one declaration, TUI and WEB realisers, CRUD through intentsvcs (status: WIP)

- AC-17.1 **ONE DECLARATION, TWO REALISERS, AND THE SAME EDIT THROUGH EITHER PRODUCES THE SAME MODEL CHANGE.** A form declared once renders in the TUI and in the browser, and an edit made through each reaches an identical store state -- asserted by driving both against the same fixture and diffing the model, never by inspecting the two renderers for similarity. **This is `AC-08.2` one layer up**: in-process and daemon already owe identical results, and two realisers of one declaration owe the same thing for the same reason. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-17.10 **A PROSE FIELD HANDS OFF TO `$VISUAL`/`$EDITOR` THROUGH THE EXISTING LAUNCHER, AND THE RETURN PATH RE-READS BEFORE IT PAINTS.** The handoff calls `launch_editor` in `intent-cli/src/render.rs` -- **cited by SYMBOL, never by line: a line number inside a criterion is a second home for a fact the file already carries** (ic, 2026-08-29, after the two this criterion first cited had drifted 62 lines in a file three nodes write) and the existing realise-then-open path -- **a second resolver or a second realiser is the Highlander defect in the one place this estate can least afford it**, since `$VISUAL`-before-`$EDITOR` is already resolved once inside that same function. **THE RETURN IS THE DANGEROUS HALF, NOT THE DEPARTURE:** after the editor exits the store is behind the file, so the realiser RE-READS that artefact and treats the editor as its authority before rendering anything derived from it. **A TUI that repaints from its stale in-memory model and then saves writes the old bytes back over what the operator just wrote** -- the destroys-authored-prose class this estate already carries five open issues for, reduced to two keystrokes. **The terminal is restored on EVERY exit path including a panic** (drop guard plus panic hook, never a tidy happy path), and the fate of an unsaved form at handoff time is STATED rather than discovered. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.11 **THE LAYOUT IS A FLAT SCROLLING COLUMN OF `{title, value, type}` ROWS WITH NO BORDERS AND NO DECORATION, AND THE ONLY FORMATTING GUARANTEE IS COLUMN ALIGNMENT.** Field names align in one column and values align in another, across the whole form -- **that alignment IS the design, so it is asserted rather than eyeballed**: every rendered row has its name starting at one column and its value at another, measured over a form whose longest name and longest value both exceed the viewport. **THE FOOT IS THE RATIFIED DESIGN'S, NOT THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL WORDING.** This row said *one modeline at the foot above a single rule*; `tui-design.md`, ratified by hv on 2026-08-29, specifies FIVE sections separated by TWO rules -- APP ROW, BODY, then STATUS, COMMAND and INFO. **THE DESIGN WINS AND THE ROW IS CORRECTED TO IT** (vc, 2026-08-30, on ic's finding): the design is a day newer, hv drove it against real data, and a criterion that contradicts a ratified design is the criterion being stale rather than the design being wrong. **ic BUILT TO THIS ROW AND WAS RIGHT TO** -- they had read only section 3 and the row is what the register offered them, which is the whole hazard: a stale criterion is indistinguishable from a current one at the point of use. **THIRD INSTANCE OF THE DATED-MEASUREMENT CLASS ON THIS THREAD** after `AC-12.4` and `AC-17.6`, and the first where a row contradicts a ratified artefact rather than merely citing a moved surface -- so the class is not only about staleness in prose, it reaches rows that were never reconciled against a document written after them. **A field whose value does not fit is truncated visibly, never wrapped into a second row**, because a wrapped value breaks the one guarantee the layout makes. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.12 **`intent explore` OPENS THE TUI AT THE TOP OF THE MODEL, AND IT IS THE SAME VIEW STACK ENTERED AT A DIFFERENT DEPTH RATHER THAN A SECOND SURFACE.** (hv requirement 2026-08-30, added while AFK; scoped by ic, minted by vc.) `explore` enters at ENTITIES; `intent edit <kind> <id>` enters the same stack at ITEM. Same keys, same menu, same descents -- **so a second navigation model is unrepresentable rather than merely discouraged.** **THE VERB IS `explore` AND NOT `browse` ON THE DESIGN'S OWN GROUNDS, NOT ON TASTE: `intent browse <kind> <id>` already ships meaning OPEN IN THE BROWSER**, and one word carrying two meanings is the exact defect `tui-design.md` section 5 records for `Up`/`Down` and section 4 for `/`-as-menu-and-prefix. **`explore` TAKES AN OPTIONAL ADDRESS** (hv, 2026-08-30, overruling vc; recorded by ic at `d9860add`). hv's words: _I see no reason why `intent expl[ore] ...` couldn't take an intent://... URL scheme URI or even an ID that it tries to match to something if it can, and if it can't it just opens at the root. That doesn't obviate `intent edit ...` in any way._ **THE RULING THIS REPLACES SAID THE OPPOSITE AND IT IS KEPT RATHER THAN DELETED, BECAUSE THE REASON IT DID NOT REACH IS THE USEFUL PART.** vc's objection was three doors onto one surface, and its whole content was NO SECOND RESOLVER -- which this does not create: `address::promote` for a bare id (the estate's one door, which refuses ambiguity by naming both forms) and `address::parse` for a URI, both reaching that one door. **Nothing new resolves anything, so the objection had no subject** (ic, 2026-08-30). Withdrawn, not narrowed. **A THIRD SPELLING -- A `/thread/ST0056` PATH -- WAS CONSIDERED AND REFUSED, AND THAT REFUSAL IS WHY THE WITHDRAWAL HOLDS:** `View::parse` validates nothing (`/banana` parses as `Collection { kind: "banana" }`), so accepting a path would need fresh validation against `nav::kinds` -- and THAT would be the second resolver the objection was right to fear. hv named two forms; the third was ic's own initiative and ic withdrew it with that reason (15:09Z). **vc's earlier reword of this row transcribed the withdrawn spelling back in, which both named a spelling the build does not have AND re-created the objection the same sentence was recording as discharged; ic refused to green the row against it, correctly** (ruled by vc, 2026-08-30). `nav.rs`'s `land` carries the identical refusal in its own doc, so the criterion and the code now agree. **THE FALLBACK IS THE ROOT PLUS THE REASON ON SCREEN, NEVER THE ROOT IN SILENCE.** hv's _just opens at the root_ contrasts with REFUSING, not with TELLING -- and a fallback that does not announce itself is the answers-confidently-from-partial-evidence class this rewrite exists to remove. `intent edit ST9999` must still refuse, because it was asked to ACT on a specific thing while `explore` was asked to OPEN THE EXPLORER near one. **AND THERE ARE THREE OUTCOMES, NOT TWO -- THE ADDRESS GRAMMAR IS WIDER THAN THE VIEW LADDER** (ic's finding, ruled into this row by vc 2026-08-30). `Entity` carries `Node`, `NodeInbox`, `Event`, `At` and `Attachment`: all addressable, none with a view. So *named nothing* and *named something this surface cannot show* are DIFFERENT FACTS and must read differently -- telling an operator that `intent:///nodes/vc` named nothing is FALSE, and it is the shape that sends them hunting for a thing they already have. **Same family as clap's `unrecognized subcommand` on an ambiguous prefix: a gate that cannot say WHICH of two things went wrong eventually says the one that starts a search instead of ending it.** **AND A FIFTH INPUT CASE THAT WAS IN NEITHER LIST, WHICH IS THE ONE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED: `ST9999`.** `promote` is purely syntactic and never reads the store, so a well-formed id for an ABSENT thread resolves perfectly; `rows_for` renders a form that will not load with its field names intact; **so an absent thread and an empty one PAINT THE SAME SCREEN.** That is `AC-17.7`'s reachable-and-blank defect arriving through the front door of the feature built after it. The presence test is an INJECTED closure and `the_presence_test_is_actually_consulted` is the control for the whole file -- a `land` that ignored its argument would pass every other assertion in it. **`Landing` LIVES IN `intentsvcs::nav`, BESIDE `View::parse`** (radius ruled by vc): the web face needs the same resolution for a URL bar and a jump-to box, and `intentd` cannot reach the CLI. **THE CHECKABLE PROPERTY, WHICH IS THE REASON THIS IS A CRITERION AND NOT A TICKET: THE TUI'S VIEW STACK AND THE WEB'S URL PATH ARE THE SAME SEQUENCE, BOTH DERIVED FROM THE SAME SCHEMA RELATIONS, SO NEITHER RENDERER INVENTS A LEVEL.** `[entities]` and `/`; `[entities, thread]` and `/thread`; `[entities, thread, ST0056]` and `/thread/ST0056`. **That is `AC-17.1` one level up -- 17.1 diffs the MODEL after an edit, this diffs the NAVIGATION -- and it is what gives `--browser` an exact meaning at any depth.** Asserted by round-tripping EVERY view the real declaration can produce through its path, never over hand-picked examples. **AND EVERY PATH SEGMENT IS A NAME THE DECLARATION ALREADY CARRIES** (ic's finding, ruled by vc 2026-08-30): `/thread/ST0056/wps`, never `/threads/ST0056/work-packages`. **A pluralisation rule is a SECOND HOME FOR NAMING** -- it mints strings that appear in no declaration, it breaks on the first kind that does not take `-s`, and it must be inverted to route. Section 9 shows a plural in prose and **the prose is what needs correcting, which is hv's hand and is flagged rather than done.** If the web face wants plurals for display, that is a rendering concern layered on ONE derived path, never a second derivation -- **because the moment the two faces derive their own, they are no longer the same sequence and this criterion is unsatisfiable by construction.** -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.2 **THE DSL DECLARES LAYOUT AND WIDGET, NEVER THE FIELD SET.** Every field in a form declaration resolves to a property of the entity schema face, and a field naming a property that does not exist is REFUSED by name at load. **A hand-authored list of fields is a second home for what `schema/*.json` already declares, and it goes stale exactly the way `populations.shipped` did** -- so the form carries order, label, widget and editability, and takes existence and type from the schema. Held to the converse too: an editable property appearing in NO form is named in the output rather than silently unreachable. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.3 **EVERY WRITE GOES THROUGH `intentsvcs`, AND NEITHER REALISER TOUCHES SQLite.** The TUI and the web face are CLIENTS exactly as the CLI and `intentd` are (`design.md:18`, hv 2026-08-15: the typed API is the only door in, which is what makes conformance structural). **A realiser holding a `rusqlite` dependency fails this criterion by construction** -- `AC-02.5` already requires `rusqlite` in exactly one Cargo.toml and this row is asserted against that same check rather than beside it. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.4 **THE WIDGET VOCABULARY IS CLOSED AND DECLARED, AND AN UNKNOWN WIDGET IS REFUSED BY NAME RATHER THAN SKIPPED.** The shipped set is `text`, `number`, `select`, `button` and `prose`; the vocabulary is declared in the DSL schema the way `entry_dispositions` and `target_states` are declared in the register, and a value outside it is a load-time refusal. **`prose` IS NOT AN INLINE MULTI-LINE WIDGET -- it is a HANDOFF to the external editor** (`AC-17.10`), which is what keeps the form a flat single-line-per-field column rather than a layout engine. **`select` on a status field offers only the legal transitions from the current state and prompts for a reason where the transition owes one** -- the machine already refuses illegal moves, and a plain dropdown would offer them and discover the refusal after the keystroke. **A skipped field renders a form that looks complete and is not**, which is the silent-partial class this thread exists to remove. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.5 **TAB ORDER IS DECLARATION ORDER, IT IS TOTAL, AND IT IS REVERSIBLE.** Every field is reachable going forward and going backward, focus wraps at both ends, and no field can be entered that cannot be left -- **the same no-trap-state property `no_state_can_be_entered_and_not_left` enforces on the transition machine**, applied to focus. Asserted by walking the whole field set in both directions and reconciling against the declaration, never by driving a few tabs and observing that it looked right. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.6 **`intent edit <kind> <id>` AND `intent browse <kind> <id>` REACH ONE MODEL THROUGH ONE SERVICE, AND THE KIND IS WHAT DISAMBIGUATES.** Measured 2026-08-29: `intent edit 1` refuses with **`1` names both a steel thread and an issue**, so the kind argument is not new syntax for its own sake -- it resolves an ambiguity the tool ALREADY reports. Measured 2026-08-29, BEFORE the shape change: `intent edit` was `edit <ADDRESS> [FILE]`, so `intent edit st 68` parsed `st` as the address and `68` as the file, and refused about the wrong thing. The shape change was therefore a deliberate surface decision belonging to the interface owner, and the register row was authored before either realiser was built. **IT HAS SINCE LANDED:** measured 2026-08-30 by ic, `intent edit <KIND> <ID> [FILE]` ships with `--editor`, `--path` and `--browser`; `intent browse <KIND> <ID>` ships; and the ambiguity refusal this row opens with no longer reproduces, because `intent edit 1` now refuses about a missing `<ID>` rather than about `1` naming two entities. **THE REQUIREMENT IS UNCHANGED AND UNSATISFIED; ONLY ITS EVIDENCE MOVED** -- the verbs shipping is not the criterion, which is that both reach ONE MODEL through ONE SERVICE, and `AT-17.6` is still to-write. **Second instance of the `AC-12.4` shape on this thread: a row carrying a dated measurement of a surface that has since moved.** The correction goes BESIDE the artefact rather than instead of it, so the record that the change was deliberate survives. Both verbs cover ST, WP and ISSUE. -- satisfied: no (computed)
- AC-17.7 **NAV IS DERIVED FROM THE MODEL, NOT HAND-BUILT, AND EVERY LEVEL IS REACHABLE AND LEAVABLE.** Up and down traverse entity-kind -> collection -> item -> child (entities -> steel threads -> ST0068 -> its work packages), for ST, WP and ISSUE alike, and the traversal is generated from the schema relations rather than from a hand-written menu -- **a hand-written navigation tree is the same second home as a hand-written field list and goes stale the same way** (`AC-17.2`). **No level can be entered that cannot be left**, which is `no_state_can_be_entered_and_not_left` applied to navigation, and it is asserted by walking every level in both directions rather than by spot-checking a path. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.8 **THE TUI REACHES EVERY AUTHORED ARTEFACT OF AN ENTITY AND REFUSES A GENERATED VIEW BY NAMING IT.** The file vocabulary the tool already states is `info, design, impl, tasks, acceptance`; some are authored and some are rendered, and **offering an edit that the next render discards is worse than refusing it** -- the estate has five open issues that are prose destroyed by a writer that was only supposed to read. **The authored/generated split is READ from the model, never hard-coded in the realiser**, so `info.md` changing disposition under the round-trip moves this behaviour without touching the TUI. -- satisfied: yes (computed)
- AC-17.9 **THE CURRENT MODE IS ALWAYS VISIBLE, AND ESC ALWAYS REACHES NAV MODE FROM ANYWHERE.** A modal interface whose mode is not on screen is the classic trap, and the failure is silent: keystrokes go somewhere the operator did not intend and nothing says so. **ESC is total** -- reachable from every field, every widget and every nav level, including mid-edit, with the disposition of an in-flight edit stated rather than left to discover. -- satisfied: yes (computed)

## Acceptance Tests

### ST-level

- AT-00.1 `tests/conformance/run_v2_suite.bash` -- covers AC-00.1 -- status: red -- **the harness EXISTS and this row was wrong at `to-write`, which is why it is red rather than merely unwritten.** It retargets through ic's `INTENT_BIN` seam (`tests/lib/test_helper.bash:21`), and **it BURNS FIRST** -- points `INTENT_BIN` at `/usr/bin/false` and requires the named files to fail before trusting any result, which is the only thing that catches a file resolving `intent` some other way and never seeing the override. Red for two named reasons, not for being unbuilt: **the surface is not ported (WP-04 is the v2 replacement and is unbuilt), so the estate cannot be green**; and this AC additionally carries the burning-coverage clause I added on 2026-08-14 against ic's `config` finding -- **no command family at zero burning coverage** -- which `coverage_map.sh` reports and which no run has yet cleared. **AC-00.1 also carries the 28 deferred non-core `pending` rows**: ic's to name, gated here, not forgiven. **A first run coming back green would mean the file list had been scoped to what already passes**, which the harness says of itself in its own header. The full 100-file estate is deliberately not the default because hv runs the full suite externally; a harness that invites a long unattended run from a tool session is one that will be run that way by accident
- AT-00.10 `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/rig_selftest.sh` -- covers AC-00.10 -- status: red -- HELD RED DELIBERATELY AND THE INSTRUMENT PASSES -- 18 of 24 refusal sites driven, six named as out of reach in the criterion. - AT-00.11 `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/of_n_closes_over_examined.sh` -- covers AC-00.11 -- status: to-write -- **DRIVE EACH INSTRUMENT ACROSS TWO TREES WHOSE FILTER AND EXAMINED POPULATIONS DIFFER; DO NOT READ THE SOURCE.** dc adjudicated ONE instrument with a purpose-built rig and five cases -- that is the cost per instrument, and any plan that assumes cheaper is assuming the proxy is the parser. **Take the gated ten first** (`canon_commit_check.sh` is done and is the worked example), then the manual seven. **The nested tree comes from `git worktree add` at a pre-relocation revision**, not from a fixture -- the point is the real population, and after WP-01 the nested shape survives only in history. **Report the nomination's REACH alongside its result**: dc's proxy was two greps and misses any count derived from a path shape without the literal in the same file, so a clean run over the ten is not a clean run over the estate, **and the tool must say so in its OUTPUT rather than leaving the reader to infer coverage from a pass.** **POPULATION, STATED RATHER THAN INHERITED: this row drives the instruments whose M CAN MOVE under the relocation -- those carrying an `intent/st` path shape.** An instrument whose output is byte-identical nested and flat is NOT in this row's population and cannot be adjudicated here; it belongs to AT-00.12. **Leaving the population unstated is how the nomination's horizon travels into the drive** (ic 2026-08-18, who found the eleventh from inside the set that had just adjudicated itself). **POSITIVE CONTROL REQUIRED BEFORE ANY CLEAN RESULT IS BANKED -- THIS ROW IS ITSELF A SWEEP AND INHERITS THE RULE IT ENFORCES** (ic 2026-08-18, from vc's withheld zero; procedure banked by ic at `5990c4df`). **A clean run over the ten is worth NOTHING until the harness has been shown to produce a finding against an instrument confirmed defective.** The principle was already here -- an observable that cannot move is not a check -- but **the STEP was not: RUN THE SWEEP AGAINST THE CONFIRMED POSITIVES BEFORE BANKING THE CLEAN RESULT.** A principle gets nodded at; a step gets executed. **The instance that proves it: vc's end-of-file test over 57 positional citations returned 0, and returns 0 against the FOUR ALREADY CONFIRMED ROTTED, because all four sit inside a 1215-line file.** **THE CONTROL IS `canon_commit_check.sh` AT A REVISION WHERE THE DEFECT REPRODUCES, AND THAT REVISION MUST BE FOUND BY RUNNING IT, NOT BY READING THE LOG.** It is REPAIRED at HEAD -- `:326` now carries `EXAMINED 2 of 1` only as a comment about its own history -- so the control is a pre-fix checkout. **AC-00.11 records the defect as live at `c51f10d5`; vc has NOT reproduced it there and that citation is a starting point, not a verified control.** The property to reproduce is an `EXAMINED n of m` with m < n. **AND THE CONTROL IS FREE: this harness already takes both trees as worktrees at named revisions, so a third checkout at the defective revision costs no new machinery** -- which removes the usual reason a positive control gets skipped. **THE TWO REVISIONS, NAMED SO NEITHER IS EVER RE-DERIVED (vc 2026-08-19, written in BEFORE the nested shape became archaeology): PRE-relocation (nested) = `8bb47e49`, verified 57 `thread.json` and 0 `.canon` entries; POST-relocation (flat) = `16048f82`, verified 0 `thread.json` and 97 `.canon` entries, 97 renames with 97 identical blob SHAs.** `git worktree add` at `8bb47e49` for the nested end. The row previously named the pre-relocation revision by PROPERTY and not by value, which is a citation carrying a subject and no revision -- the form this estate rules against, in the row that drives the instruments that rule against it. **AND FIVE OF THE TEN GATED TOOLS CARRY NO `intent/st` PATH SHAPE AT ALL** (dc's enumeration at `f789ae48`, replacing the uncommitted two-grep proxy): nested and flat emit byte-identical output for those five, so driving them here is a vacuous pass. **They belong to AT-00.12's population, not this one** -- which is what AT-00.12's separate existence was minted to buy, now paying out in the concrete rather than in principle.
- AT-00.12 `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/of_n_labels_its_derivation.sh` -- covers AC-00.11 -- status: green -- **READ THE INSTRUMENT'S OUTPUT; DO NOT DRIVE THE TREE.** The declarative half of AC-00.11, minted as its own row because its POPULATION differs from AT-00.11's -- **not because it is a harder version of the same check.** (ic 2026-08-18, routed before building the harness rather than after; ruled by vc.) **AT-00.11's population is the instruments whose M CAN MOVE under the relocation. This row's population is EVERY instrument that emits an `N of M`, path shape or not.** Bundling the two makes a green ambiguous about which population was covered -- **which is AC-00.11's own defect, committed by the row that enforces AC-00.11** (the EXTENT limb: a subset of the right KIND passes a depth check). **`rig_selftest.sh` IS THE FIRST MEMBER AND THE REASON.** Its `18 of 24` carries no `intent/st` path shape at all, so nested and flat emit byte-identical output and the two-tree differential is a **vacuous pass by the estate's own rule -- an observable that cannot move is not a check.** **THE BLIND SPOT IS INHERITED, ONE LEVEL APART: the same absence of a path shape that made dc's two-grep nominating proxy blind to the eleventh makes AT-00.11's DRIVE METHOD blind to it too.** `10 IS A FLOOR` was read correctly for the nomination and then the adjudication inherited the same horizon. No amount of differential rigour reaches an observable that does not move. **WHAT IT REQUIRES:** where derivation is possible, the number is derived (AT-00.11 proves that); **where it is not, the recorded number is LABELLED AT THE NUMBER and what would derive it is NAMED** -- AC-00.11's third arm, which existed in words with no driver pointing at it. **A clean run of AT-00.11 over the ten plus ic's two would otherwise report a clean estate while the instrument that MOTIVATED the third arm went untested by the thing that tests the criterion.** **THIS ROW'S NATURAL FAILURE MODE IS THE CAREFUL READ, AND IT MUST BORROW AT-00.11'S DISCIPLINE TO SURVIVE IT** (ic 2026-08-18, from the rebuild that produced the retraction in AC-00.10). Reading an instrument's output to check that M is labelled is a READING task, and **a careful read produced a confident wrong definition of `rig_selftest.sh`'s own denominator twice.** What corrected it both times was DRIVING -- mapping cases to sites and finding two that mapped to ZERO. **So this row does not merely inspect the output string: it maps each recorded number to the population that would derive it, and a number mapping to zero or to two candidate populations is itself the finding.** A declarative check that only greps for a label passes on a label attached to nothing. **SELF-GRADING HAZARD, RULED 2026-08-18 (ic flagged it before building; vc ruled). `rig_selftest.sh` IS NOT EXCLUDED, BECAUSE EXCLUDING IT TREATS A ONE-FILE PROBLEM THAT THIS IS NOT.** The instrument will be written by the node who has just spent an evening making `rig_selftest.sh` compliant, **so the risk is not that one file is graded by its own author -- it is that the INSTRUMENT'S DEFINITION OF COMPLIANCE becomes _whatever `rig_selftest.sh` now does_, which biases every file it grades.** Excluding the exemplar by name leaves that untouched and buys nothing. **THE REMEDY IS A MUTATION TEST AGAINST THE CRITERION RATHER THAN THE EXEMPLAR, WITH TWO NAMED MUTANTS: (i) the number LABELLED but its derivation NOT NAMED; (ii) the derivation named but NOT AT the number.** Both must go red. **If the instrument can only recognise the exemplar's exact shape, both mutants pass and it is an exemplar-matcher wearing a criterion's name.** **AND vc ADJUDICATES `rig_selftest.sh` INDEPENDENTLY AND BLIND -- before seeing the instrument's verdict on it -- then compares.** Blind-then-compare is ic's own discipline from the declared-marker enumeration, applied to the one file where it is worth the cost, **because AC-00.10 gates every other row's verdict in this thread.** **Printing the self-graded property is owed but is the WEAKEST of these and must not be mistaken for the remedy: stating a bias does not remove it.** **STATUS CORRECTED to-write -> red 2026-08-18 (ic caught it; `stale_at_check.sh` fired on two of their commits against this row).** The file exists at 229 lines, and by this estate's own rule **`to-write` means UNWRITTEN while `red` means it EXISTS and does not pass** -- a built instrument recorded as unwritten understates the estate in the one direction nobody audits, because an unwritten row invites no questions. **AT-00.11 stays `to-write` deliberately: its file genuinely does not exist, and it is gated on cc's FILE move rather than their code.** **vc's BLIND ADJUDICATION OF `rig_selftest.sh` WAS SENT BEFORE READING THE INSTRUMENT OR ic's VERDICT, and it is PASS with one residual**: the output names `$DIE_SITES` as _`die` call-sites_, which names the KIND but not the METHOD -- **and the method is exactly where the retired 24 failed**, since that same phrase admitted both the left-margin scan (24) and the observable count (41). A reader cannot tell from the output which produced it; naming the forms counted would close it. **THE BLIND PAIR AGREED (both PASS) AND THE AGREEMENT WAS THE LEAST USEFUL PART -- RECORD THE REASON, BECAUSE AN AGREEING BLIND ADJUDICATION LOOKS LIKE A NULL RESULT AND IS NOT ONE** (ic 2026-08-18, on the run itself). **The yield was the DIVERGENCE IN WHAT EACH METHOD COULD SEE.** vc read the emitted output as a reader and found that `$DIE_SITES` named a KIND rather than a METHOD; **ic's tool structurally could not produce that finding** -- `$DIE_SITES` is not an operand of any ratio, so the parser never looked at it, and for `$TOTAL_SITES` it asks only _does a derivation exist in the source_, never _do the words naming it identify a countable thing_. Conversely ic's parser classified `:339`'s retired ratio in prose as **UNCLASSIFIABLE, claiming nothing rather than guessing** -- which a reader would have argued about. **SO THE TWO DIRECTIONS OF THE AGREEMENT CLASS ARE NOW BOTH ON THE RECORD AND THEY SHARE ONE DISCRIMINATOR: COULD EACH INSTRUMENT HAVE PRODUCED THE OTHER'S FINDING?** If NO, agreement is evidence of complementary coverage and the divergence is the result. If YES -- one method used twice -- agreement is evidence of nothing, and that is the regex-reproducing-the-hand-count case in AC-00.10. **Same observation, opposite value, and the discriminator is never the verdicts.** **AND ic's TOOL IS A FORM-CHECKER THAT CANNOT CHECK A REFERENT** -- their own roster bar, arriving in the instrument they built hours after quoting it at others; **it is AC-01.7's class (a declaration must RESOLVE, not merely parse) in a new instrument.** The tool now states that limit in its own reach output, attributed. **A reader is the instrument for that class and a second grep would never have found it.** **DRIVEN GREEN 2026-08-30 (vc): rc=0 over 23 ratio instances, partition closes 16 + 6 + 1 = 23.** It went red on exactly ONE finding and the finding was **vc's own roster row**, written the day before, transcribing `instrument_reach_census.sh`'s DERIVED figures into `runner_roster_check.sh`'s prose -- flagged correctly as `N [RECORDED] M [RECORDED]` with no declaration. **THE FIX WAS TO DELETE THE COPY, NOT TO LABEL IT, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THIS ROW'S OWN SUBJECT:** a label would have certified a number the tool DERIVES as one somebody RECORDED, which is AC-00.13's corollary exactly -- the remedy for a dangling value committing the inexpressible-value defect. A roster row is prose ABOUT an instrument; the instrument prints the figure; **a prose copy of a derived number has no witness and drifts the first time the corpus grows.** The population moved 24 -> 23 because the fix REMOVED a ratio instance rather than reclassifying one, and the partition closing at the new total is what says so.
- AT-00.13 `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/partition_closes_check.sh` -- covers AC-00.12 -- status: green -- **REQUIRE THE SUM IN THE OUTPUT, NOT THE ABILITY TO COMPUTE IT.** An instrument passes only if it PRINTS its parts and asserts they close over a named whole. **POSITIVE CONTROLS ARE ON RECORD AND MUST BE DRIVEN BEFORE ANY CLEAN RESULT IS BANKED: `EXAMINED 2 of 1 ... the other -1` (impossible, and loud only by luck) and cc's `58` against 57 rows (possible, and therefore silent).** **THE SECOND IS THE REAL TEST -- an instrument that catches only the impossible remainder has not been shown to work**, because the silent overshoot is the case the criterion exists for. **REACH, IN THE OUTPUT: this row checks that a partition ASSERTS closure; it cannot check that the WHOLE is the right whole** -- cc's `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE` miss closes arithmetically over the wrong population and passes here. That belongs to AC-00.11 and saying so is required, or a green reads as coverage it does not have. **RED 2026-08-30 (vc): `partition_closes_check.sh` built and driven; rc=1 on the live estate with THREE findings, and the row stays red until they are closed.** Findings: `machine_table_check.sh` (GUARD AXIS -- 47 agree, 0 disagree, 0 UNMEASURED of 47 rows), `rulings_check.sh` (41 declared against 39 + 1 + 1 + 0 + 0), and `runner_roster_check.sh`, WHICH WAS vc's AND IS FIXED IN THE SAME COMMIT -- it now prints `16 gated + 28 manual + 20 not-an-instrument = 64` and refuses at exit 2 if that fails to close. The other two are routed to their owners. **BOTH POSITIVE CONTROLS THIS ROW NAMES ARE DRIVEN, AND THE ROW WAS RIGHT THAT THE SECOND IS THE REAL TEST.** cc's silent overshoot (`3 + 4 = 8`, possible and therefore quiet) reds arm A. **AND THE FIRST ONE REQUIRED AN ARM THAT DID NOT EXIST:** `EXAMINED 2 of 1 ... the other -1` carries no `+` and only three integers, so arms A and B were both structurally blind to it -- an of-N remainder is a partition stated in a different GRAMMAR. Arm C reaches it, fires on `2 of 1`, and stays silent on a legitimate `300 of 510`. **Had the row not named that control, the tool would have shipped claiming a class it could only half see.** **SEVEN MORE CONTROLS, ALL AGAINST A PREDICTION WRITTEN FIRST, INCLUDING TWO THAT WERE LIVE FALSE POSITIVES IN THE FIRST DRIVE AND MUST STAY DEAD:** a machine NUMBER matching two counts (4 = 2 + 2), and a filesystem path whose digits happen to sum. **THE FIRST DRIVE REFUTED THE PREDICTION THREE WAYS AND THAT WAS ITS WHOLE VALUE:** arm A predicted 0 and returned 1 which was THE PARSER, not the estate (it summed every integer left of the `=`, so `510 recorded ... across 60 + 9 = 69` came out 579); arm B predicted 3 and returned a DIFFERENT 3 -- missing `corrected_check.sh` whose partition spans two lines, finding `rulings_check.sh` which nobody predicted, and matching `machine_table_check.sh` on the WRONG line with the real one hidden behind a `head -1`. **REACH, AND THE FIRST LIMIT IS THE CRITERION'S OWN JUSTIFICATION: a partition that is BOTH unstated AND broken is invisible to this tool and to every other**, because nothing separates three unrelated integers from a failed partition without knowing which were meant to be parts, and only the author knows that. The stated sum is the single thing that makes a partition checkable by anyone but its author -- which is why this criterion demands the STATEMENT rather than demanding correctness. Also: 16 of 65 instruments (the gated set is the only one with a safe bare invocation), per line, over output rather than source, and at least three parts because two is coincidence. **MANUAL, NOT GATED, FOR TWO INDEPENDENT REASONS:** its subject fails today, and it costs 17.1s because it RUNS the gated set -- more than twice the whole gate budget. **GREEN 2026-08-30 (vc), driven live after dc landed both fixes at `a5e04f1f`: rc=0, `5 state a closure, 0 leave one unstated, 11 print no partition this tool can recognise. THE PARTITION CLOSES: 5 + 0 + 11 = 16.`** All three work-list members are in: `machine_table_check.sh` and `rulings_check.sh` by dc, `runner_roster_check.sh` by vc in the commit that opened this row. **AND dc CORRECTED ONE OF THE THREE ASSIGNMENTS, WHICH IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING ON THIS ROW.** vc routed `rulings_check.sh` as the same case as the other two. It is not: its `declared` figure is computed as `$((OK + BAD + HV + DANGLING + PROV))`, **so the sum closes BY CONSTRUCTION and no mutation of any bucket could ever break it.** Stating it and refusing on it would have been a dead guard -- **shipped inside a fix for the dead-guard class, nominated by the instrument built to catch it.** dc built the check that can actually fail instead: `SEEN` counted at the top of the loop, buckets compared against the rows actually read, and the malformed-id `continue` promoted from a silent hole to a declared `SKIPPED` bucket. **THAT IS NOW A STATED REACH LIMIT IN THE TOOL: arm B cannot tell a partition MEASURED against a population from one DERIVED from its parts, so a finding here is a CANDIDATE -- it says a partition is unstated, never that stating it would be worth anything.** dc drove six mutations with a baseline in the same run, and a seventh that passed because the MUTATION failed rather than the guard -- a skip planted on an id no row carries, re-driven against a row that exists.
- AT-00.14 `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/vocab_adequacy_check.sh` -- covers AC-00.13 -- status: red -- **THIS ROW CANNOT BE SATISFIED BY A MEMBERSHIP CHECK AND THAT IS THE POINT.** Adequacy is not mechanically decidable, so the instrument does the one thing that is: **for every enumerated vocabulary in the model, enumerate the STATES IN USE and require each to map to exactly one value.** A state mapping to none, or to two, is the finding. **NAMED MUTANTS, BOTH MUST GO RED: (i) a state that maps to no value; (ii) a state that maps to two.** **REACH: an instrument cannot enumerate states nobody has written down, so a clean run means _no INEXPRESSIBLE state was declared_, never _the vocabulary is adequate_.** Stating that in the output is mandatory -- **a green here is exactly the safe-looking value the criterion is about, and this row would otherwise commit its own defect.**

**BUILT 2026-08-30 (vc). THE INSTRUMENT EXISTS, IS DRIVEN, AND ITS SUBJECT FAILS: `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/vocab_adequacy_check.sh`, manual, 0.12s (n=5, no spread). RED WITH A CITATION RATHER THAN GREEN, and the row is more useful red than it was `to-write`.** Live estate returns exactly **1**, and it is `INV-03` -- issue **0167**.

**THE DESIGN THIS NOTE PRESCRIBED AND THE DESIGN BUILT ARE THE SAME THING ARRIVED AT FROM OPPOSITE ENDS, AND SAYING SO MATTERS BECAUSE THEY DO NOT LOOK ALIKE.** The note asks to *enumerate the STATES IN USE and require each to map to exactly one value*. **The note's own reach clause concedes that states nobody has written down cannot be enumerated** -- so the question becomes WHERE a state in use is written down, and the answer is: **in the authored prose beside the enum.** A vocabulary that cannot hold a state does not produce silence; it produces an author writing the truth next to the box. **The prose IS the register of states in use, and a contradiction between it and the enum IS a state mapping to no value.**

**BOTH NAMED MUTANTS ARE DRIVEN, AND THE SECOND ONE IS EXPRESSIBLE ONLY BECAUSE THE MODEL ALREADY HAD TO ALLOW IT.** (i) *maps to no value* -- a row declaring `ratified` whose prose says the ratification is outstanding. (ii) *maps to two* -- `rulings` is an ARRAY (it had to be: one row carries two rulings), so a row can declare `ratified` AND `provisional` about itself with both in the machine-read half. **Measured: five rows carry two rulings today and all five AGREE, so the live zero on that arm is a real zero rather than an arm that has never fired.**

**THE PREDICTION REFUTED THE INSTRUMENT THREE TIMES AND THAT IS THE EVIDENCE, NOT THE PASSES.** (1) A bare `outstanding|pending|awaiting` over the whole field returned **10 of which ONE was real** -- seven were one boilerplate string repeated across rows, two were HISTORY narrating a state the row had LEFT (*it was `pending-hv`, which is now answered*). (2) Tightening it with a fixed 70-character leading context then **DROPPED the true positive**, whose hedge sits 50 characters into the field: a loose pattern banked nine falsehoods and the tight one banked a clean sweep. (3) The subject-bound pattern predicted 1 and returned **8**: seven rows carry *hv ratification is therefore **NOT** outstanding*, and a window wide enough to bind subject to hedge is wide enough to span the `NOT` that reverses it -- **the instrument read seven rows SAYING IT IS FINE as seven problems**, well-formed and internally consistent at 8. **Every one of the three produced a confident number and none of them announced anything; only a prediction written first separated them.**

**AND A FOURTH, IN THE HARNESS RATHER THAN THE SUBJECT:** the count and the findings both left on stdout, so the command substitution captured the findings INTO the count and the contradicting fixture scored `FINDING...1` against a wanted `1` -- **an arm reporting a miss it had not made.** Caught by the fixtures, which is what they are for.

**THE NEGATIVE CONTROL IS WHAT STOPS FOUR PASSES BEING ONE METHOD USED FOUR TIMES**, and the history control is what keeps the two `pending-hv` narrations dead.

**REACH IS IN THE OUTPUT, NOT ONLY HERE, AND IT IS THE POINT RATHER THAN A CAVEAT.** AC-00.13 states adequacy is not checkable; this finds ONE SIGNATURE of it. **A vocabulary inadequate with NO prose beside it is invisible BY CONSTRUCTION -- the commoner and worse case -- and is not a residual to be closed later by widening a regex.** So is inadequacy the author never noticed, since the signature IS the author noticing. The hedge vocabulary is OPEN and undercounts, and its false negatives are exactly the rows whose authors stated it well, in their own words rather than in a keyword -- **`self_provenance_check.sh`'s finding, inherited rather than escaped.**

**WHAT MAKES THIS GREEN IS NOT A CLEANER REGEX.** It is `INV-03` gaining a vocabulary that can hold *ruled by vc, hv ratification outstanding, not blocking* -- or hv ratifying it outright, which dissolves the instance without touching the vocabulary. **Re-anchoring the enum to agree with the prose is the one fix that must not be made**, per this criterion's own corollary.

**FOUR OF FIVE CLAUSES ARE NOT ASSERTED BY ANY ROW COVERING THIS CRITERION, AND TWO OF THOSE ARE ASSERTED BY ROWS COVERING OTHER ONES. A criterion is not satisfied because its subject matter is tested somewhere in the estate; it is satisfied when a row that COVERS IT is green.**
