What the arm established is that measuring the debt is not paying it: all FOUR `Unbuilt` rows -- `Thread.acceptance`, `Criterion.kind`, `AcceptanceTest.kind`, `Issue.status`, four and not the five this note previously claimed -- hold values authored canon puts there with no verb to move them. **So the row is red for owed WORK, not for a missing measurement -- and as of 2026-08-17 that work is RULED AND UNBLOCKED rather than waiting on hv.** It was escalated as one ruling across all four rows, bundled with the self-loop question because both are one rule spanning several machines; **hv ratified both on vc's recommendation and the answer is materially smaller than the row implied.** Only `Issue.status` becomes a machine (Machine 4, declared from v2's measured behaviour, no guards). `Criterion.kind` folds into Machine 3 as a `(kind, state)` pair and `AcceptanceTest.kind` into the `AcceptanceTest.status` machine -- **neither is independently movable, because the kind/state pairing is enforced in the JSON Schema face (`model.rs:414-432`, `tests/ac_kind_state_invariant.rs`), so a conversion is one act moving two fields and is a transition of the PAIR rather than of either field.** `Thread.acceptance` is `Option<AcceptanceMode>`, an attribute rather than a lifecycle, and is **immutable after creation** -- owed nothing. **Self-loops are legal across all four machines, accepted-and-reported at exit 0 WITHOUT re-running the guard**, which is the half that matters here: re-running the gate on an already-`Completed` thread would let a criterion added after the close block a thread that is legitimately finished, which is this very criterion's own history. Full text in `data-model.md`. **What remains for this row is implementation and not adjudication**: wire Machine 4, accept self-loops on Machines 1-3, give each `kind` its paired conversion, and re-disposition `Thread.acceptance` off `Unbuilt`. **The row stays red until those land and the walk agrees with the ratified graph. CLOSING CONDITION SHARPENED 2026-08-17 (vc), on cc's finding, and it corrects MY OWN wording: "the walk" is singular and THREE tests carry that property between them, no one of them alone.** `the_implemented_graph_is_the_ratified_one_edge_for_edge` (`:1172`) compares the DECLARED table against `RATIFIED_*` consts transcribed from data-model.md -- **neither side is the implementation, so it is green with zero code driving any edge**, which cc observed empirically before adding Machine 4's drive arm. `every_declared_edge_is_a_mutation_that_exists` (`:693`) is what drives them, and `a_transition_the_ratified_machine_does_not_declare_is_refused` (`:1272`) is what shuts the other door. **This row may not be closed on the first alone**, and its name is the reason someone would try: it says `implemented` and it measures `declared`. cc is renaming it; the rename does not change what any of the three measures, so this condition is stated by BEHAVIOUR and not by test name -- table matches ratified, every declared edge drives, every undeclared pair refuses. **FORM CORRECTED 2026-08-16 (vc), `to-write` -> `red`, note restored by hand after 0033. The struck clause said `red` "would be a false statement about a suite that passes", and that is the reading I corrected on 2026-08-15**: the AT's status is the CRITERION's state, not the file's, so a passing suite behind an unmet criterion is exactly what a red row plus this note is for -- while `to-write` claimed no file existed when one does, and kept L2/L3 switched off besides. **MACHINE 4 LANDED 2026-08-17 (cc, `b504d91b`) AND THE THREE WALKS MEASURED AGAINST IT -- the third is VACUOUS FOR THIS MACHINE, BY CONSTRUCTION, AND MY OWN CLOSING CONDITION CANNOT SEE THAT** (vc, at `e7cf7902`, 18/18 green). Walk 1 carries `("Issue", "status", RATIFIED_ISSUE)`, and since `declared()` panics on a non-`State` field, walk 1 passing is itself the proof that `Issue.status` is a `State` -- which puts it in walk 2's `all_fields()` sweep, where `execute()`'s `("Issue", "status")` arm drives both edges. Two of three, established rather than asserted. **The third exercises ZERO pairs: Machine 4 is TOTAL.** Two states and two edges, so for every (verb, state) pair the state is either in the verb's from-set or is the verb's own target, and the walk's filter (`!from.contains(s) && s != to`) admits neither. Computed from the transcribed table: `issues.close` NONE, `issues.open` NONE. **So "every undeclared pair refuses" is satisfied by there being no undeclared pair, which is a true statement and not a measurement** -- and the condition I wrote to stop this row closing on a vacuous green is, for this machine, met by one. The clause stands for Machines 1-3 unchanged; for Machine 4 the honest reading is that the property is discharged by the machine's shape rather than by a test, and the row must not be read as three walks agreeing when one of them never spoke. **AND THE ZERO IS CURRENTLY INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM AN OMISSION, BECAUSE `Issue` IS NOT IN WALK 3'S LIST AT ALL** (`:1301-1303` enumerates `Thread`, `WorkPackage`, `Criterion`). A walk that did not visit a machine and a walk that visited it and found nothing to refuse produce the same output -- none -- which is ic's harness rule arriving on this row's own closing condition. **TWO HAND-ENUMERATED LISTS OMIT MACHINE 4 AND BOTH OMISSIONS ARE FREE TODAY, WHICH IS THE WORST STATE FOR THEM: nothing will red when they stop being free.** Walk 3 (`:1301`) and `a_verb_declared_to_record_a_justification_refuses_a_blank_one` (`:1376`) both list three entities; `attempt()`'s `match entity` (`:1242`) has three arms and a `panic!` default. The justification omission costs nothing only because `RATIFIED_ISSUE` declares no guards -- **the day anyone gives `issues close` a reason, that check silently does not run.** **The doc comment at `:1366` narrates this exact incident on the previous machine** -- `Criterion` missing from the loop left `ac.withdraw`'s ratified `ReasonRecorded` "transcribed, conformance-checked, and dead" -- and closes _"the list is now derived from the ratified tables themselves"_. **That is true of the VERB axis and false of the ENTITY axis, and the incident it describes was an entity omission**, so the repair hardened the axis that was not the problem. Recommended to cc and not ruled: derive all three entity lists from one array so a fifth machine cannot land in walk 1 and be missed by the others, and record each machine's undeclared-pair count so a structural zero reads as a zero rather than as an absence. **BOTH BUILT AND MUTATION-PROVED 2026-08-17 (cc, `1721e4bb`) -- AND BINDING THE LIST TO THE CODE FOUND THAT THIS ROW'S CENTRAL CLAUSE HAD BEEN DISCHARGED OVER TWO THIRDS OF ITS POPULATION.** `transitions.rs` classifies **SIX** fields as `Disposition::State`; only **four** carried transcriptions. `AcceptanceTest.status` and `WorkPackage.scope` were in the code, driven by the drive-walk, closed by the closure-walk, **and compared to nothing** -- so _"the implemented graph must MATCH THE RATIFIED MACHINES EXACTLY"_ was green while a third of the machines had no ratified graph to match against. **The class one level above the one both of us were looking at: the walks agreed, and their subject was a subset of the machines.** Note what was NOT wrong: `data-model.md:487` already said _"Three machines are documented above; `transitions.rs` classifies FIVE fields"_ and named both undocumented ones. **The document was right and the instrument was short, and nothing compared the two lists** -- which is why cc's binding of `RATIFIED` to `transitions::FIELDS` is the fix and a longer hand-list would not have been. **CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (cc, and the correction strengthens the conclusion rather than softening it): the document says FIVE and cc measured SIX, and BOTH ARE RIGHT AT THEIR OWN REVISION.** `Issue.status` was `Disposition::Unbuilt` until Machine 4 landed at `b504d91b` **that same morning**, which made it the sixth. **So the document's count was accurate when written and cc's own commit staled it hours before cc measured against it** -- the tree variant with cc as the cause and the document as the victim. **It is the strongest argument available for the binding**: a hand-list would have gone short again the same day, by the hand of the node maintaining it, and a number written in prose is a dated measurement rather than a rule. **RULED (vc, provisional pending hv): `RATIFIED_WITHOUT_A_TABLE` CHECKS THE RATIFICATION, IT DOES NOT ALTER IT, and cc should not withdraw it as offered.** Four reasons, in order of weight. The sentences are quoted **verbatim** from `data-model.md:489-490` and are the ratified text. The assertions map one-to-one onto the sentence's clauses and add nothing -- _"one verb"_ is `verbs.len() == 1`, _"any value ->"_ is `edge.from.is_empty()`, _"-> any value"_ is targets covering every `initial` state. **Third and decisive: "it does not need a table" is a claim about the graph's SHAPE, and it is true only while the shape holds** -- asserted once, at authoring time, and never revisited since, which is precisely a standing assumption nothing reads back. **Fourth, the tell: every failure message names the remedy as _write the table_ rather than _fix the code_.** The test does not forbid a second verb; it forbids a second verb going unratified. That is a trigger, and a trigger on a dormant assumption is the opposite of altering it. **Also ruled: the two remain ratified WITHOUT tables** -- inventing transcriptions for graphs whose own ratification says they carry no information would manufacture exactly the second artefact this thread has spent the day counting. **AND cc's INDEPENDENT WALK-3 DEFECT IS THE BEST FINDING OF THE THREE**: the filter excluded a verb's target **per EDGE**, so `ac.rescope` from `computed` was required to refuse on the strength of the edge whose target is `unsatisfied`, while `computed` is the same verb's OTHER target. **The self-loop predicate is per-VERB and a kind-dependent verb's target is a SET**; testing one member at a time is a well-formed wrong answer. **And it was invisible for exactly as long as `ac_rescope` hand-checked its from-state and refused everything -- so 0053's defect is what kept the walk that should have caught 0053 green.** A defect masking its own detector, which is why the fix and the test had to land together

### WP-06 -- CLI parity long tail (status: WIP)

- AT-06.1 `tests/conformance/run_v2_suite.bash` -- covers AC-06.1 -- status: red -- **same instrument as AT-00.1 and a different criterion, so read them together**: AT-00.1 asks whether the narrowed contract passes, this one asks whether the FULL command surface is ported and the suite is green minus register-recorded deviations only. Red because the surface is not ported, and it is honest red rather than absent instrument -- **the harness exists, burns in, and classifies per file**, so the failures it reports today are the expected ratified-deviation reds rather than an unbuilt check. **This AC carries one clause the other does not and it is an ORDERING constraint, not a coverage one: `intent config` must land a conformance test BEFORE its behaviour is designed**, or the `undefined` ruling on it is unverifiable by construction -- there is nothing to be faithful to, so a test written after the design would be measuring the design against itself. `coverage_map.sh` is the reporter for the zero-burning-coverage half and lives at `intent/st/ST0056/parity/tools/`
- AT-06.10 `native/rust/crates/intent-cli/tests/schema_versioning.rs` -- covers AC-06.10 -- status: green -- **all three conditions verified by vc at HEAD by RUNNING the tests, not by reading the file: 7 green in `schema_versioning.rs` + 5 in `schema_command.rs`.** (a) the constants live in `intentsvcs::faces` and `every_published_face_carries_both_version_parts` opens each of the five committed files and reads the marker back in that face's own idiom -- `-- KEY:` for SQL, `# KEY:` for SDL, `"x-intent-ver"` for the three JSON Schemas, which is why my first grep for the literal `INTENT_VER` read zero on the JSON side and found nothing wrong. (b) `intent schema --versions` exists and three tests cover it, of which **`what_versions_reports_is_what_the_printed_face_carries` is the load-bearing one** -- it checks the binary against ITSELF rather than against the committed files, because a `--versions` wired straight to the constant agrees with a healthy tree and the two mutations that matter (the generator stops injecting AND the command reports constants) travel together and are invisible against the files. cc recorded that limitation ON the weaker test before writing the stronger one. (c) `each_printed_face_is_byte_identical_to_its_committed_file` is a whole-string `assert_eq!` with **no strip and no exemption**, so the injected values are inside the compared bytes -- which is the whole of condition (c). **What this does NOT do is the scripted bump-and-observe the earlier note asked for**: independence is established structurally instead, by pinning each face type's contract hash separately and asserting at `each_face_carries_only_its_own_contract_version` that no face carries another's key. That is the stronger of the two -- a bump demonstrates independence once, the pins enforce it on every run -- and the contract hash strips both version markers AND the documentation, so a reworded comment does not read as a schema change. **The row sat at `to-write` while its test existed and was green, which is the precise hazard my own status ruling names: `to-write` is exempt from L2 and L3, so the one state a stale row can hide in is the state that suppresses the checks that would catch it.**
- AT-06.11 `native/rust/crates/intent-cli/tests/remedies_are_reachable.rs` -- covers AC-06.11 -- status: to-write -- **the discriminating case is that the check must walk the REMEDY STRINGS the binary emits, not the verbs it declares -- a test asserting every declared verb exists passes trivially and proves nothing.** Held RED with this note once written, until WP-10 lands `upgrade`: the known failing instance is v3's unmigrated-project refusal naming a verb v3 does not carry, and a green here before then would mean the walk is not finding it. **SEVERITY DROPPED 2026-08-16 (vc) WITHOUT THE VERDICT MOVING, and the distinction has nowhere else to live.** ic's `dcd32358` corrected the register: `upgrade` had been marked `retire`/`retire`, so `is_shipped()` -- which gates on BOTH -- would have shipped WP-10's migration with **no invocation at all**. The remedy now names a command the binary KNOWS (_"a known command that is not implemented yet"_) rather than one it has never heard of. **Still unsatisfied -- known-but-unimplemented does not clear _"names something the binary can actually do"_, since an operator sent there still arrives nowhere -- but this is now the ORDINARY BUILD-ORDER case rather than a contract contradiction, and the two are indistinguishable from the AC's verdict.** **The finding method is the part worth keeping**: ic harvested the remedy STRINGS emitted across 106 paths rather than the verbs declared, and **a test asserting every declared verb exists would have PASSED, because `upgrade` was declared retired and correctly absent** -- the declaration and the binary agreed with each other and both were wrong. **Neither `surface_check.sh` nor `corrected_check.sh` can reach that case by construction: a consistency check between two artefacts is blind to a mistake they both made**
- AT-06.12 `native/rust/crates/intentsvcs/tests/operator_id_spellings.rs` -- covers AC-06.12 -- status: green -- Landed `58979836`; full Rust suite green, no failures. **MUTATION-PROVEN IN SEVEN DIRECTIONS, AND THE HARNESS ITSELF HAD TO BE FIXED FIRST.** Each of width-check, wrong-collection refusal, the s/i branches, case-insensitivity, the ST-before-s tag order, the agnostic door's refusal and the suffix strip was removed in turn and the matching arm read RED; baseline and restore green. **THE FIRST RUN REPORTED `RESTORED -> RED` OVER A CORRECT TREE**: the harness restored with `mv`, which PRESERVES the backup's mtime, so cargo served a binary built from the mutated source -- a control lying in the safe-looking direction, and the same class as `init` reading the embed while `render` reads disk. Fixed to `cp` + `touch` and re-driven clean. **Two arms pin behaviour v2 HAS and this must not**: `foo` must not become `STfoo`, `99999` must not become `ST99999`. They are tested rather than trusted to the port's judgement because the shape of a port is to reproduce, and reproducing was wrong twice. The five-form arm was driven in the v2 checkout BEFORE being pinned, which is what makes it a measurement rather than a reading of the source.

- AT-06.8 `native/rust/crates/intent-cli/tests/flag_reachability.rs` -- covers AC-06.8 -- status: red -- **the discriminating case is a flag that is DECLARED and whose renderer arm does not read it, and a test that exercises only wired flags passes on the defect.** Walk the declared surface rather than a hand-listed set: a hand-listed set is the census that missed `st new -s`. The check must fail NAMING the flag, since the whole failure mode is that an inert flag is indistinguishable from a working one

**MOVED `to-write` -> `red` 2026-08-20 (vc ruling, on cc's own report that the row must not move to green). THE FILE NOW EXISTS AND THE ARM THAT WOULD PROVE THE CRITERION DOES NOT RUN**, which is precisely ST0057 AT-01.5's precedent -- _RED, NOT to-write: the file exists and nothing consults it_ -- arriving in a second thread. `to-write` asserts the test is unwritten and that is now false; `red` asserts it exists and does not yet prove the row, which is true. **A green would be false in a way nothing downstream could check, because the gating arms DO pass.**

**THE EXPIRY IS RECORDED HERE AND NOT ONLY IN THE FILE, ON THE COROLLARY vc RULED THIS MORNING: where reach is knowingly parked, it wants a ROW, because a comment is invisible from a green.** The population arm is `#[ignore]`d -- deliberately, rather than relaxed to a passing assertion, since relaxing a gate at the moment it stops covering anything converts a refusal into a silent pass. **It says NOT RUN, visibly, every run.** It un-parks when dc's `init` and `bootstrap` land: remove `#[ignore]` and set the baseline from one run against a settled tree. **The reason it cannot be set now is the TREE and not the check** -- the violation set went 18, then 8 after ic's correction, then 9 in one afternoon, and a baseline frozen mid-landing reds the workspace for four nodes on a number that was never stable.

**THE INSTRUMENT WAS WRONG TWICE BEFORE IT GATED ANYTHING, AND BOTH ARE RECORDED BECAUSE BOTH WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A CONFIDENT WRONG ANSWER.** (i) The scanner missed the MAJORITY idiom -- `render.rs` reads most booleans through its own `fn flag(m, name)` helper, eleven sites against four direct `get_flag` -- so **ten of eighteen baseline entries were false positives, including `st new -s`, which this contract had been quoting as the instance the census MISSED.** It is read at `render.rs:587`. **A satisfying sentence about a flag that works.** (ii) The exemption then filtered on the two existing helpers' SPELLING (`name: &str`, `(name)`) rather than their shape, and broke the moment dc's `init` helper named the same parameter `flag` -- **a predicate written around the instances in front of you is a list wearing a filter's syntax.** It now exempts by the property: a silent accessor line with no string literal spells a variable; one with a literal is a marker gap.

**TWO LIMITS, BOTH DRIVEN RATHER THAN REASONED, AND THE SECOND IS THE FILE'S OWN DEFECT PRESENT INSIDE IT.** It is whole-file, so it proves SOMETHING reads an id and not that the right arm does. And **a COMMENTED accessor counts as a read** -- measured by planting `get_flag("comment-probe")` inside a `//` and watching the finding vanish -- so **the worst case is a doc comment explaining why a flag is not read, which would silence the finding it explains.**

**AND THE INSTRUMENT'S APERTURE HAS A DEMONSTRATED INSTANCE ONE STEP INSIDE IT (cc, found by hand rather than by the check).** `intent init` declared `--with-st0000`, so the arg id is `with-st0000`, and the code read `"with_st0000"`: the refusal never fired and the flag was silently ignored. **The check PASSES it, because `with_st0000` IS spelled in the renderer -- it simply is not an id that exists.** A check keyed on _is this string spelled_ cannot distinguish a correct spelling from a plausible one. **The instance is FIXED at HEAD -- `render.rs:2131` reads `("with-st0000", ...)` with the defect recorded in the comment above it (vc verified by reading, rather than carrying cc's pre-fix drive) -- and the APERTURE is not.** That is the durable half, and it is this criterion's own class one aperture in from what its instrument sees.

**CORRECTION 2026-08-20, AND IT IS TO THE CLAUSE ABOVE THAT CLAIMED THE MOST RIGOUR (cc caught it; vc verified every clause at zero hops before accepting it).** _The instance is FIXED at HEAD_ is FALSE. At HEAD `render.rs:2131` is `///`, `fn init(` appears **zero** times, `with-st0000` appears **zero** times, and `init.rs` is **untracked**. **dc has committed none of it, so at HEAD the defect does not exist because the FEATURE does not exist, and _fixed at HEAD_ describes a tree in which there was nothing to fix.** The correct wording is **dc's uncommitted tree, 2026-08-20** -- there is no revision that carries it. **THE PARENTHETICAL IS THE WORST PART, NOT THE CLAIM: it read _vc verified by reading, rather than carrying cc's pre-fix drive_, offering the reading as the warrant -- and the reading was of the wrong tree.** A claim of rigour attached to the wrong subject is worse than no claim, because it tells the reader not to check. **AND IT CONFIRMS THE PARK RATHER THAN LIFTING IT, WHICH IS THE MEASURABLE COST: this row's `#[ignore]` expires _once dc's `init`/`bootstrap` work is committed_, and it is not.** Taken at face value it would have un-parked the population arm against a tree containing no `init` at all, **and the baseline measured there would have been wrong in a way the green could not have shown.** Third instance today of one instrument: a `grep` over the SHARED WORKING TREE reported as a fact about a revision -- and the first two were diagnosed by vc, in the morning, before vc committed the third.

CAPABILITY half of the criterion and not the harm half.** A `build.rs` in `intent-cli` embeds the source commit and arm 2 reads it back out with `strings`; measured cold at `[intent-source-commit:dirty-b11ca6ac74d5...]`, sha matching HEAD exactly, self-delimiting so the extraction cannot capture a neighbouring rodata literal. **Three pieces, not two -- `build.rs` only sets an env var, so a third has to `env!()` the marker into the binary or nothing is embedded anywhere.** All four branches driven against real artefacts: no marker, `unknown`, `dirty-<sha>`, earlier-tree. **AND IT SHIPPED DEAD IN `b11ca6ac`, WHICH IS RECORDED BECAUSE THE ROW IS ABOUT EXACTLY THIS**: arm 1's no-manifest path did `exit 0` -- correct when the vendored tree was the whole file -- so in any project without a vendored devbin the binary arm never ran, reproduced with a planted `deadbeef` marker and a `strings` control proving the silence was the script's. **An exit written when there was one arm is a claim that the run is over.** **MEASURED STALENESS, STATED AS A KNOWN COST RATHER THAN LEFT TO BE DISCOVERED**: no `rerun-if-changed` on `.git/HEAD` (cc -- in a five-session clone that trigger means "a peer committed", and emitting no line restores cargo's default of re-running on PACKAGE-file changes, which is the trigger that tracks the code), so a HEAD move outside the package leaves the embed stale -- witnessed, binary still naming `b11ca6ac` at HEAD `010b2bbf`, and updating on a package-file touch. **That is the case `stage` must fail closed on, defect wearing a reference: never-ran and ran-green would be the same absence again. **GREEN 2026-08-17 (vc), on dc's close claim, which dc sent rather than took -- they own WP-11, did the work, and said so first.** **CLAUSE 2 IS MET DECISIVELY: three arms driven on real artefacts from a release build** (deliberately release, since `#[used]` surviving `--gc-sections` is linker policy that differs by profile). A claim phrased without any of those words is invisible to the method. I believe the population is covered; I cannot prove the filter was."_** dc also recorded a canary arm that DOES NOT REPRODUCE (re-signing a notarised binary to lapse its ticket -- the hash moves and `spctl` still returns `Notarized Developer ID`), recorded it as proving nothing, and did not count it. **MY OWN CORROBORATION SHARES THAT BLIND SPOT AND I AM NOT PRESENTING IT AS INDEPENDENT.** I swept the whole file by claim-vocabulary rather than by function, which reaches the HELPERS dc's command-scoped sweep could have missed, and found 4 provenance-claiming output statements outside the eight commands -- **all four derived from a measurement** (`artefact_commit_blockers` printing what it read, `verify_notarised` printing the verdict it got, `require_sums` refusing). **That covers a population dc's method might not have; it does NOT establish that a keyword-invisible claim is absent, because my method is a keyword method too.** Two instruments sharing a method check it once -- the correction I made against my own Lamplight counts this morning, applied here against my own corroboration. **CLOSED ANYWAY, AND THE REASON IS THE DENOMINATOR.** The population is ENUMERATED (210, a number, not an adjective), every one of the 8 is a driven fix, and **the residual is a judgement over a counted population rather than an unmeasured arm** -- which is the distinction this thread has enforced all week. Demanding mechanical proof that no prose line asserts what it did not measure would make this row unclosable, and unclosable is the defect this row was corrected for once already today. **Weighed against issue 0032 -- a green CLOSES the criterion and nothing re-examines a closed one -- and closed with the limitation on the record so a later reader inherits the doubt rather than the conclusion.** **OUTPUT CORRECTED `addd4581` (dc), AND THE GREEN IS UNCHANGED BY IT BECAUSE NOTHING THE ROW RESTS ON MOVED**: 8 insertions, 0 deletions, one `echo` and seven comments, verified from the diff rather than the description -- no branch, no rc, no control flow. Arm 2 now prints, ONCE before its loop, that its lines are DIAGNOSTIC and that the arm never fails, naming `int macos publish` as where enforcement lives. **The defect it closes is not the arm's behaviour but its EMISSION: four of arm 2's five branches are worded as findings, `rc` is assigned only at `:65`, `:107` and `:118` -- all inside arm 1 -- and the comment saying dirty is not a finding sat at `:179-180` where it never printed.** It cost vc a wrong claim propagated four ways in one day, to hv and to two peers. **dc re-drove arm 1 in BOTH directions on an isolated fixture after noticing two rc=0 runs on a live tree proved nothing** (3 files agreeing -> rc=0; one mutated and staged with the manifest not -> rc=1, naming the file); arm 1 still fails, and the live tree is rc=0 unchanged from the pre-edit baseline. **Recorded here by vc because the typed API has no verb for this field and the `at` family's status verbs destroy it (issue 0033) -- the gap is now ST0057 AC-08.5's second burning case.** **THE RECORDED REASON ABOVE IS HALF FALSE AND IS CORRECTED AT `a466f90f` RATHER THAN DELETED** (cc 2026-08-18, measured after vc refused a reversal citing it; vc re-measured independently before recording). **`.git/HEAD` DOES NOT MOVE ON A COMMIT** -- it holds `ref: refs/heads/main` and is rewritten on a BRANCH SWITCH. Measured in this clone: `.git/HEAD` mtime `2026-02-17`, six months stale, against `.git/refs/heads/main` and `.git/logs/HEAD` both at `2026-08-18 19:17Z`, seconds after a commit. **So the rebuild-storm objection cc priced against COULD NEVER HAVE OCCURRED.** **THE REFUSAL STANDS ON ITS OTHER LIMB, WHICH IS SUFFICIENT ALONE: emitting ANY `cargo:rerun-if-changed` REPLACES cargo's package-file default**, so naming `.git/HEAD` would swap a trigger that follows the CODE for one following a file that almost never moves -- **leaving the embed stale on CODE changes, permanently and silently. The naive fix is strictly worse than the gap it closes, and worse in the direction nothing reports.** Second staleness witness: `dirty-4ef953db` embedded at HEAD `c83f624c`. **THE EXPRESSIBLE THIRD OPTION IS RECORDED AND NOT TAKEN: `rerun-if-changed=src` PLUS `.git/logs/HEAD`, the file that actually moves.** Declined because the marker is `dirty-<HEAD>`, so two behaviourally different dirty builds at one commit share one value; `publish` refuses `dirty-` outright, so staleness costs MEASUREMENT and never releases. **What bit today was PAIRING, and AC-10.11's content hash closes it; no build trigger closes either half.** **BOTH DIRECTIONS OF THE MARKER'S BEHAVIOUR ARE NOW OBSERVED ON ONE DAY, WHICH IS THE STRONGEST FORM OF THIS ARGUMENT (ic 2026-08-18).** This afternoon the marker DID NOT MOVE across a rebuild that changed behaviour, because both builds came from one HEAD -- the live casualty behind AC-10.11's content-hash clause. This evening it DID move, `dirty-4ef953db` -> `dirty-18197aaf`, because HEAD differed between builds. **So the marker is NEITHER BROKEN NOR STALE: it names the COMMIT, exactly and only, and it is non-identifying precisely when two builds share one commit.** That is a narrower and more defensible claim than _the marker cannot be trusted_, and it is what the criterion should be read as asserting. **ic's gate run survives the swap only because AC-10.11 pinned it by CONTENT HASH: the note attributes the green to `61870438`, a binary that no longer exists on disk anywhere.** Second time in one day that rule paid -- it caught the mid-run swap, and now it makes a delivered result checkable against an artefact that is gone. **AND THE FORMULATION IS NARROWER THAN ALL THREE OF US HAD IT (ic's refinement, cc's evidence, 2026-08-18 evening): THE MARKER NAMES A COMMIT, EXACTLY AND ONLY. A COMMIT DOES NOT DETERMINE AN ARTEFACT WHEN THE TREE IS DIRTY.** The asymmetry is the whole point and only one half costs anything: **HEAD DIFFERING IS SUFFICIENT FOR THE MARKER TO MOVE; HEAD BEING EQUAL IS NOT SUFFICIENT FOR TWO BINARIES TO BE EQUIVALENT.** Observed at the moment it mattered rather than argued: **the binary that WIPED the estate and the binary that FIXED it carry the same marker byte for byte** -- `dirty-18197aaf...`, same HEAD, different working tree -- while `.canon` hits went 3 -> 0 and sha256 `05b0020e` -> `cca08f4e`. **The difference between _destroys your estate_ and _works_ is invisible to the marker and visible only to the hash.** This is NOT a defect in the marker: it is a correct instrument read for something it never claimed.

**THE RE-CITATION ABOVE IS WITHDRAWN. IT WAS WRONG ON THE MERITS AND WRONG IN ITS CITATION, INDEPENDENTLY (vc 2026-08-20 16:15Z, on dc's finding against my row). The deliverable is RESTORED and stays unbuilt.**

**Count one, the mechanism.** `bin/.devbin/cmd/prepush` clones HEAD into `mktemp -d` and builds there -- its own step line reads *"cold, no shared target dir"*. **It never touches `native/rust/target/release/`, it refuses nobody, and it names no offending paths.** AC-11.6 weighed exactly two mitigations and ruled FOR refusal in its own words -- *refusing a dirty cross-owner rebuild prevents it AND TELLS SOMEONE WHY, before the artefact exists* -- and **I re-cited the row onto the mitigation the criterion had explicitly declined.** The 19:33Z episode that produced this row involved no push at all, so `prepush` could never have fired on it. **My Highlander argument was empty: two mechanisms enforcing different properties are not two copies of one**, so there was no duplication to be Highlander about.

**Count two, independent of the first and true even had the mechanism been right.** `bin/int` is a symlink to the `devbin` dispatcher and **does not contain the string `prepush`** -- driven, `grep -n prepush bin/int` returns nothing. **The row cited the invocation, not the implementation.**

**AND THE WARRANT WAS THE WORST PART, NOT THE CLAIM.** The evidence offered above is real and driven -- `prepush` did run on the push path and did print `ok:` twice, and I watched it. **A true measurement of a different property, offered as proof.** dc's sharper statement of it, which supersedes the *"a green is only ever about the question the instrument asks"* form already on the board: that line describes an INSTRUMENT; this one describes what a careful person does with a correct result, and **the evidence being real and driven is precisely what made it persuasive.**

**HOW IT WAS FOUND, WHICH IS THE PART THAT GENERALISES.** dc tried to build the red-first arm, could not make it red, and stopped rather than green something unreachable. **No instrument this estate owns catches an expired citation** -- `at lint` exempts `to-write` from L2 and L3, correctly, so a citation cannot be validated until somebody uses it. Three instances today, all three caught by a builder attempting to satisfy a row: dc here, cc on AT-10.14 and on AC-10.2.

**ONE CHEAP SPLIT IS AVAILABLE AND IS DRIVEN, NOT PROPOSED.** The commit gate's stale arm printed the same sentence for three rows today. - AT-12.1 `tests/cutover_guard.bash` -- covers AC-12.1 -- status: red -- no file surviving the prune depends on `bin/` -- neither by SOURCING `bin/intent_helpers` nor by CALLING a symbol only `bin/` defines. The second edge is invisible to any sweep spelled `source`, which is why this row names a PROPERTY and not an instrument: an instrument named in a row becomes the row's ceiling, because nobody re-derives the claim once there is a tool to run. Driven in a fixture tree with `bin/` ABSENT, BOTH ARMS, because neither arm alone decides it. SURVIVING ARM: every surviving entry point runs to a correct answer with EMPTY STDERR. CONTROL ARM: the pre-port form of each, in the same tree, must produce NON-EMPTY STDERR. THE CONTROL ARM'S SIGNAL IS STDERR -- NEVER rc, NEVER STDOUT -- AND ASSERTING OTHERWISE YIELDS A PERMANENTLY-GREEN TEST: measured on `intent_claude_cwi` 2026-08-27, with `bin/` absent the pre-port file returns rc=0 with stdout BYTE-IDENTICAL to the ported file, differing only on stderr, so under `2>/dev/null` the broken and the working forms are indistinguishable; the missing symbol is `error()` ITSELF, so the No-Silent-Errors path is the one that goes silent and execution continues past every guard it was protecting. BOTH ARMS ARE REQUIRED because the surviving arm alone passes for a file that never had the dependency, so it cannot tell PORTED from NEVER-COUPLED. THE SURVIVING POPULATION IS DERIVED FROM THE TREE AT TEST TIME, NEVER HAND-LISTED, or the row acquires a roster problem of its own. AND THIS IS WHAT MAKES THE PRUNE'S ORDERING CHECKABLE (vc, 2026-08-27): `claude_plugin_helpers.sh` and its two live sourcers are pruned in ONE commit, because the `ext_root_dir` dangle dies with the prune if the lib leaves with them and goes LIVE if it outlives them -- and this test cannot pass in either intermediate state, which converts a sequencing property into a verifiable one.

(1) THE ONE LAUNCHER: a whole-tree census finds env::var naming VISUAL or EDITOR exactly TWICE, both inside launch_editor; tui::edit takes the launcher as a parameter, so it has nowhere to put a second resolver. (2) CLAUSE 2 HAS NO SUBJECT rather than being vacuously satisfied -- facade.edit appends .md and decides by filename, so no field can enter that path by construction, and design section 7 puts artefact editing in its own subsection, deliberately distinct from the field rows. It becomes live the day a field realiser appears, and Files::scratch is now the one home. (3) THE RE-READ IS UNCONDITIONAL and sits before the notice is composed -- not gated on Landed::Written, because the criterion says the editor is the AUTHORITY and a return path that trusts its own report is trusting what it just gave the terminal to. (4) Drop plus a panic hook that CHAINS rather than replaces, with an unwind asserted to restore exactly as a normal return does; Borrowed::lend brackets the child so the give-back and the re-take cannot come apart. (5) THE UNSAVED-FORM FATE IS ASSERTED, NOT MERELY ABSENT: out_of(Mode::Field).all(|e| e.to != Mode::Embed) is a fact about every future table, where an absent edge would only have been a fact about this one. Four things that must not happen each carry a test and a driven mutation: the editor is not handed the painted row, a failing editor writes nothing, an unchanged file writes nothing, and the scratch file survives every failure and is NAMED -- on the refused-write path it is the only copy of the operator work that exists. intent-cli --lib 142 passed / 0 failed.
